# Algorithm research report (2026-11-16)

Output of the monthly research agents in `.github/workflows/algorithm-audit-drift.yml`: one agent per problem of `R/package_metadata/audits/algorithm_taxonomy.json` (task prompts in `.github/prompts/`, method in `.github/prompts/research_agent_CLAUDE.md`). Everything here is **unverified**: web claims, not measurements. A finding means *re-look*, not *wrong*. What each agent actually searched is in the companion `-searches.md` file. Procedure: `R/package_metadata/new_feature_plans/v1_0_5/algorithm_audit_runbook.md`.

Compared with the previous results (`research_log.json`): 7 new, 202 unchanged, 87 updated.

## 1. Faster-algorithm findings
### adjacent-cat-cr-logit-mle: lbfgs, newton-raphson, analytic-derivatives → Sufficient-statistic / covariate-pattern compression. Tabulate the data once per fit into G distinct (x row, y) patterns with integer counts c_g; for treatment-only, G ≤ 2K, a 2×K table. Then evaluate NLL, gradient and Hessian as count-weighted sums over the G patterns. For AC this is the grouped form of the Poisson-log-linear / multinomial equivalence used by brglm2::bracl with frequency weights. For CR, the existing fast_continuation_ratio_regression_weighted_cpp already accepts subject weights and can take the counts directly. The NLL is exactly the ungrouped NLL: identical (x, y) rows are summed with multiplicity, so no multinomial constant appears. The MLE, vcov and neg_loglik are therefore unchanged up to floating-point summation order.
- **Current:** Every likelihood, gradient and Hessian evaluation loops over all n subjects (AC: O(n(K+p)) per eval, O(n(K²+Kp+p²)) per Hessian; CR: over N_aug ≤ n(K−1) expanded rows). This holds even when the design is treatment-only (X = w, built in inference_ordinal_adj_cat_logit.R when no covariates are selected) or has only a few discrete covariates. It happens in every replicate fit (randomization kernel, parametric-bootstrap y_sim refits, CI-inversion constrained refits).
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by any source. Theoretical: after one O(n) (or O(n log n) for general X) tabulation, the per-evaluation cost goes from O(nK) to O(G·K). Treatment-only means G ≤ 2K, so roughly n/(2K) fewer flops per eval: about 50x at n=500, K=5. A whole fit then costs about one pass over the data instead of ~30–45 L-BFGS evaluations or ~5–8 Newton gradient+Hessian passes. In the randomization kernel, tabulating each permuted w column is O(n). (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Gains only when G ≪ n: treatment-only or low-cardinality discrete covariates. With continuous covariates G = n, so the original path must stay as a fallback (check G < n/2, say). Summation order changes, so results move at the ~1e-15 level; deterministic for any thread count because tabulation is a sequential count. Needs count-weighted versions of the AC objective/Hessian; CR can reuse the weighted entry point. Check that callers reading per-subject outputs (X_aug, z, weights_aug in CR) do not need the uncompressed arrays. Fitted probabilities per subject can be recovered by indexing the pattern. Separation/boundary behaviour is identical, since the likelihood is the same function.
- **Suggested action:** Prototype: add a compressed-pattern path to AdjacentCategoryLogitNegLogLik/hessian and route CR through the weighted kernel with counts when the number of distinct (x, y) rows is below about n/2. Start with treatment-only fits and compute_adj_cat_logit_distr_parallel_cpp. Gate on equality of b, ssq_b_1, neg_loglik to 1e-10 against the current path. This mirrors the category-tabulation trick the catalog already adopted for JT/ridit (catalog §6 TODO-9, §10 TODO-1).
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_adjacent_category_logit.cpp::fast_adjacent_category_logit_internal`, `R/EDI/src/fast_adjacent_category_logit.cpp::AdjacentCategoryLogitNegLogLik`, `R/EDI/src/fast_adjacent_category_logit.cpp::hessian`, `R/EDI/src/fast_continuation_ratio_regression.cpp::fast_continuation_ratio_internal`, `R/EDI/src/fast_continuation_ratio_regression.cpp::ContinuationRatioObjective`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/brglm2/vignettes/adjacent.html https://jaycrick.github.io/MAST90084-Statistical-Modelling-Scripts/quarto_scripts/A2-likelihood-multinomial.html https://www.stata.com/support/faqs/statistics/logistic-regression-with-grouped-data/ https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-31186-4_6 https://arxiv.org/abs/2112.02621

### adjacent-cat-cr-logit-mle: lbfgs, newton-raphson → Damped Newton-Raphson with step-halving on the analytic Hessian, with L-BFGS as the fallback. It is already implemented (optimization_alg="newton_raphson") and already used in compute_adj_cat_logit_distr_parallel_cpp; this means making it the default (catalogued as v1.0.5 catalog §2 TODO-1/TODO-2). New external evidence: a benchmark of ordinal logistic MLE (Harrell, fharrell.com, 2024-11-28, revised 2025-06-20) with k=10 intercepts, p=5, n=100,000 timed NR at 0.084 s against optim BFGS at 0.391 s, and at k=1000, p=1 NR at 0.0095 s against BFGS at 0.0375 s. Its stated conclusion: NR is the best Hessian-using algorithm for speed and convergence, and BFGS is appealing only with very many intercepts.
- **Current:** optimization_alg defaults to "lbfgs" (LBFGSpp, strong Wolfe, f-plateau stop) for the strictly concave AC and CR fits, and R callers pass no optimization_alg.
- **Claimed speedup:** About 4–5x wall-clock over BFGS on cumulative-logit ordinal fits at d≈15 (Harrell's benchmark, rms lrm.fit, not the AC/CR models or LBFGSpp). The catalog's theoretical estimate is 1–2x fewer gradient-equivalents. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same unique MLE (strictly concave, canonical link, so observed = expected information); numbers move at the convergence-tolerance level (~1e-6) because the stop rule changes from f-plateau to a gradient/Newton-decrement test. That is also a robustness gain: the score at the reported MLE becomes ~0, which matters for score/LR CI inversion. The benchmark is on the cumulative logit with R's BFGS, not on LBFGSpp, so the ratio for EDI must be measured. Combined with finding 1, the Hessian cost becomes negligible on treatment-only designs.
- **Suggested action:** Run the A/B harness for catalog §2 TODO-1/TODO-2 (wall clock and iterations, AC and CR, n in 50–2000, K in 3–7, p in 1–10). Then switch the defaults to newton_raphson with the existing L-BFGS fallback.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_adjacent_category_logit.cpp::fast_adjacent_category_logit_internal`, `R/EDI/src/fast_adjacent_category_logit.cpp::fast_adjacent_category_logit_cpp`, `R/EDI/src/fast_adjacent_category_logit.cpp::fast_adjacent_category_logit_with_var_cpp`, `R/EDI/src/fast_continuation_ratio_regression.cpp::fast_continuation_ratio_internal`, `R/EDI/src/fast_continuation_ratio_regression.cpp::fast_continuation_ratio_regression_cpp`, `R/EDI/src/fast_continuation_ratio_regression.cpp::fast_continuation_ratio_regression_weighted_cpp`, `R/EDI/src/fast_continuation_ratio_regression.cpp::fast_continuation_ratio_regression_with_var_cpp`
- **Sources:** https://fharrell.com/post/mle/ https://cran.r-project.org/package=rms https://cran.r-project.org/package=ordinal

### balanced-allocation-optimization: simulated-annealing, branch-and-bound-milp → (Re-reported, unchanged.) With p = 1, mahal_dist, D and l1 are all monotone in |Σ_T x̃ − Σ_C x̃|, which is balanced number partitioning (Kallus 2018, eq. 2.8). Use the balanced largest differencing method (BLDM, O(n log n) time, O(n) space) as the start and Mertens' BLDM + complete Karmarkar-Karp to search towards a certified optimum. The algorithm is anytime and deterministic. The anytime guarantee holds for |n_T − n_C| ≤ 1. Other n_T need Mertens' cardinality pruning, and the first solution is then not guaranteed to be feasible.
- **Current:** With p = 1 covariate, EDI solves the problem with GLPK Fortet MILP at n ≤ 20, or 4 chains × 20k-move SA above that. For l1 it always uses the MILP.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified for EDI. Mertens reports BLDM at O(n log n). CKK node counts grow exponentially below n_c ≈ 30 (25-bit numbers) and fall above it. With 12 or fewer significant digits, problems of arbitrary size are solvable in practice; double-precision covariates push the hard region to larger n. No source compares this with SA or MILP. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Only p = 1 and the kinds that reduce to |sum difference| are covered; the A-ratio and custom kinds are not. Certification may be exponential for high-precision inputs near n ≈ 50–60, so it needs a node cap. It is deterministic. It removes SA randomness for these kinds, and the mirror coin still randomizes the label.
- **Suggested action:** Prototype a C++ BLDM+CKK kernel with a node/time cap behind a p == 1 branch in optimal_solve_auto. Validate it against enumeration at n ≤ 24 and against SA at n = 50/100/200. Add a row to catalog §12 tables (a)/(b).
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/helper_optimal_annealing.R::optimal_solve_auto`, `R/EDI/src/design_optimal_annealing_search.cpp::annealing_design_search_cpp`, `R/EDI/R/helper_optimal_milp_solvers.R::milp_solve_quadratic`, `R/EDI/R/helper_optimal_milp_solvers.R::milp_solve_l1`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/cs/9903011 https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/cs/9903011 https://arxiv.org/abs/1312.0531 https://cran.r-project.org/package=GreedyExperimentalDesign

### balanced-allocation-optimization: branch-and-bound-milp → (Re-reported, updated.) A low-rank convex MIQCP through the CRAN package scip 1.10.1-1 (Apache ≥ 2): minimize θ subject to θ ≥ ||z||², z = Mw (p continuous), Σw = n_T, w binary. This removes the commercial-solver objection recorded in catalog table (a). New this run: ompr 1.0.4 supports only linear objectives, so this has to use scip's own model API (scip_add_quadratic_cons), not the existing ompr path.
- **Current:** The GLPK Fortet/McCormick linearization uses n(n−1)/2 auxiliary variables and hits a cliff between n = 20 and 25 (311 s at n = 25).
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified; no source benchmarks this problem. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Adds a new optional dependency and a second model-building path. SCIP enforces convex quadratic constraints by outer-approximation cuts, so performance at n = 20–40 is unmeasured. The ratio (A) kind still needs Dinkelbach around it.
- **Suggested action:** Re-look: benchmark scip low-rank MIQCP against GLPK Fortet at n = 15/20/25/30 (p = 5), behind requireNamespace('scip'), and against the enumeration prototype.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/helper_optimal_milp_solvers.R::milp_solve_quadratic`, `R/EDI/R/helper_optimal_milp_solvers.R::milp_solve_A_dinkelbach`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=scip https://cran.r-project.org/package=ompr https://cran.r-project.org/web/views/Optimization.html

### balanced-allocation-optimization: branch-and-bound-milp → (Re-reported, unchanged.) Use HiGHS as the MILP backend, either through ROI.plugin.highs (GPL-2 | GPL-3) or by calling the HiGHS C API from EDI's C++ through highs 1.15.1-2. The 1.15.1-2 NEWS confirms that all 177 C API functions are registered with R_RegisterCCallable (LinkingTo: highs). HiGHS still has no MIQP, so it helps only the linearized models.
- **Current:** GLPK is called through ompr/ROI.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified. Earlier evidence suggests results depend on the formulation. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Adds a dependency, and GLPK can win on some linearized models. The new parallel MIP solver in HiGHS 1.15 must be run single-threaded or checked for determinism.
- **Suggested action:** Re-look: benchmark GLPK against HiGHS on milp_solve_quadratic at n = 15/20/25 and on milp_solve_l1 at n = 20–200, with HiGHS threads fixed at 1 for determinism.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/helper_optimal_milp_solvers.R::milp_solve_quadratic`, `R/EDI/R/helper_optimal_milp_solvers.R::milp_solve_l1`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=highs https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/highs/news/news.html https://cran.r-project.org/package=ROI.plugin.highs

### beta-regression-mle: likelihood-factorization → Call EDI's existing fast_beta_regression_weighted_cpp on the interior rows with the BB weights (the same weighted beta MLE), keeping betareg/lm.wfit as fallbacks. Skip the zero/one logits unless the estimand is marginal.
- **Current:** Each Bayesian-bootstrap replicate fits β with the betareg::betareg formula interface (optim BFGS + Fisher scoring, model.frame overhead), and fits two binary logits for γ0/γ1 even when the estimand only needs β.
- **Claimed speedup:** From EDI's own benchmark page (fetched): at N=1000, the C++ beta regression takes 0.88 ms vs 24.85 ms for betareg.fit (28.4x). The formula betareg() call adds model.frame overhead on top. Not measured on this exact path. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same weighted MLE within optimizer tolerance, but not bit-identical to betareg (different optimizer path and φ start). Check agreement on weighted data. Deterministic.
- **Suggested action:** Prototype: swap betareg::betareg for fast_beta_regression_weighted_cpp in the ZOIB BB replicate, gate the logits on the marginal estimand, verify coefficient agreement with betareg, then time a B=1000 run.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_proportion_zero_one_inflated_beta.R::compute_estimate_with_bootstrap_weights`
- **Sources:** https://rawcdn.githack.com/kapelner/EDI/main/R/benchmark/benchmark_model_fits_R.html https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/betareg/vignettes/betareg.html https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.14011

### beta-regression-mle: likelihood-factorization → One vectorized call over the interior indices: rbeta(length(idx), pmax(mu[idx]*phi,1e-4), pmax((1-mu[idx])*phi,1e-4)). R recycles the shape arguments to the result length.
- **Current:** The PB simulator draws interior values with an interpreted for (i in 1:n) loop calling rbeta(1, a, b) per row.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified. Removes an O(n) interpreted loop of n separate rbeta calls per PB replicate. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Identical draws only if the loop draws in index order and interleaves no other RNG calls (u for the category must be drawn first, as a vector). Confirm with identical() under a fixed seed.
- **Suggested action:** Adopt as a low-priority cleanup, gated on an identical() check of y_sim against the loop under a fixed seed.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_proportion_zero_one_inflated_beta.R::simulate_under_lik_null`
- **Sources:** https://stat.ethz.ch/R-manual/R-devel/library/stats/html/Beta.html

### binary-glm-mle: lbfgs → Make "irls" (Newton with LDLT, already in C++) the wrapper default, matching the C++ default and the catalog row that calls L-BFGS the non-default alternative.
- **Current:** The R wrappers default optimization_alg = "lbfgs" (helper_glm_fit.R:852, :923). KK21 calls them per covariate with p≈2 and refits under separation.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified for EDI. scikit-learn 1.9.1 docs recommend newton-cholesky for n_samples >> n_features. fastglm's vignette shows Cholesky-IRLS as the fastest exact GLM fit. The 5–8 Newton iterations vs tens for L-BFGS is the catalog's estimate, not a measurement. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Same MLE within tolerance. Under separation, the iterate at which ‖β‖ crosses the threshold may differ between optimizers, so which covariate gets dropped could change. A/B the KK21 weights.
- **Suggested action:** Re-look (carried over from 2026-11-16; still unaddressed at 8b718ae1). Confirm whether the wrapper default "lbfgs" is intentional. If not, switch it to "irls" and A/B KK21. Fix the inventory wording 'C++ default L-BFGS' either way.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/helper_glm_fit.R::fast_logistic_regression`, `R/EDI/R/helper_glm_fit.R::fast_logistic_regression_with_var`
- **Sources:** https://scikit-learn.org/stable/modules/generated/sklearn.linear_model.LogisticRegression.html https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/fastglm/vignettes/benchmarks-fastglm.html

### binary-glm-mle: irls → Route that branch to fast_logistic_regression_cpp(X, y, estimate_only = TRUE), as line 257 already does. It solves the same Bernoulli quasi-score equations by C++ IRLS with an LDLT step. Keep glm.fit as a fallback on non-convergence.
- **Current:** The estimate_only + harden=FALSE branch calls base R glm.fit(family = quasibinomial()) (inference_proportion_fractional_logit.R:165), which runs in resampling loops.
- **Claimed speedup:** fastglm CRAN benchmark vignette (2026-11-06; logistic, p=30): glm.fit 2.75 ms vs C++ Cholesky IRLS 0.67 ms at n=1,000 (~4×), and 29.0 vs 4.7 ms at n=10,000. Not measured on EDI. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same QMLE within the convergence tolerance. glm.fit's convergence criterion (relative deviance) differs from the C++ one (score/step norm), so the last digits can differ. Deterministic either way.
- **Suggested action:** Re-look (carried over from 2026-11-16; code unchanged). Switch the branch to the C++ kernel and benchmark one bootstrap at EDI's n.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_proportion_fractional_logit.R::compute_estimate_with_bootstrap_weights`, `R/EDI/R/inference_proportion_fractional_logit.R::generate_mod`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/fastglm/vignettes/benchmarks-fastglm.html https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/fastglm/news/news.html

### blocking-partition: branch-and-bound-milp → An exact subset dynamic program written in C++ (deterministic, no solver). Let f(S) be the minimum cost of partitioning the set S of unassigned subjects, with a count of how many size-⌈n/B⌉ blocks are still allowed. The lowest-index subject in S always opens the next block; enumerate its s−1 block-mates among S (s ∈ {⌊n/B⌋, ⌈n/B⌉}), add that block's within-pair distance sum, and recurse. Anchoring on the lowest element removes the B! label symmetry the MILP suffers from. States are bitmasks (2^n, about 1M at n=20, 8 MB of doubles). Transitions per state are at most C(n−1, s−1), so the total is well under 3^n, and 3^16 ≈ 4.3e7 already fits in about 2 s per the cited DP write-up. Exact for any D: custom, negative or asymmetric-symmetrised. Above a size cap, keep the MILP as a fallback.
- **Current:** ompr/ROI MILP with binary z_ijk (n²B/2) and three McCormick rows, symmetry broken only by x_11 = 1, solved by GLPK. It is re-solved on every randomization-bootstrap replay draw; the docs measure about 1.7 s at n=12, B=3 and about 79 s at n=16, B=4.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not benchmarked by any source for this problem. The source for the general submask-partition DP solves N=16 (3^16 ≈ 43M transitions) within 2 s. The fixed-block-size version enumerates far fewer transitions: for n=16, s=4, about 16.5k reachable states × ≤455 transitions, versus EDI's measured 79 s for the MILP. Any ratio is my own estimate, not a measurement. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same objective and exact optimum. Ties between equally optimal partitions may resolve to a different (equally optimal) partition than GLPK returns, so test fixtures that pin specific block ids may need refreshing. The memory of O(2^n) doubles means a hard cap of about n ≤ 22–24 with a MILP fallback beyond it. The ompr path is already impractical beyond about n=16, so the cap costs nothing in practice. New C++ code, but small. It also makes the roi_solver dependency optional for small n.
- **Suggested action:** Add a row to catalog §12 options table (c): 'exact subset DP with lowest-element anchoring (C++, no solver), n ≤ ~22'. Prototype it as an arm of the v1.0.5-TODO-5 benchmark against the current MILP at n ∈ {8, 12, 16, 20}, checking objective equality (not block-id equality). If it wins, dispatch method='ompr' to the DP below the cap. This directly fixes the documented hours-long replay cost at n=16.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/design_fixed_optimal_blocks.R::solve_optimal_blocks`
- **Sources:** https://phuongdinh1411.github.io/cses-analyses/problem_soulutions/dynamic_programming_at/grouping_analysis https://mtuann.github.io/competitive-programming-cpp/topics/dp/bitmask-dp/ https://par.nsf.gov/biblio/10426618

### blocking-partition: branch-and-bound-milp → When ⌊n/B⌋ = ⌈n/B⌉ = 2, the objective Σ_blocks D_ij is exactly minimum-weight perfect non-bipartite matching. Dispatch to nbpMatching::nonbimatch, which is already an EDI import used by helper_matching.R. It runs the Derigs/Edmonds blossom algorithm, exact and polynomial (O(n³)). blockTools' algorithm='optimal' uses the same approach for n.tr=2.
- **Current:** The same exponential MILP is used even when every block has size 2 (n = 2B).
- **Claimed speedup:** Exponential to O(n³). Not quantified by a source at EDI's sizes. Pair blocking is optimally solvable while blocks larger than two are NP-hard (Karmakar 2022, citing Greevy et al. 2004 and Lu et al. 2011). (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: high)
- **Trade-offs:** Applies only to user-chosen B = n/2. The default B = floor(√n) gives pairs only at n = 4–5. nonbimatch scales distances to integers (precision 6 in helper_matching.R), so there is tiny rounding and possibly a different choice among tied optima. Custom distance functions that return negatives need an offset, which leaves a perfect matching's optimum unchanged.
- **Suggested action:** In solve_optimal_blocks, add a fast path when n == 2B: call the existing compute_binary_match_structure / nbpMatching::nonbimatch on D and convert the pairs to block ids. Add a test that the objective equals the MILP's at n=8, B=4.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/design_fixed_optimal_blocks.R::solve_optimal_blocks`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=nbpMatching https://par.nsf.gov/biblio/10426618

### blocking-partition: branch-and-bound-milp → Use HiGHS as the open-source MILP backend, through ROI.plugin.highs if it accepts binary/integer types, or through the highs package's model API directly. designmatch 0.5.5 (2026-02-20), a CRAN matching/blocking-design package, now uses highs as its default solver. The scip CRAN package (1.10.1-1, 2026-10-04, Apache-2.0) is a second option through its own model-building API.
- **Current:** The roi_solver set is closed to glpk/gurobi/cplex (helper_optimal_milp_solvers.R:30), and GLPK is the open-source default.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified for this model. The general MIPLIB 2017 figures from Mittelmann (via arXiv 2312.13527, cited in the previous run) give a shifted geometric mean of 7317 for GLPK vs 3114 for HiGHS, about 2.3×. This is far smaller than the reformulation and DP gains. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** ROI.plugin.highs's CRAN refman describes it as a 'quadratic solver' and shows only an LP example. Its MILP support through ROI was not confirmed this run and must be checked before relying on it. Superseded at n ≤ ~20 if the subset DP lands; still useful beyond the DP cap.
- **Suggested action:** In the v1.0.5-TODO-5 benchmark, first confirm that ROI.plugin.highs solves a binary model. If it does, time GLPK vs HiGHS on the current and reformulated models above the DP cap, and add 'highs' to the closed roi_solver set if it wins.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/design_fixed_optimal_blocks.R::solve_optimal_blocks`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=highs https://cran.r-project.org/package=designmatch https://cran.r-project.org/package=ROI.plugin.highs https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/ROI.plugin.highs/refman/ROI.plugin.highs.html https://cran.r-project.org/package=scip https://cran.r-project.org/web/views/Optimization.html

### blocking-partition: branch-and-bound-milp → For dist ∈ {euclidean, mahal}, the kernels return squared distances, so when n mod B = 0 the objective is (n/B) × equal-size MSSC. Exact cardinality-constrained MSSC methods then apply: Piccialli & Sudoso's SDP branch-and-cut, and Brusco-style repetitive branch-and-bound. Re-reported from the previous run; nothing newer turned up in the 2025–2026 arXiv scan.
- **Current:** General MILP for every distance kind.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified against GLPK. Piccialli & Sudoso say they solve instances '10 times larger than ... state-of-the-art exact methods'. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** The SDP code depends on MATLAB/Gurobi-class tooling and no repository is linked. At EDI's n ≤ 20 the subset DP is simpler, so this is relevant only if larger exact n are wanted.
- **Suggested action:** Keep as a note in catalog table (c), below the subset DP. Do not pursue unless exact blocking at n ≫ 20 becomes a requirement.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/design_fixed_optimal_blocks.R::solve_optimal_blocks`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.08901 https://doi.org/10.1007/s10107-023-02021-8

### blocking-partition: balanced-clustering → fastcluster::hclust, a drop-in replacement for stats::hclust (or hclust.vector to avoid materialising dist). Re-reported from the previous run.
- **Current:** stats::hclust(dist(X), 'ward.D2') picks the r = n mod B held-out subjects before anticlust::balanced_clustering.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified; the package page says only 'much faster' algorithms. K-way already costs only about 6 ms per replay draw at n=40 and about 21 ms at n=400. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: unknown; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** New this run: fastcluster's own test script notes ties 'may be resolved differently' from stats::hclust for ward.D2. The held-out subjects, and hence the blocks, could change on tied data such as discrete covariates. Adds a dependency (FreeBSD/GPL-2 dual license, compatible).
- **Suggested action:** Re-look only if profiling shows the hclust holdout as a hotspot at large n. If so, A/B fastcluster against stats for identical cutree output, including tied-covariate fixtures.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/design_fixed_optimal_blocks.R::solve_kway_blocks`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=fastcluster https://cran.r-project.org/package=anticlust https://cran.r-project.org/web/views/Cluster.html

### bootstrap-confidence-interval: double-bootstrap → Draw the outer×inner set once (101 outer refits, each with 51 inner refits) and store each sorted inner distribution, or equivalently u_b, the inner-CDF position of θ̂. Then score coverage at all 5 grid α from the same set, with common random numbers across the grid. The grid and the nearest-coverage pick stay as they are. This is the half of v1.0.5-TODO-2 that keeps the estimator; going to continuous prepivoted α can follow separately after its A/B test.
- **Current:** For each of 5 grid α values, draw a new set of n_outer=101 outer resamples, refit, run an inner bootstrap of 51 on each, and check coverage at that α. Then pick the α whose coverage is nearest 1−α and run one outer B. Total about 5·101·52 + B ≈ 26.8k refits at defaults.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not a source claim; this is arithmetic on the code. About 26.8k → 101·52 + 501 ≈ 5.8k refits (≈4.6×). MetricGate's documentation describes the same trick ('store the sorted inner distributions up front', so each candidate α becomes a lookup), but gives no timing. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same procedure and same estimator. The Monte Carlo draws change, so seeded numbers differ from the current output, but reusing draws across the grid lowers the noise in comparing coverage between grid points. Seeding through set.seed(private$seed) stays deterministic. The inner bootstraps should still get distinct per-outer seeds (I8). Lee & Young's 1996 sequential (SPRT) inner stopping could cut the 51 inner refits further, but no fetched source quantifies it.
- **Suggested action:** Hoist the outer×inner loop out of the α-grid loop in ci_calibrated_bootstrap. This can be done now, ahead of or as step 1 of v1.0.5-TODO-2, and needs no coverage A/B beyond a regression check.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_non_param_boot.R::ci_calibrated_bootstrap`
- **Sources:** https://doi.org/10.1093/biomet/75.4.661 https://academic.oup.com/biomet/article-lookup/doi/10.1093/biomet/75.4.661 https://metricgate.com/docs/double-bootstrap-calibration/ https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9868.00245 https://ideas.repec.org/a/bla/jorssb/v62y2000i2p479-491.html
- **No open copy found** for doi:10.1093/biomet/75.4.661 (publisher page blocks automated access)
- **No open copy found** for doi:10.1111/1467-9868.00245 (publisher page blocks automated access)

### bootstrap-index-generation: iid-index-resampling, stratified-resampling → (a) The same rejection loop, but mark seen positions in a dense, reusable std::vector<uint8_t> or bitset of length n. The RNG calls happen in the same order, so the output is bit-identical. (b) Rate-based dispatch as in dqrng: partial Fisher-Yates on a reusable scratch copy of the ids when k/n > 0.5 (this is v1.0.5-TODO-2 and changes the stream), the bitset otherwise.
- **Current:** replace = FALSE: rejection sampling of positions, with duplicates detected through a fresh std::unordered_set<int> per call.
- **Claimed speedup:** From the CRAN dqrng vignette, measured against base R rather than EDI. At m=1e6, n=1e4 (1% rate), bitset rejection takes 103 µs, R's hash variant 658 µs and sample.int 1.94 ms. At a 60% rate, partial Fisher-Yates takes 10.3 ms against 63.7 ms for sample.int. Not quantified at EDI's n of roughly 100-1e4, and negligible end to end next to B refits. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** (a) keeps the output bit-identical and determinism intact. (b) changes the realized draws (same distribution), so seed-pinned fixtures need regenerating. dqrng is AGPL-3, which is only conditionally compatible, so implement the textbook technique rather than copying its code.
- **Suggested action:** When v1.0.5-TODO-2 is picked up, first swap the unordered_set for a reusable dense marker (bit-identical) and microbenchmark it at k/n in {0.25, 0.5, 0.9} with n up to 1e4. Adopt partial Fisher-Yates for k > n/2 only if it is still materially faster. Add the bitset row to the catalog §7 options table.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/exchangeable_resampling_draws.cpp::sample_group_units`, `R/EDI/src/exchangeable_resampling_draws.cpp::exchangeable_resampling_draws_cpp`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/dqrng/vignettes/sample.html https://cran.r-project.org/package=dqrng

### bootstrap-index-generation: iid-index-resampling → Batched ranged random integer generation (Brackett-Rozinsky & Lemire, SPE 2025): several unbiased bounded integers from one 64-bit word, with one multiplication and no division per value in the common case.
- **Current:** One Lemire nearly-divisionless bounded draw per index from a freshly seeded edi_rng::RRng (MT19937), O(size) per replicate.
- **Claimed speedup:** The paper reports 1.5-2.5x on shuffles with fast 64-bit generators and 2.6-4.7x with ChaCha, on in-cache arrays. It was not benchmarked for repeated draws from one fixed range or with 32-bit MT19937. Not quantified at EDI sizes. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** It changes the realized index stream (same distribution), so it needs a fixture refresh. It needs 64-bit words, while RRng is a 32-bit MT, so the gain may be small. Index generation is tiny next to per-replicate refits and per-call MT seeding.
- **Suggested action:** No action unless profiling shows RNG/index time matters. If it does, prototype a batched bounded_rand in RNG.h and bundle it with the v1.0.5-TODO-2/TODO-3 stream-change window.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_sample_int.cpp::sample_int_replace_internal`, `R/EDI/src/fast_sample_int.cpp::resample_group_rows_internal`, `R/EDI/src/bootstrap_indices.cpp::bootstrap_indices_internal`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.06213 https://doi.org/10.1002/spe.3369 https://github.com/lemire/batched_random

### bootstrap-randomization-test: brt-monte-carlo → Sequential Monte Carlo testing by betting (Fischer & Ramdas, JRSSB 2025). A test martingale bets on whether each BRT draw's statistic is at or beyond the observed one, and its p-process is valid at any stopping time, including the B cap. The binomial and binomial-mixture strategies stop early for rejection and for futility. The updates are closed form, so they slot into the existing batch loop over the common-random-number prefix (get_brt_distribution_prefix) with O(1) extra work per draw.
- **Current:** BRT sequential early stopping (compute_two_sided_brt_pval_with_sequential_mc, inference_all_abstract_rand_bootstrap.R:930, used in the CI search) takes batches of draws and stops once a per-look Clopper–Pearson band excludes the threshold. There is no multiplicity control across looks, and a fixed B is used otherwise. Besag–Clifford (TODO-156) is the tracked alternative but stops early only for futility.
- **Claimed speedup:** Source claim, not measured on EDI. On Fisher's sharp-null real data at α=0.05, the binomial strategy needed a mean of 85 permutations (median 53) and the binomial mixture 147, against 160–200 for Besag–Clifford (h=8–10) and a fixed T=1000 in the simulations (arXiv 2401.07365v5 §7.4). The simulations say substantially fewer permutations than Besag–Clifford under strong alternatives. Not quantified against EDI's current Clopper–Pearson band. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** This is a stopping-rule and policy change. The reported p-value becomes an anytime-valid one instead of the fixed-B (or band-stopped) MC proportion, so this is not the same answer. It is also a robustness gain: the current per-look band has no error control across looks. Determinism is kept if draws stay seeded per draw index and batch boundaries are fixed. The same candidate was reported for the plain randomization p-value in run 43903509109 (batch E.1). This extends it to the BRT path. The related catalog row (anytime-valid confidence sequences, 'redesign of the testing layer') concerns the CI search, not this per-test p-value.
- **Suggested action:** Fold the BRT sequential-MC path into the TODO-156 / v1.2.0 anytime-results proposal (algorithm_choice_audit.md TODO-43). Prototype the binomial betting strategy as an opt-in alternative to the Clopper–Pearson band in compute_two_sided_brt_pval_with_sequential_mc, then compare draws used and the error across looks on existing benchmark workloads. Also cite DOI https://doi.org/10.1093/jrsssb/qkaf014 (taken from the arXiv record; not fetched).
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_rand_bootstrap.R::approximate_rand_bootstrap_distribution_beta_hat_T`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_rand_bootstrap.R::generate_rand_bootstrap_draws`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.07365 https://arxiv.org/html/2401.07365v5 https://github.com/fischer23/MC-testing-by-betting https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18908

### clmm-ordinal-mixed-fit: gauss-hermite-quadrature → Add cluster weights to the native C++ GH objectives (_glmm_engine.h GLMMObjective, used by fast_ordinal_clmm for all 4 links, and fast_ordinal_glmm). Each group's log-integral and its gradient are multiplied by w_g, the weight being constant within a KK pair or singleton, so the replicate maximizes Σ_g w_g·log∫∏_i p_ig(u)φ(u)du with the analytic gradient and L-BFGS, warm-started from the point estimate. This matches the cluster Bayesian bootstrap (context row_to_unit = match cluster), uses the same estimator family and quadrature as the point estimate, and needs neither the R model frame nor the cloglog link-swap probe.
- **Current:** Each Bayesian-bootstrap replicate of KKCLMM/KKGLMM calls ordinal::clmm(y ~ X + (1|group), weights = pair-level weights expanded to rows, nAGQ = 20 adaptive). On failure it runs a clm fit plus a 5-start log σ multistart; if vcov is not finite it refits at nAGQ 7/11/15/1. Per the clmm source, nlminb is called with no gradient (finite differences, each objective call rerunning the inner Newton for the conditional modes), and the Hessian is numerical (deriv12). Case weights enter per observation inside the integrand: getNAGQ/getNGHQ_C accumulate weights[j]*log(pr) before the node sum.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by any source. Mechanism, from the clmm source: d+1 objective evaluations per FD gradient, each with an inner mode-finding Newton and 20-node AGQ, versus one analytic-gradient pass at 20 fixed nodes, plus R formula/model-frame overhead and a fallback ladder of up to 10 fits per replicate. Order-of-magnitude gains per replicate are plausible but unmeasured. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Partly a correctness finding, not only a speedup. clmm's per-observation weights give Σ_g log∫∏p^{w_g}φ, which differs from the cluster-weighted likelihood Σ_g w_g log∫∏pφ whenever w_g ≠ 1, for singletons and pairs alike. The current replicates therefore estimate a different weighted objective: within-cluster information is effectively tempered or sharpened by w_g, which can distort σ̂ and the conditional β̂. The native version changes replicate results toward the intended estimator, so it needs an A/B and a re-check of CI coverage, not a tolerance-equivalence test. Determinism is unaffected (no RNG in the fit). A cheaper stopgap, clmm nAGQ = -20 (non-adaptive, matching the native kernel's rule), skips the per-group mode solve but keeps the weight semantics problem. GPL: native code, no new dependency.
- **Suggested action:** Investigate, prioritised above speed: (1) confirm the weight-semantics mismatch numerically: for one KK dataset and one weight draw, compare clmm's weighted -logLik with a direct Σ_g w_g·log∫ evaluation at the same θ; (2) add a w_g argument to the GLMMObjective/OrdinalGLMMObjective value and gradient (group-level multiply); (3) switch weighted_ordinal_random_intercept_fit callers to it, A/B the Bayesian-bootstrap CIs, and time one replicate against the clmm path. Also retire the stale model-simplification-surrogate inventory row.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/helper_ordinal_mixed_fit.R::weighted_ordinal_random_intercept_fit`, `R/EDI/R/inference_ordinal_KK_clmm_abstract.R::compute_weighted_clmm_estimate`, `R/EDI/R/inference_ordinal_KK_combined.R::compute_estimate_with_bootstrap_weights`
- **Sources:** https://raw.githubusercontent.com/runehaubo/ordinal/master/R/clmm.R https://raw.githubusercontent.com/runehaubo/ordinal/master/src/utilityFuns.c https://search.r-project.org/CRAN/refmans/ordinal/html/clmm.html https://cran.r-project.org/package=ordinal https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/ordinal/NEWS

### clmm-ordinal-mixed-fit: gauss-hermite-quadrature, lbfgs → Adaptive GH with an exact gradient of the AQ approximate log-likelihood for a single grouping factor (Stringer, arXiv 2310.01589 v2): implicit differentiation of the per-group mode, reusing the inner Newton factorisation, optimised with L-BFGS via LBFGSpp and followed by a Newton polish. This answers the catalog's TODO-153 objection that the AGQ gradient lacks an envelope identity.
- **Current:** Non-adaptive GH with 20 fixed nodes (centred at 0, scaled by σ) inside an L-BFGS (LBFGSpp) outer MLE with the analytic gradient.
- **Claimed speedup:** Paper (Bernoulli GLMM): typically 2–4× faster than lme4/GLMMadaptive at m=1000, n=5; up to 7–10× on toenail data. These are relative to other packages, not to fixed-node GH. Against EDI's 20 fixed nodes the saving is probably modest, since the paper needs about 7–17 AGQ points at small clusters. Not quantified for ordinal models. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: unknown; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Results move at quadrature tolerance, more accurate at large σ. Per-group mode solve adds cost for pairs (m_g ≤ 2). The paper covers Bernoulli only, so ordinal third derivatives must be derived. The aghqmm reference package states no license, so derive the method from the paper; do not copy code.
- **Suggested action:** Unchanged from the previous run: cite Stringer 2024 in v1.0.5 TODO-153 and catalog T1, with a lowered node-saving expectation for 20-node ordinal kernels. Prototype AGQ on the 60-node Poisson GLMM before the ordinal kernels.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_ordinal_glmm.cpp::OrdinalGLMMObjective`, `R/EDI/src/fast_ordinal_clmm.cpp::run_fast_ordinal_clmm`, `R/EDI/R/inference_ordinal_KK_clmm_abstract.R::shared_rcpp`, `R/EDI/R/inference_ordinal_KK_combined.R::shared_rcpp`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.01589 https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2310.01589 https://arxiv.org/html/2310.01589v2 https://github.com/awstringer1/aghmm-paper-code

### complete-randomization-draws: fisher-yates-shuffle → A small C++ kernel. For each draw column, a partial Fisher–Yates of k = min(n_T, N−n_T) positions per block, using edi_rng::bounded_rand. It keeps the block_ra law (random rounding of N·prob_T, Bernoulli for singleton blocks) and seeds per draw index so results do not depend on thread count. randomizr 2.0.1 made the same kind of move: blocked assignment now runs in one C++ call.
- **Current:** block_ra_batch, in vectorized R: per block, N·r runif doubles, a two-key radix order(), a rank scatter, and several N·r temporaries. It is linear, with a large constant.
- **Claimed speedup:** From the randomizr 2.0.1 NEWS (CRAN, Apple M4 Pro, R 4.6.0): block_ra went from 8.9 ms to 1.1 ms at N=20,000 with 2,000 blocks, and from 46 ms to 6.5 ms at N=100,000 with 10,000 blocks, when moving from per-block R calls to C++. EDI's baseline is already vectorized, not per-block, so the gain for EDI is smaller and not quantified. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Same distribution, but the realized draws change, so seed-pinned fixtures must be refreshed. It also adds one more C++ entry point. The gain is a constant factor only, and it matters only if block draws are a measurable share of a design's randomization-inference time.
- **Suggested action:** Profile block_ra_batch at typical N·r (1e5–1e6) against a design's per-draw statistic time. Write the kernel under v1.0.5-TODO-11 only if draws are a material share; otherwise close v1.0.5-TODO-11 as Keep. Bundle any stream change with v1.0.6 TODO-149(c).
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/design_fixed_blocking.R::block_ra_batch`, `R/EDI/R/design_fixed_blocking.R::draw_ws_raw`, `R/EDI/R/design_fixed_blocked_cluster.R::draw_ws_raw`, `R/EDI/R/design_fixed_cluster.R::draw_ws_raw`, `R/EDI/R/design_fixed_optimal_blocks.R::draw_ws_raw`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/randomizr/news/news.html https://cran.r-project.org/package=randomizr https://arxiv.org/html/2605.05099

### complete-randomization-draws: fisher-yates-shuffle → Batched ranged random integer generation (Brackett-Rozinsky & Lemire, Software: Practice and Experience 55(1), doi 10.1002/spe.3369). It takes two or more unbiased bounded indices from one random word; with 32-bit words, two indices fit while i(i−1) < 2^32, which covers EDI's n.
- **Current:** Durstenfeld Fisher–Yates using one MT19937 32-bit word per position, with Lemire's multiply-shift rejection.
- **Claimed speedup:** From the arXiv abstract: it 'can more than double the speed of unbiased random shuffling for small to moderately large arrays'. The paper measured this with 64-bit PCG and Lehmer generators, not MT19937 and not specifically at n = 100–10,000. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Exact and deterministic, but it changes the seed-pinned R/Python stream again. The shuffle is rarely dominant in its callers: rerandomization spends O(np) per draw on the balance objective.
- **Suggested action:** Record this as an option in the catalog P2 table. Consider it only together with another planned stream change, such as v1.0.6 TODO-149(c), so seeds are refreshed once, and only if profiling shows RNG words are material.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/RNG.h::fisher_yates_shuffle`, `R/EDI/src/generate_permutations.cpp::generate_permutations_ibcrd_internal`, `R/EDI/src/rerandomization_helpers.cpp::complete_randomization_forced_balanced_cpp`, `R/EDI/src/rerandomization_helpers.cpp::complete_randomization_imbalanced_cpp`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.06213 https://doi.org/10.1002/spe.3369 https://github.com/lemire/batched_random

### conditional-logit-matched-fit: clogit-logistic-reduction → Route the fit through a compiled IRLS/Newton with prior weights and an offset (or fixed index). Option (a): add a case-weights argument to EDI's existing C++ fast_logistic_regression[_with_var]_cpp, which already supports fixed_idx/fixed_values and warm starts. This is the same change as the cpoisson weights item, v1.0.5 TODO-2. Option (b): use the fastglm-style RcppEigen IRLS (pivoted QR default, step-halving, weights+offset) as a reference.
- **Current:** Stacked discordant-pair-difference + reservoir weighted logistic fitted by R stats::glm.fit (binomial, maxit 100, warm start, cold retry), after an R pivoted QR in assemble_clogit_reservoir_logit; info recomputed in R. This runs in every bootstrap replicate and in every fixed-beta_T null refit during likelihood-test CI inversion.
- **Claimed speedup:** fastglm's own vignette claims 2.2x (QR) to 4.1x (Cholesky) over glm.fit for logistic n=1000, p=30, rising to 2.9x/7.2x at n=1e5. These are the source's claims, not measured on EDI. More gain is likely at EDI's small n, where R per-call overhead dominates (not quantified). (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same MLE within tolerance. Deterministic, since there are no random draws. glm.fit's convergence criterion (relative deviance change 1e-8) differs from the C++ kernel's, so estimates differ at about tolerance level. The aliased-column drop (pivoted QR tol 1e-7) and the treatment-survives check must be preserved; the C++ kernel's hardened QR column dropping is already used by the sibling cond-logit path. A weights argument is needed because the bootstrap weights are continuous. Implementation burden is small, since the kernel already exists.
- **Suggested action:** Fold into v1.0.5 TODO-2: add a prior-weights argument to fast_logistic_regression[_with_var]_cpp. Then switch fit_clogit_reservoir_logit (full fit, bootstrap and fit_null) to it, with an equivalence test against glm.fit and a microbenchmark at typical KK sizes.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_incidence_KK_cond_logit_glmm_abstract.R::fit_clogit_reservoir_logit`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/fastglm/vignettes/benchmarks-fastglm.html https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/fastglm/refman/fastglm.html https://cran.r-project.org/package=fastglm

### conditional-logit-matched-fit: bfgs → The C++ Newton kernel fast_cpoisson_combined_with_var_cpp with a row-weights argument and step-halving: the same concave optimum, quadratic convergence and compiled evaluation. This is the existing catalog §10 Adopt item (v1.0.5 TODO-2), still unimplemented. glm2/fastglm-style deviance-decrease step-halving gives the needed robustness.
- **Current:** R optim(method='BFGS') on R-coded weighted cpoisson NLL and score, maxit 300, reltol 1e-8, in each bootstrap replicate.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by a source for this model. Expected gains: fewer iterations (Newton, about 5 vs BFGS 20-100) and the R-to-C++ move (cf. fastglm's 2-7x for compiled vs R IRLS). (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same estimator within tolerance. The BFGS reltol stop is looser than Newton's gradient-based stop, so results shift slightly toward the true optimum. Deterministic. Needs halving and LDLT-failure checks (the robustness part of the same TODO).
- **Suggested action:** Implement v1.0.5 TODO-2 (weights argument plus halving in fast_cpoisson_combined). Replace the optim call and benchmark the bootstrap loop.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_count_KK_cond_poisson.R::compute_weighted_combined_estimate`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/fastglm/vignettes/benchmarks-fastglm.html https://journal.r-project.org/articles/RJ-2011-012/

### constrained-binomial-mle: irls → Exponential-cone program: maximise Σ y·η + (1−y)·log(1−exp η) subject to Xβ ≤ 0 for the log link, or the identity-link analogue with log μ and log(1−μ) terms. Solve it with a primal-dual interior-point conic solver: ECOS (GPL-3, R binding ECOSolveR) or Clarabel (Apache-2.0, needs Rust). This form needs no feasible start and returns certified dual multipliers. Use it as a fallback when IRLS fails the KKT gate, or as the main solver.
- **Current:** Fisher-scoring IRLS with LDLT and step-halving, infeasible-η rejection, Fisher-metric active-set projection, a KKT convergence gate and a cycle guard. The cold start can be infeasible.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not a speedup at EDI's sizes. Schwendinger, Grün & Hornik 2021 (§4.6, n=500, hard boundary scenarios), as extracted from the open PDF: only ecos, ipopt* and auglag* always reached the optimum, and their timings are 'rather similar' at 10 covariates. ECOS is 'among the quickest' at 100 covariates. Default glm/glm2 IRLS had 99.9% non-convergence at 10 covariates (1.6% with maxit 10,000), against 0% for ECOS. The timings appear only in a figure, so no IRLS-versus-ECOS ratio is quantified. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** This is a robustness finding, not a speed finding. Benefits: no feasible start needed and certified boundary optima. Costs: a new solver dependency (ECOS is GPL-3 C, which is fine; Clarabel needs a Rust toolchain), and the conic solve probably costs more per fit than a converged IRLS at p≈10. Accuracy is set by interior-point tolerances (~1e-8), so results match IRLS only within tolerance. EDI's own projection and KKT gate already fix much of the glm/glm2 failure mode, so the gain over EDI is unmeasured. The holiglm vignette warns that nested exponential cones scale poorly; the log-binomial needs no nesting.
- **Suggested action:** Add 'exponential-cone interior point (ECOS/Clarabel)' to catalog §1 O4. In the v1.0.5 TODO-32 constrained-solver Prototype, A/B test an ECOS fallback for fits that fail kkt_verdict_at_bounds, on boundary-MLE fixtures (e.g. Schwendinger's simulation C designs), measuring converged rate and wall-clock at EDI's n and p.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_log_binomial_regression.cpp::fit_constrained_binomial_cpp_impl`, `R/EDI/src/fast_log_binomial_regression.cpp::fit_constrained_binomial_weighted_cpp_impl`
- **Sources:** https://d-nb.info/1231156651/34 https://link.springer.com/doi/10.1007/s00180-021-01084-5 https://cran.r-project.org/package=holiglm https://www.jstatsoft.org/article/view/v108i07 https://cran.r-project.org/package=ECOSolveR https://cran.r-project.org/package=clarabel https://github.com/embotech/ecos https://github.com/oxfordcontrol/Clarabel.cpp https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.12762

### constrained-binomial-mle: finite-differences → Closed-form score X'(w∘∂ℓ/∂η) and observed Hessian X' diag(w∘∂²ℓ/∂η²) X. For the log link these are (y−μ)/(1−μ) and −(1−y)μ/(1−μ)²; for the identity link, y/μ−(1−y)/(1−μ) and −y/μ²−(1−y)/(1−μ)². Schwendinger et al. 2021 §2 state the gradient X'(y−μ)/(1−μ) and the Gram (X'DX) form of the Hessian.
- **Current:** Central finite differences: score from 2p log-likelihood evaluations (h=1e-6); Hessian from a 4-point stencil with 4·p(p+1)/2 log-likelihood evaluations (h=1e-4). This was still the code at commit 8b718ae (lines 840-920).
- **Claimed speedup:** Theoretical, from the catalog's O5 operation counts (not measured): about p× fewer flops (O(np²) against O(2np³)) and about 2p² fewer exp/log1p calls per Hessian. It also removes the NaN/Inf Hessians that FD stencils produce when they cross the boundary. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: high)
- **Trade-offs:** None statistically: the closed form is exact, and results change only by the FD truncation error (~1e-6 relative), so CI tests need tolerances of at least 1e-5. This is already 'Adopt' (v1.0.5 TODO-1, catalog O5); it is re-reported only because the code still uses FD.
- **Suggested action:** Implement the already-adopted v1.0.5 TODO-1 analytic score and Hessian for the log- and identity-binomial links, weighted and unweighted, and keep an FD test that cross-checks them at interior points.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_log_binomial_regression.cpp::constrained_binomial_score_cpp_impl`, `R/EDI/src/fast_log_binomial_regression.cpp::constrained_binomial_hessian_cpp_impl`, `R/EDI/src/fast_log_binomial_regression.cpp::constrained_binomial_weighted_score_cpp_impl`, `R/EDI/src/fast_log_binomial_regression.cpp::constrained_binomial_weighted_hessian_cpp_impl`
- **Sources:** https://d-nb.info/1231156651/34 https://link.springer.com/doi/10.1007/s00180-021-01084-5

### covariance-from-information: lu-decomposition → Factor once. Either reuse the FullPivLU (lu.inverse()), or, for SPD information, use LDLT/LLT with a pivot-based definiteness check and then solve(Identity).
- **Current:** A FullPivLU invertibility check, followed by a separate information.inverse() that runs a second (partial-pivot) LU factorization.
- **Claimed speedup:** From Eigen's dense decomposition benchmark (float; the page says to roughly double the times for double). At 8x8: LLT 0.05 ms, LDLT 0.07 ms (1.3x), PartialPivLU 0.08 ms (1.5x), FullPivLU 0.10 ms (1.9x). At 100x100, FullPivLU is 10.6x LLT. FullPivLU plus PartialPivLU is about 2.6x one LDLT on the factorization step at 8x8. Not quantified for a whole fit. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** A constant-factor gain of microseconds per fit at p of about 20 or less. The Hessian build, O(n p^2), dominates. The code comment says the current pair is kept on purpose for bit-compatibility. Switching to LDLT changes which inputs are flagged singular.
- **Suggested action:** No new work item. Keep this attached to v1.1.0-TODO-155. The minimal fix is lu.inverse(), which reuses the one factorization. Run an A/B on the vcov fixtures before considering LDLT.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/_helper_functions_core.h::invert_free_information`, `R/EDI/src/fast_negbin_regression.cpp::fast_neg_bin_with_var_cpp`, `R/EDI/src/fast_beta_regression.cpp::fast_beta_regression_with_var_cpp`, `R/EDI/src/fast_ordinal_regression.cpp::fast_ordinal_regression_internal`
- **Sources:** https://libeigen.gitlab.io/eigen/docs-nightly/group__DenseDecompositionBenchmark.html

### cox-ph-fit: breslow-risk-set-cumsum → After the existing order(), take a single within-stratum reverse cumsum of exp(eta - max eta) and read off one risk sum per failure time. This gives the Breslow cumulative hazard and the NLL, and is the same identity coxdev's header and EDI's own compute_cox_neg_ll_only use.
- **Current:** .breslow_hazard does an O(nK) per-event rescan, sum(risk_o[y_o >= t_k]), after it has already sorted. kk_cox_fully_fixed_null_fit uses the R rescan NLL .cox_neg_loglik_breslow_r.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by any source. The complexity drops from O(nK) or O(n^2) to O(n) after the sort. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** None in the estimator; the result matches to floating-point summation order. Verify agreement to 1e-10.
- **Suggested action:** Carried over; the code is unchanged. Implement the audit's Adopt verdict (v1.0.5 audit TODO-7) for .breslow_hazard, and route kk_cox_fully_fixed_null_fit to the C++ compute_cox_neg_ll_only.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/helper_survival_fits.R::.breslow_hazard`, `R/EDI/R/inference_survival_coxph.R::.cox_neg_loglik_breslow_r`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=coxdev https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19081 https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2410.19081

### cox-ph-fit: newton-raphson → Build the rungs lazily, generating the next rung only after the previous fit is rejected. The order is the same and the same design is accepted, so results are bit-identical.
- **Current:** The ≤13-rung correlation-threshold design ladder is built in full (drop_highly_correlated_cols + QR per rung) before the first fit, then fitted in order until the first acceptable fit.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified. It saves up to about 12 × O(np^2) per call, multiplied by the randomization or bootstrap replicates. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Only the code structure changes. The survival NEWS (fetched) confirms the reference fitter offers nothing comparable, so this is internal to EDI.
- **Suggested action:** Carried over; the code is unchanged. Profile shared() on a KK strat-Cox randomization run, and make the ladder lazy if the cost is material.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_survival_KK_strat_cox.R::shared`, `R/EDI/R/inference_survival_KK_strat_cox.R::shared_combined_likelihood`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/survival/news.html

### cox-ph-fit: newton-raphson → Build the native weighted Efron Newton kernel (bayesian_bootstrap_performance.md TODO-6.1) on coxdev's header-only C++, via LinkingTo or a port. It already handles weights, Efron or Breslow ties, strata and start times by reverse cumsums. Assemble X'IX from its eta-space operators in O(np^2), then run EDI's damped LDLT Newton.
- **Current:** Each Bayesian-bootstrap draw calls survival::coxph (Efron ties, weights, warm-started via init), paying R overhead per draw.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by any source; coxdev publishes no timing against coxph (GitHub README, Performance section). The saving is the R-side coxph overhead per draw. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** A new dependency (coxdev, GPL>=3; upstream BSD-3) or a port with attribution. It needs equivalence tests against coxph(weights, ties='efron') with strata and heavy ties.
- **Suggested action:** Carried over (coxdev 0.4.0, 2026-11-07, confirmed this run). When TODO-6.1 is scheduled, prototype it on the coxdev header and benchmark per-draw time at n=500.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/globals.R::weighted_cox_bootstrap_surrogate_fit`, `R/EDI/R/inference_survival_coxph.R::compute_estimate_with_bootstrap_weights`, `R/EDI/R/inference_survival_strat_cox.R::compute_estimate_with_bootstrap_weights`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=coxdev https://github.com/jonathan-taylor/coxdev

### cumulative-link-ordinal-mle: bfgs → Expose the weights argument that FixedOrdinalRegression (ordinal_fixed_link_helpers.h:230) already has through the probit and cloglog C++ wrappers, the same way fast_ordinal_regression_weighted_cpp does for logit. Fit each draw with EDI's C++ Newton, warm-started from the full-data fit. This gives the same weighted cumulative-link MLE, which is concave for probit and cloglog. Cauchit is nonconcave and should keep a multistart or polr check. Mind the cloglog sign convention (eta_sign=+1) when mapping coefficients.
- **Current:** On every Bayesian or nonparametric bootstrap draw, the probit, cauchit and cloglog ordinal classes (and the adjacent-category and stereotype logistic surrogates) call MASS::polr with weights. That means building a data frame and model frame, a glm start, and optim BFGS on exp-increment cutpoints with reltol 1e-10.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not measured for EDI. Harrell's benchmark (fharrell.com/post/mle, rms 7.0, n=100,000, 10 intercepts, p=5): MASS::polr 2.40 s, optim BFGS 0.391 s, compiled Newton-Raphson 0.084 s, about 28x faster than polr. At EDI's smaller n, polr's fixed R overhead (model.frame, glm.fit start) dominates even more. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** The estimator is the same weighted MLE within optimizer tolerance, but the old optimum came from polr's reltol stop, so numbers move at about the 1e-6 level. Deterministic. Needs two small wrapper changes plus tests. Cauchit is excluded or needs a guard because its likelihood is nonconcave. The polr fallback can be kept for failures.
- **Suggested action:** Prototype: add weights to fast_ordinal_{probit,cloglog}_regression_cpp, route weighted_ordinal_bootstrap_surrogate_fit for probit and cloglog through them with Newton and a warm start, and A/B against polr for coefficient agreement and wall-clock time on EDI's bootstrap fixtures.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/globals.R::weighted_ordinal_bootstrap_surrogate_fit`
- **Sources:** https://fharrell.com/post/mle/ https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/rms/NEWS https://cran.r-project.org/package=MASS

### cumulative-link-ordinal-mle: lbfgs → Damped Newton on the analytic observed Hessian, which is already implemented as optimization_alg='newton_raphson'. This is the catalog's Prototype (v1.0.5 algorithm-audit TODO-1); this run adds source evidence that quantifies the gain.
- **Current:** LBFGSpp L-BFGS is the default for the concave cumulative-link fits (logit, probit, cloglog), stopping on a relative f-decrease.
- **Claimed speedup:** From Harrell's rms 7.0 comparison (ordinal, n=100k, 10 intercepts, p=5): Newton-Raphson 0.084 s against optim BFGS 0.391 s, about 4.7x. The post also reports that BFGS ends with a large residual gradient when tight absolute convergence is needed. That benchmark is dense BFGS, not L-BFGS, at larger n than EDI's. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same unique MLE (strictly concave), so the result is the same within tolerance and gradient-certified. Newton steps may fail from far starts, but the fallback to L-BFGS already exists. Not for cauchit. This adds evidence for an item already tracked; it is not a new option.
- **Suggested action:** Use this evidence to move v1.0.5 algorithm-audit TODO-1 (Newton default for concave ordinal fits) forward. Run the A/B harness at EDI's sizes.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_ordinal_regression.cpp::fast_ordinal_regression_internal`, `R/EDI/src/fast_ordinal_probit_regression.cpp::fast_ordinal_probit_regression_internal`, `R/EDI/src/fast_ordinal_cloglog_regression.cpp::fast_ordinal_cloglog_regression_internal`
- **Sources:** https://fharrell.com/post/mle/ https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/rms/NEWS

### cumulative-link-ordinal-mle: analytic-derivatives → Use the exact-width cell differences from rms orm_links.f90 celldiff (reworked in rms 8.2-0, 2026-10-22). Logistic: 2 sinh(dl/2) sqrt(f1 f2). Cloglog: g2 * (-expm1(-t2 * expm1(dl))). Cauchit: atan2(dl, 1 + x1 x2)/pi. Probit: difference the smaller-valued tail (Phi-bar when x2 > 0). The top and bottom categories use the complementary CDF through argument flipping.
- **Current:** The cell probability is computed as cdf(alpha_k + s*eta) - cdf(alpha_{k-1} + s*eta), with the top category as 1 - cdf, then floored at 1e-12. Both cancel catastrophically when both CDFs are near 1 or the cell is narrow, and the floor zeroes the gradient information.
- **Claimed speedup:** None. This is a robustness and accuracy improvement at the same cost: O(1) per observation, a few extra transcendental calls. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** This is about robustness, not speed. It matters only with extreme linear predictors (near-separation, far starts, resampling draws with sparse categories), where the current 1e-12 floor and the cancellation can stall the optimizer or bias the information matrix. The gradient and Hessian need consistent treatment. rms notes that its Hessian terms still cancel for narrow cells on non-logistic links. rms is GPL (>= 2), so its approach can be ported with attribution.
- **Suggested action:** Investigate: add a tail-accurate cell-probability helper per link in ordinal_fixed_link_helpers.h, and test it against high-precision references for extreme eta. This upgrades the catalog §2 incidental note on cancellation, using the rms 8.2-0 rework as evidence.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/ordinal_fixed_link_helpers.h::neg_log_likelihood`, `R/EDI/src/ordinal_fixed_link_helpers.h::FixedOrdinalRegression`
- **Sources:** https://github.com/harrelfe/rms/blob/master/src/orm_links.f90 https://github.com/harrelfe/rms/blob/master/src/ormll.f90 https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/rms/NEWS

### cumulative-link-ordinal-mle: irls → fastglm::fastglm_ordinal(nonparallel=) (fastglm >= 0.1.3, CRAN 0.1.4 dated 2026-11-11). It is a compiled C++ PPO maximum-likelihood fit (logit, probit, cloglog) using observed-information Newton steps, with a block factorization when there are many categories.
- **Current:** The first PPO ladder rung is VGAM::vglm(cumulative, parallel=), an R-level IRLS/Fisher-scoring fit.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified: the NEWS and the ordinal vignette give no timings against vglm, clm or polr. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: unknown; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** weights= is documented as frequency counts, so how it behaves with fractional bootstrap weights is unknown. It stops with convergence.reason='ordering_boundary' on improper fits, where vglm behaves differently. No cauchit. Adds a dependency (GPL (>= 2), compatible).
- **Suggested action:** Re-look, as in the previous run: A/B fastglm_ordinal(nonparallel=) against vglm on EDI's PPO fixtures for agreement, improper-fit handling and wall-clock time. Check fractional weights.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_ordinal_partial_proportional_odds.R::fit_vgam`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=fastglm https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/fastglm/news/news.html https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/fastglm/vignettes/ordinal-fastglm.html

### cumulative-link-ordinal-mle: multistart → The cumulative cloglog log-likelihood is concave in (alpha, beta), because the extreme-value density is log-concave (Pratt 1981, as stated in Kosmidis). The MLE is therefore unique, and restarts only repair non-convergence. Gate the restarts on a failed convergence certificate (gradient norm plus a positive-definite Hessian, or the Newton decrement), or replace them with a damped-Newton polish.
- **Current:** Four deterministic refits from the primary solution (treatment coefficient set to 0, reflected, shifted by -1 and +1), keeping the lowest NLL.
- **Claimed speedup:** No source quantifies it. From the code structure, up to 4 fewer fits per call when the certificate passes. This is theoretical, not measured. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same estimator, deterministic. The risk is a certificate threshold that is too loose. Kosmidis notes concavity can fail only with negative adjusted counts, which does not apply to the MLE.
- **Suggested action:** As in the previous run: gate the cloglog restarts on a convergence certificate, or replace them with a Newton polish, then A/B on the null-refit multistart cases.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_ordinal_cloglog.R::.cloglog_full_data_multistart`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/1204.0105 https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/1204.0105

### dense-linear-algebra: cholesky-ldlt → Form only one triangle with a symmetric rank-k update: `MtM.setZero(); MtM.selfadjointView<Eigen::Lower>().rankUpdate(M.transpose());` and pass the selfadjoint view to LDLT. This is Eigen's SYRK path; only the meaningful triangle is updated. Inside the OpenMP region, prefer Eigen's rankUpdate over the BLAS-backed symmetric_crossprod (cblas_dsyrk) to avoid nested multithreaded BLAS. Optionally accumulate MtM row by row while M is filled, so the n x p matrix is never stored.
- **Current:** In each of B replicates, after building the n x p resampled design M, `M.transpose() * M` is a general GEMM that computes all p^2 entries of the Gram matrix (O(n p^2)). It dominates the replicate cost at n>>p, ahead of the p^3/3 LDLT.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by a source. Theoretical: about half the flops of the dominant Gram build (n p(p+1)/2 vs n p^2 multiply-adds), so up to ~2x on that term per replicate. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same estimator and determinism (each replicate is single-threaded, seeded by index), but rounding differs at the last bits because the summation order changes, so bit-identical regression tests need tolerance. The LDLT residual guard is unaffected. The same full-GEMM pattern appears in ols_distr_parallel.cpp:183 (bordered-normal-equations entry, outside this problem).
- **Suggested action:** Swap the Gram build for selfadjointView().rankUpdate in compute_rand_bootstrap_ols_parallel_cpp. Benchmark paired ABBA at typical n (100-1000) and p (3-20), and check results match to 1e-12.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/rand_bootstrap_ols_parallel.cpp::compute_rand_bootstrap_ols_parallel_cpp`
- **Sources:** https://libeigen.gitlab.io/eigen/docs-nightly/classEigen_1_1SelfAdjointView.html https://libeigen.gitlab.io/eigen/docs-nightly/group__DenseDecompositionBenchmark.html

### dense-linear-algebra: sherman-morrison → Use the algebraically identical form W_g = SSW_g + (v_e/a_g) * S_g^2/m_g, where SSW_g = sum (r - rbar_g)^2 is computed by a two-pass or Welford pass over the group. Both terms are nonnegative, so there is no cancellation. The cost is the same O(n) (one more pass over a small group, or Welford in the same pass).
- **Current:** The per-group compound-symmetry quadratic form is computed as W_g = Q_g - (v_b/a_g) S_g^2 (Q_g = sum r^2, S_g = sum r, a_g = v_e + m_g v_b), a difference of two nearly equal large quantities when v_b >> v_e. For m_g = 1 it equals r^2 v_e/(v_e+v_b), and it carries a relative error of about eps*(1+v_b/v_e).
- **Claimed speedup:** none (robustness finding, same O(n) cost) (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** This is about robustness, not speed. It matters only near the boundary where the random-intercept variance dwarfs the residual variance (v_b/v_e of roughly 1e6 or more), where W, the log sigma_e gradient and L-BFGS line searches lose digits. Rounding changes the last bits elsewhere. The cancellation mechanism is the textbook one-pass variance problem (Chan, Golub & LeVeque 1983).
- **Suggested action:** Add a unit test with v_b/v_e = 1e8 and singleton groups that compares W against long-double or MPFR. If it shows error above 1e-8 relative, switch to the centred form in neg_ll_and_grad and the matching Hessian code.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_gaussian_lmm.cpp::neg_ll_and_grad`
- **Sources:** https://www.johndcook.com/blog/?p=455 https://doi.org/10.1080/00031305.1983.10483115
- **No open copy found** for doi:10.1080/00031305.1983.10483115 (publisher page blocks automated access)

### design-matrix-preprocessing: pairwise-correlation-filter → Add a helper, e.g. drop_highly_correlated_cols_ladder(M, thresholds), that removes constant columns once and computes R = cor(M) once. For each threshold t, it takes pairs = which(abs(R) > t, upper triangle), drops unique(pairs[,2]) and returns the kept js. That is exactly what the current function returns at each t. It holds because each Pearson r_ij uses only columns i and j, stats::cor computes each pair from per-column quantities, constant-column removal does not depend on t, and the previous finding showed round 2 never fires. Remove the repeat loop in the single-threshold function as well (previous finding).
- **Current:** Each caller's threshold ladder calls drop_highly_correlated_cols(X_cov_orig, thresh) once per rung, on the same matrix: 12 rungs in cox_design_candidates (inference_survival_KK_lwa_cox_ivwc_abstract.R:180-183), KK strat Cox (:158-160), KK rank-regr IVWC (:146-148) and KK GEE (inference_mixin_kk_gee_shared.R:338-340), and 3 rungs in the Weibull frailty fit (:303-306). Each call recomputes apply(var) and a full stats::cor and then repeats cor once more in a redundant second round, so a call costs up to 24 O(np^2) correlation computations, per replicate on BB paths.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by any source. By the argument above, the correlation work per call drops from up to 24 cor() calls (12 rungs × 2 rounds) to 1, and from 6 to 1 for the 3-rung frailty ladder. The downstream QR and fit per candidate are unchanged, so the end-to-end gain depends on how cor cost compares with the fits. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Should give identical keep-sets at every rung, since it is the same cor entries and the same thresholding. I have not verified bitwise equality of cor on a column subset against a sub-block of the full cor; add a test. If the incidental #8 over-drop fix (drop one column at a time) is adopted later, the ladder helper must change too. With one-at-a-time dropping, the kept sets are still computable from a single R. Swapping stats::cor for a BLAS crossprod (coop::pcor style) would be faster still at large p, but it is not bitwise identical, and multithreaded BLAS can break determinism at a threshold boundary, so I do not recommend it.
- **Suggested action:** Implement a single-cor threshold-ladder helper and use it at the five ladder call sites. Combine it with the catalog §10/§11 incidental notes to cache the candidates per call, since the predictors do not depend on the bootstrap weights. Gate on a test that the per-rung keep-sets are identical to the current function's across the test corpus.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/helper_model_matrix.R::drop_highly_correlated_cols`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/caret/news/news.html https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/coop/readme/README.html

### design-matrix-preprocessing: qr-decomposition → Use a single column-pivoted QR with the required columns forced to the front (LAPACK DGEQP3 JPVT 'leading columns'), with rank taken from a |R_kk| threshold. Or keep the greedy order and append columns with O(np) QR updates instead of refactoring (Hammarling & Lucas; fastQR on CRAN provides add/delete-column updates under GPL>=2).
- **Current:** Greedy selection: a fresh ColPivHouseholderQR of the growing trial matrix for every required column and every candidate column, plus one QR on the residualized remainder. About O(n p^3).
- **Claimed speedup:** Hammarling & Lucas say updating is 'much faster' than refactoring, with no number given. The UpdatableQRFactorizations.jl README benchmarks add_column! at 0.000635 s against a stdlib qr at 0.0069 s (n=1000, m=128), about 10x per added column. O(n p^3) → O(n p^2) is theoretical. Not quantified at EDI's p. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: unknown; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** The keep-set can change on near-collinear designs (pivot and threshold differences). The keep-set is cached, so this path is not hot. DGEQP3 does not return rank. Unifying the rank threshold with matrix_rank_cpp's 1e-7 is a separate consistency item.
- **Suggested action:** Keep as the existing Prototype-lite (v1.0.5 catalog §1 item 6). Prototype it with column-append updates, which keeps the current greedy keep-set semantics, and gate on keep-set equality across the test corpus.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/qr_reduce_design_matrix.cpp::qr_reduce_preserve_cols_cpp`
- **Sources:** https://www.netlib.org/lapack/explore-html/d0/dea/group__geqp3_gae96659507d789266660af28c617ef87f.html https://eprints.maths.manchester.ac.uk/1192/ https://cran.r-project.org/package=fastQR https://github.com/SebastianAment/UpdatableQRFactorizations.jl

### exact-conditional-tests: noncentral-hypergeometric-enumeration → For p-value-only calls: (a) call EDI's existing zhang_exact_fisher_pval_cpp(n11, n10, n01, n00, delta_0), which uses the same minlike rule with relative tolerance 1e-7 in log space; or (b) the version-safe minimal change: pass conf.int = FALSE to fisher.test, which drops both CI uniroots in every supported R version; or (c) once EDI's minimum R includes it, use fisher.test(..., pval.only = TRUE), new in R-devel, which also skips the CMLE.
- **Current:** pval_exact_fisher(delta_0) calls the full stats::fisher.test(table, or = exp(delta_0), conf.level) on the single-stratum path. Besides the O(S) minlike p, that runs a uniroot for the CMLE and two uniroots for the CI limits, each step an O(S) pmf pass. Only the default call (delta_0 = 0, alpha = 0.05) is cached.
- **Claimed speedup:** R-devel NEWS (2026-05-27) says pval.only gives 'substantial speed gains in the case of large counts' but gives no number. By construction it removes the ~3 uniroot searches (tens of O(S) pmf passes) per p-value call. Not measured. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same minlike p within 1e-7 relative tolerance. Option (a) needs an equality test against fisher.test(...)$p.value over a grid of tables and delta_0 values, including the boundaries x = lo/hi and delta_0 = ±Inf, which the C++ returns as NA. Deterministic; GPL-2+ R code. The gain only matters on paths that call pval_exact_fisher repeatedly, such as CI inversion and resampling.
- **Suggested action:** Low priority. Route the single-stratum pval_exact_fisher through zhang_exact_fisher_pval_cpp, or at least pass conf.int = FALSE, keeping the full fisher.test for the CI and estimate. Gate it with an equality test against fisher.test(...)$p.value. Revisit pval.only when EDI's minimum R (currently >= 3.5.0) includes it.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_indicidence_exact_fisher.R::get_exact_fisher_htest`
- **Sources:** https://developer.r-project.org/blosxom.cgi/R-devel/2026/05/27 https://raw.githubusercontent.com/wch/r-source/trunk/src/library/stats/R/fisher.test.R https://stat.ethz.ch/pipermail/r-help/2005-November/082935.html

### exact-conditional-tests: exact-binomial-test → R's .binom_test_two_sided_pval_minlike: find the opposite-side boundary (a linear scan on one side of the mode, or a binary search for n >= 1234, contributed by Ravi Varadhan), then p = pbinom(yL) + pbinom(yR-1, upper). That is O(log n) dbinom calls plus two pbinom calls.
- **Current:** Minlike two-sided p computed by evaluating R::dbinom at all n+1 outcomes and summing exp of those at or below the observed density × (1+1e-7): O(n) dbinom calls per p-value.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by the source; by construction it goes from O(n) to O(log n) dbinom evaluations for large n, or about 2x fewer below n = 1234. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Same minlike p (R uses the same 1e-7 relative tolerance). The pbinom tail sums may differ from the explicit sum in the last bits. At EDI's discordant-pair counts d, the per-call cost is microseconds even inside the Zhang CI bisection, so the practical gain is small. GPL-2+ source.
- **Suggested action:** Low priority. When zhang_exact_speedups.cpp is next touched, port R's boundary search plus the pbinom tails; otherwise Keep.
