(applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Off the hot path: only the KK21 fallback runs it, so the overall gain is small. The fastglm benchmarks are the package author's own. A new dependency (fastglm) is avoidable by reusing fast_neg_bin_cpp. Better convergence is a robustness gain as well as a speedup. Rows where glm.nb ended at a degenerate theta above 1e6 would change.
- **Suggested action:** Low priority: replace MASS::glm.nb in robust_negbinreg with fast_neg_bin_cpp (preferred, already in EDI), or with fastglm_nb. Then re-run the KK21 count-matching fixtures.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/helper_robust_regression.R::robust_negbinreg`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/fastglm/vignettes/benchmarks-fastglm.html https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/fastglm/vignettes/nb-convergence-fastglm.html https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/fastglm/news/news.html https://cran.r-project.org/package=fastglm

### nonparametric-bootstrap-distribution: nonparametric-bootstrap → Cheap bootstrap (Lam 2022): ψ̂ ± t_{B,1−α/2}·S with S² = (1/B)Σ(ψ*_b − ψ̂)². It is asymptotically valid with B as small as 1, typically B ≈ 5–10, and Lam & Liu (ICML 2023) give finite-sample coverage bounds. New since the last run: the Studentized Cheap Bootstrap (He, Lam & Yan, arXiv 2606.25968, 2026-06-24) gets higher-order coverage, as conventional studentization does, from very few Monte Carlo replications. That makes a cheap counterpart for the (θ*, se*) studentized CI path possible too. Both would be offered as new opt-in CI types for classes with expensive refits.
- **Current:** B (default ~501) full refits per bootstrap CI. The studentized path needs B fits including the SE, in a serial loop.
- **Claimed speedup:** Refits drop from ~501 to ~5–10 (~50–100x fewer), by arithmetic, not a measured benchmark. Lam's ar5iv simulation (previous run): coverage 0.92 at B = 5 and 10 against 0.69/0.80 for the percentile bootstrap at the same B. The studentized version's abstract gives no numbers ('very few Monte Carlo replications'). (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** This is a different interval: wider at very small B, and not the B = 501 percentile/BCa/bootstrap-t interval, so it must be an opt-in CI type, not a replacement. Determinism is fine with per-draw seeds. The studentized variant is a preprint not yet in a journal (DOI only 10.48550/arXiv.2606.25968), and its coverage at EDI's sizes needs an A/B in the harness.
- **Suggested action:** Consider an opt-in cheap-bootstrap CI type (B ≈ 10) for classes with expensive refits (GLMM, survival, ordinal). Track the Studentized Cheap Bootstrap (arXiv 2606.25968) as its studentized companion. Validate coverage against the B = 501 percentile, BCa and bootstrap-t intervals in the A/B harness, then record the result in the catalog §9 / P3 options table.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_non_param_boot.R::approximate_bootstrap_distribution_beta_hat_T`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_non_param_boot.R::compute_reusable_bootstrap_worker_distribution`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_non_param_boot.R::approximate_bootstrap_statistics_beta_hat_T`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.00090 https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2202.00090 https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.10974 https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25968

### nonparametric-bootstrap-distribution: nonparametric-bootstrap → k-step bootstrap (Davidson & MacKinnon 1999; Andrews 2002, Econometrica 70(1):119–162): a fixed k Newton / line-search Newton steps from θ̂ per replicate. It is higher-order equivalent to the standard bootstrap for t-CIs and Wald tests under the iid and block bootstraps.
- **Current:** Each replicate is fully refitted to convergence from a warm start at the full-data MLE.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified. Andrews calls it 'a very attractive alternative computationally'. With EDI's warm start, the gain is only the iterations beyond k plus convergence checks: likely ≈1–3x for Newton/IRLS families and more for L-BFGS families. This is an estimate, not measured. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** An approximation to the full-refit bootstrap whose equivalence is asymptotic only. It needs specs that expose a Newton step (Hessian), and it is risky for non-concave likelihoods. Determinism is unaffected. It may give no gain if warm-started refits already converge in 2–3 steps.
- **Suggested action:** First measure how many iterations warm-started bootstrap refits actually take on representative fixtures. Only if that is well above 3 for some family, prototype an opt-in k-step mode for studentized/t-based bootstrap CIs; otherwise record 'no gain at EDI's warm-start setup' in the catalog §9 J options table.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_non_param_boot.R::approximate_bootstrap_statistics_beta_hat_T`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_non_param_boot.R::compute_reusable_bootstrap_worker_distribution`
- **Sources:** https://ideas.repec.org/a/ecm/emetrp/v70y2002i1p119-162.html https://doi.org/10.1111/1468-0262.00271
- **No open copy found** for doi:10.1111/1468-0262.00271 (publisher page blocks automated access)

### normal-cdf-quantile: erfc-normal-cdf → Build the negative-x branch on the scaled complementary error function erfcx, as SciPy xsf log_ndtr and Stan Math PR #3406 do:
- log Phi(x) = log(0.5*erfcx(-x/sqrt2)) - x^2/2 for x<-1 (xsf threshold);
- Phi(x) = 0.5*exp(-x^2/2)*erfcx(-x/sqrt2);
- Mills ratio phi/Phi = sqrt(2/pi)/erfcx(-x/sqrt2), with no exp or log.
erfcx options: Cody rational approximations (Stan PR) or Johnson's Faddeeva Chebyshev tables (MIT; vendored in xsf faddeeva.h). This gives near-machine-precision value and gradient over the whole negative tail with no clamp and no series branch-boundary jump.
- **Current:** Phi = 0.5*fast_erfc(-x/sqrt2) (Cephes), log Phi = log of that, both clamped for |x|>=8 (log Phi flat at -35.05, Phi floored at 6e-16). Catalog TODO 2 / v1.0.5-TODO-22 plans a 4-term asymptotic Mills-ratio series below -8.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not measured against EDI. Stan PR #3406 reports std_normal_lcdf (double) 1.35-1.44x and Phi 1.27-1.43x faster than Stan's previous implementation (A&S 7.1.26 plus Taylor pieces) on an AMD EPYC 9965. Later commits report 1.2-1.4x for z in [-30,30]. Jenkins suite-level ratios were about 1.0, and a bot review notes the benchmark source is missing from the PR. Against EDI's Cephes erfc+log, expect about equal cost: one rational evaluation, and the exp is removed from the log Phi and Mills-ratio paths. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** This is mainly an accuracy and robustness fix: it implements the already-adopted tail fix rather than speeding up current code. Values change only where EDI now clamps (|x|>=8), plus last-ulp differences between the erfcx and Cephes paths if it is used for x<-1. It is deterministic. Licenses: xsf is BSD-3-Clause, Stan Math is BSD-3-Clause and Faddeeva is MIT, all GPL-3-compatible with attribution. The Stan PR is unmerged and still under review.
- **Suggested action:** When implementing v1.0.5-TODO-22, prefer an erfcx kernel (port xsf faddeeva erfcx, or Stan's Cody-based one after merge) over the 4-term asymptotic series, and return log Phi and the Mills ratio from one call for the probit/dep-cens score. Gate: R::pnorm(log.p=TRUE) grid on [-40,40] at 1e-12 rel, re-checked against R >= 4.5.0 given its pnorm underflow change. Also microbenchmark against the current fast_log_pnorm on [-8,8].
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_erfc.h::fast_log_pnorm`, `R/EDI/src/fast_erfc.h::pnorm_fast`, `R/EDI/src/fast_math_utils.cpp::fast_log_pnorm_vec_cpp`
- **Sources:** https://github.com/scipy/xsf/blob/main/include/xsf/stats.h https://github.com/scipy/xsf/blob/main/LICENSE https://github.com/stan-dev/math/pull/3406 https://github.com/stan-dev/math/blob/develop/LICENSE.md https://github.com/scipy/xsf/blob/main/include/xsf/faddeeva.h https://cran.r-project.org/doc/manuals/r-release/NEWS.html

### ols-fit: qr-decomposition → Arm-wise (block-diagonal) solve. With covariates centered at the full-sample means (w-independent), the interacted design spans the same column space as the block-diagonal [(1−w)[1,Xc], w[1,Xc]], so τ̂_Lin = (γ̂1) − (γ̂0) from separate per-arm OLS fits (Ding §30.2.2). Per draw: precompute G = ZᵀZ and Zᵀy once (Z = [1, Xc]); form the smaller arm's Gram G_a and Z_aᵀy in O(min(n_T,n_C)(p+1)²/2) (or both arms directly, n(p+1)²/2, for accuracy); get the other arm by subtraction; then two (p+1)×(p+1) LDLT/LLT solves. If the studentized statistic or HC2 is needed, per-arm leverages h_i = z_iᵀG_arm⁻¹z_i give the HC2 sandwich of the interacted fit, since the EHW sandwich is invariant to the reparametrization. That equivalence is my algebra, not a fetched statement; verify it numerically. Build this into the C++ batch kernel that candidate 9 already plans.
- **Current:** Lin (2013) interacted OLS: stats::lm.fit (Householder QR) on the n×(2p+2) design [1, w, Xc, w·Xc], followed by ols_hc2_post_fit_cpp. In the randomization null (compute_fast_randomization_distr, catalog §10 Prototype TODO candidate 9) this full QR is redone for every draw, about 8n(p+1)² flops per draw plus R call overhead.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by any source. Theoretical flop ratio about 8n(p+1)² / (n(p+1)²/2) ≈ 16x per draw (≈32x with the smaller-arm subtraction), on top of the 10–100x the catalog expects from moving the loop to C++. The Eigen benchmark shows normal-equations LLT at 4.3–5x faster than HouseholderQR even before the column-count halving. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same OLS solution up to roundoff, not bit-identical to lm.fit. Normal equations square κ (already accepted for fast_ols.cpp, catalog §1 row 'least squares'), and the G − G1 subtraction adds cancellation, so with poorly scaled covariates form both arm Grams directly. reduce_design_matrix_preserving_treatment drops collinear columns of the interacted design; the arm-wise form must reproduce the same drops (e.g. a covariate constant within one arm drops only that arm's slope) or the estimator differs. The HC2 equivalence needs a numerical check against lm.fit + ols_hc2_post_fit_cpp. It is deterministic, with no RNG change.
- **Suggested action:** Fold into catalog §10 TODO candidate 9 (C++ batch randomization kernel for Lin): implement the per-draw step as arm-wise Gram + two small LDLT solves rather than a QR of the interacted design. Gate it on equality with the current lm.fit path (tolerance about 1e-10) on fixtures that include within-arm-constant covariates.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_continuous_lin.R::shared`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/html/2305.18793 https://doi.org/10.1214/12-aoas583 https://arxiv.org/abs/1208.2301 https://eigen.googlesource.com/mirror/+/03f646b7e352a68f26fff252615bedb4a2b359aa/doc/DenseDecompositionBenchmark.dox

### pairwise-distance-matrix: brute-force-distance → Also accept a compiled user distance: an RcppXPtrUtils::cppXPtr pointer routed through EDI's existing user-compiled-fn plumbing, as parallelDist does for custom distances. Keep the R-function path, but hoist the xi/xj buffers out of the loop and evaluate through a pre-built call (Rf_lang3 + Rf_eval) rather than Rcpp::Function::operator().
- **Current:** One Rcpp::Function call per pair (n(n−1)/2 R calls), allocating two fresh NumericVectors per pair.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified for this kernel. Mailing-list measurements seen in search results (not fetched; indicative only) put Rcpp::Function at several times the cost of a direct call. The XPtr path removes R interpreter overhead per pair entirely. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Adds API surface (a compiled distance). The R path stays inherently O(n²) R calls. The end-to-end effect is small because the matrix is cached per design and the ompr MILP dominates. Deterministic, with no numerical change.
- **Suggested action:** Low-priority re-look: add XPtr custom-distance support and hoist the per-pair allocations. Otherwise keep the verdict 'Keep (inherent to a black-box R fn)'.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/optimal_blocks_distance.cpp::distance_matrix_custom_cpp_impl`, `R/EDI/src/optimal_blocks_distance.cpp::distance_matrix_custom_cpp`, `R/EDI/src/optimal_blocks_distance.cpp::optimal_blocks_distance_matrix_cpp`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=parallelDist https://cran.r-project.org/package=RcppXPtrUtils

### pairwise-distance-matrix: mahalanobis-quadratic-forms, gauss-jordan-elimination → LLT of (S + relative ridge × mean diag S), whiten once (Z = X L⁻ᵀ), then a direct per-pair squared-Euclidean loop on the rows of Z, not the Gram expansion: O(np² + n²p). This is the existing Adopt verdict, confirmed again.
- **Current:** Gauss-Jordan explicit S⁻¹ with an absolute 1e-8 ridge, then a per-pair matvec d'S⁻¹d: O(n²p²).
- **Claimed speedup:** About p× on the pair loop (the audit's theoretical estimate). The Eigen benchmark at 8x8 gives LLT 0.05 ms against PartialPivLU 0.08 ms, which affects only the O(p³) part. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Equal within tolerance, not bit-equal: MILP tie-breaking on near-equal distances may change. A relative ridge changes values slightly for badly scaled covariates (a robustness gain).
- **Suggested action:** No new action: this confirms v1.0.5-TODO-29 (catalog §8 TODO-1). Implement with a relative ridge and keep the direct-difference loop on Z.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/optimal_blocks_distance.cpp::distance_matrix_mahal_cpp`, `R/EDI/src/optimal_blocks_distance.cpp::compute_mahal_inverse_covariance`, `R/EDI/src/optimal_blocks_distance.cpp::invert_matrix_cpp`
- **Sources:** https://skfolio.org/generated/skfolio.utils.stats.squared_mahalanobis_dist.html https://libeigen.gitlab.io/eigen/docs-nightly/group__DenseDecompositionBenchmark.html

### parametric-bootstrap-simulation: parametric-bootstrap → k-step parametric bootstrap: per replicate, take a fixed small number k of Newton steps from the original-sample estimate instead of iterating to convergence. Offered as an opt-in mode (re-reported from the previous run; still stands).
- **Current:** B = 199 replicates, each a full constrained and unconstrained refit to convergence, warm-started from the base MLE
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by the sources. Davidson & MacKinnon (1999) say only that very few steps are needed, fewer for LR tests and with Newton than quasi-Newton. The saving is the iterations beyond k per refit, which is modest because replicates are already warm-started. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Estimator change at finite n: the bootstrap statistics are not the fully converged refits. They are asymptotically higher-order equivalent (Andrews 2002), but not identical. Deterministic. Needs per-family Newton steps that the C++ refits already expose.
- **Suggested action:** Record the k-step bootstrap in the catalog §9 J options table as an estimator-change option. Before prototyping, measure the iteration counts the warm-started workers actually use. If they converge in 2–3 Newton steps already, drop it.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_param_boot.R::run_param_bootstrap_replicates`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_param_boot.R::compute_lik_ratio_bootstrap_two_sided_pval`, `R/EDI/R/inference_ext_param_bootstrap_estimate.R::run_param_bootstrap_estimate_replicates`
- **Sources:** https://ideas.repec.org/a/ier/iecrev/v40y1999i2p487-508.html https://doi.org/10.1111/1468-2354.00026
- **No open copy found** for doi:10.1111/1468-2354.00026 (publisher page blocks automated access)

### performance-autotuning: ab-timing-comparison → Analyze the per-round paired differences a_i − b_i with a paired t-test or Wilcoxon signed-rank test, or a seeded percentile-bootstrap CI. Accept only when the CI lies entirely beyond a noise band (keep 5%). Stop adaptively: add pairs until the CI is decisive, with a minimum and a maximum number of pairs (perfgate-paired; zenbench). Randomizing the order within each round (RMIT, Laaber et al. 2019) is the established design for a noisy host. Melis 2026 gives designs whose contrast estimators stay consistent under stateful bias.
- **Current:** Interleaved ABAB timing, analyzed unpaired. A candidate is accepted if the median improves by at least 5% and the absolute gain exceeds 2·IQR(candidate). Repetitions are fixed (3/5/9 presets) and there is no multiplicity control.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified for repetition savings. Google Benchmark reports about 40% lower run-to-run variance from random interleaving, which EDI already does. Laaber et al. report detecting slowdowns of 10% or less with same-instance randomized interleaving. Adaptive stopping saves repetitions on clear wins and losses (the tools describe this qualitatively). (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Developer tooling only; package results are unchanged. The benefit is partly robustness (fewer false accepts across hundreds of cells), not only speed. A bootstrap needs a fixed seed to stay deterministic. Adaptive stopping needs a sequential correction or a conservative alpha.
- **Suggested action:** No verdict change. Implement the paired-difference half of v1.0.5-TODO-12 as described (paired test or seeded bootstrap CI, 5% noise band, adaptive stopping with a minimum and maximum number of pairs), and self-check it on seeded synthetic timings.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/local_machine_tuning_harness.R::edi_tuning_accept_candidate`, `R/EDI/R/local_machine_tuning_harness.R::edi_tuning_interleaved_ab`, `R/EDI/R/local_machine_tuning_harness.R::edi_tuning_blocked_ab`
- **Sources:** https://docs.rs/crate/perfgate-paired/latest/source/README.md https://docs.rs/crate/zenbench/0.1.1/source/METHODOLOGY.md https://research.chalmers.se/publication/?id=511491 https://doi.org/10.1007/s10664-019-09681-1 https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17261 https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2606.17261
- **No open copy found** for doi:10.1007/s10664-019-09681-1 (publisher version is paywalled)

### permuted-block-randomization: permuted-blocks → Generalise generate_permutations_spbr_internal (generate_permutations.cpp) to accept a vector of block sizes, or add a sibling that does. Map keys to dense integer ids once, keep reusable per-stratum std::vector<int> state, loop over all r draws inside one C++ call, and write directly into an n×r integer matrix. This is the batched pattern EDI already uses for SPBR, and the one randomizr 2.0.1 adopted for block_ra.
- **Current:** draw_ws_raw runs replicate(r, random_block_size_redraw_w_cpp(keys, …)): one .Call per replicate. Each call converts n CharacterVector entries to std::string, does a std::map<std::string, std::vector<double>> lookup per subject (O(n log S·|key|)), allocates a new vector per block, returns double, and then the result is coerced back through as.numeric/matrix.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not measured for EDI. As an analogue, randomizr 2.0.1 NEWS reports 5–8× (e.g. N=20,000 with 2,000 blocks: 8.9 ms → 1.1 ms) from replacing per-block R-level calls with a single C++ call. The EDI gain comes from removing r R→C++ round trips plus the per-replicate string conversion and map lookups. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same randomization distribution, but a different draw stream for a given seed: one seed per call instead of one unif_rand seed per replicate, unless per-draw seeds are derived by index, which also keeps future OpenMP parallelism deterministic. This is a constant-factor win only. The draw is usually cheap next to the test statistic, so profile before prioritising. It can be done together with the incidental #2 / F1 correctness clean-up already planned for this design.
- **Suggested action:** Prototype a batched random-block-size kernel with dense stratum ids, modelled on generate_permutations_spbr_internal. Check marginal and block-size distribution equivalence against the current function, and benchmark draw_ws_raw at typical n (100–1000) and r (1000–10000).
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/random_block_size_speedups.cpp::random_block_size_redraw_w_cpp`, `R/EDI/R/design_seq_one_by_one_random_block_size.R::assign_wt`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/randomizr/news/news.html https://cran.r-project.org/package=randomizr

### pvalue-combination: pvalue-combination-rules → Compute the central log-choose terms (log C(row1,x) + log C(row2,col1-x)) once per data set, cached alongside incid_exact_zhang_stats or computed in a per-CI-search C++ helper. Each delta_0 then needs only log_w[x] = logdc[x] + x*log_or, a log-sum-exp and the two-sided sum. This is the pattern R's own fisher.test uses: logdc is computed once by do.logdc, and dnhyper adds log(ncp)*support for each ncp.
- **Current:** Every evaluation of the reservoir p-value at a new delta_0, i.e. every step of the Zhang exact-CI bisection, rebuilds the full noncentral-hypergeometric log-weights. That is 3(S+1) std::lgamma calls plus 2 more, where S = support size, although only the x*log_or term depends on delta_0.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by any source. Per evaluation it replaces about 3(S+1) lgamma calls with S+1 multiply-adds; the log-sum-exp and exp calls remain, so the expected gain is roughly 2-4x on that function. It is multiplied by the number of bisection steps in each CI search, which is the hot path for this row. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same p-values up to floating-point rounding of the summed log-weights. It is deterministic and has no thread or seed dependence. The absolute saving is small (microseconds per call at EDI's reservoir sizes), so it matters only for repeated CI searches, e.g. inside simulations or run_all_inference. It needs a cache keyed on the 2x2 margins.
- **Suggested action:** Low-priority micro-optimisation. When touching zhang_exact_speedups.cpp, add a variant that takes precomputed central log-weights (or a vectorised p(delta_0) over the bisection), and test it for equality against the current function.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/zhang_exact_speedups.cpp::fisher_noncentral_two_sided_pval`, `R/EDI/R/inference_helpers_zhang.R::zhang_pval_exact_combined`
- **Sources:** https://raw.githubusercontent.com/wch/r-source/trunk/src/library/stats/R/fisher.test.R

### pvalue-combination: pvalue-combination-rules → Tail-accurate equivalents at the same O(1) cost: qnorm(p/2, lower.tail=FALSE), and 2*pnorm(abs(z), lower.tail=FALSE) for the combined p; -expm1(2*log1p(-p)) for min-p; and optionally the df=4 closed form p1*p2*(1 - log(p1*p2)) for Fisher. For CCT, use the ACAT form: 1/(p*pi) for p < 1e-15 and pcauchy(T, lower.tail=FALSE).
- **Current:** Stouffer uses qnorm(1 - p/2), which becomes Inf once p/2 < ~1e-16 because 1-p/2 rounds to 1. min-p uses 1-(1-min p)^2, which suffers catastrophic cancellation for min p below ~1e-8. CCT uses tan/atan without the small-p branch (Adopt, v1.0.5 TODO-7, still open).
- **Claimed speedup:** Not a speedup; same cost. This is a numerical robustness finding. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: high)
- **Trade-offs:** Robustness rather than speed. Results are identical for moderate p and correct for extreme p, with no change to determinism. Exact p-values from the binomial and hypergeometric components can be very small at large m, so the Stouffer branch can return 0 or NaN-adjacent values today.
- **Suggested action:** Fold into v1.0.5 TODO-7 (CCT tail numerics): apply the same lower.tail/log1p rewrites to the Stouffer and min-p branches of zhang_combine_exact_pvals, with unit tests at p = 1e-20.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_helpers_zhang.R::zhang_combine_exact_pvals`, `R/EDI/R/inference_suite.R::cct_combine_pvalues_full`
- **Sources:** https://stat.ethz.ch/R-manual/R-devel/library/stats/html/Normal.html https://github.com/yaowuliu/ACAT/blob/master/R/ACAT.R

### pvalue-combination: pvalue-combination-rules → Re-reported from the previous run, still standing. It is the Wasserstein-adjusted discrete combination of Contador & Wu (SJS 2025, DOI 10.1111/sjos.12787; arXiv 2309.07692), extended to Stouffer and the other rules in arXiv 2508.02647 (under revision at JCGS), and implemented in DPComb 1.0.1 (CRAN, MIT), which supports binomial and noncentral-hypergeometric components. Related: mid-p combination with convex-order bounds (Rubin-Delanchy, Heard & Lawson, arXiv 1505.05068), which are conservative yet more powerful. If the exact joint convolution option in catalog §11 is chosen, it can be computed in O(d log d + S log S) rather than O(d*S): sort each component's support by its log-p, take cumulative masses, and use a two-pointer sweep over the threshold log pM + log pR <= obs.
- **Current:** Fisher chi^2_4 / Stouffer / min-p on two discrete exact p-values, treated as continuous, so the test is conservative (Investigate).
- **Claimed speedup:** Not a speedup over the current O(1) combination; this is an estimator (test) change. The sources give no runtime numbers. The sort and two-pointer note is my own complexity remark on the catalog's O(d*S) exact option, not a sourced claim. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Changes the reported p-values and the CI endpoints. The DPComb/Wasserstein and mid-p versions are not exact-size tests, while the exact convolution is exact but needs a tie convention.
- **Suggested action:** Keep as a comparator for resolving the Investigate verdict in catalog §11. Use DPComb as a test oracle if a mid-p or adjusted variant is added. Use the two-pointer sweep if the exact convolution is adopted.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_helpers_zhang.R::zhang_combine_exact_pvals`, `R/EDI/R/inference_helpers_zhang.R::zhang_pval_exact_combined`, `R/EDI/src/zhang_exact_speedups.cpp::fisher_noncentral_two_sided_pval`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=DPComb https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.07692 https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.02647 https://arxiv.org/abs/1505.05068

### quantile-rank-regression: induced-smoothing, sandwich-estimator → aftsrr(se='ISCF', B=1): the Johnson–Strawderman sandwich with closed-form V (viClo: one getSi pass building N² pairwise differences, aggregated by cluster through rowsum(id)). Same smoothed Gehan β̂ and same induced-smoothing slope A. B is unused by viClo, but aftsrr only runs the variance branch when B>0, so B must be ≥1. Optionally keep ISMB as a fallback when ISCF returns a non-finite value.
- **Current:** aftgee::aftsrr(se='ISMB', B=100) whenever a variance is needed. The SE comes from viEmp, a loop over B=100 multiplier draws, each evaluating the smoothed Gehan estimating function in O(n² p). The loop draws from R's RNG, so EDI wraps every call in a digest-hashed set.seed/restore block. That happens in each randomization and bootstrap replicate that needs an SE, and on every design-ladder rung.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by any source. From the aftgee source fetched this run, the variance step drops from B=100 O(n²p) estimating-function evaluations to one O(n²) closed-form pass, so variance-path fits should drop roughly by the share that the 100 resamples take of the total (the point solve is ~10–50 evaluations under BBsolve). The win grows with n because every evaluation is quadratic. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same point estimate. The SE estimator changes: closed-form V vs. multiplier-empirical V, asymptotically equivalent, but finite-sample SEs and Wald CIs differ, so coverage needs an A/B check, especially with matched-pair clusters. It is deterministic and RNG-free, so the seeding wrapper (.kk_rank_regr_aftsrr_seeded) becomes unnecessary for the SE and thread/seed determinism is trivially satisfied (robustness gain). ISCF with clustered ids is coded (rowsum by id) but I found no validation of it. Note also an aftgee bug seen in the source: the ISMB branch reads vISMB$An.nv (nonexistent) instead of An.inv. EDI reads the SE from summary(), so this is likely harmless, but worth a check. GPL≥3 aftgee is already a dependency.
- **Suggested action:** Investigate: on EDI fixtures (matched and reservoir, n 50–1000), compare ISCF vs ISMB(B=100) SE values and timings. If they agree within the A/B tolerance for coverage, switch to se='ISCF', B=1 and drop the RNG wrapper from the SE path. Catalog this as an option on the NEW-rank-AFT row.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_survival_KK_rank_regr_ivwc_abstract.R::aftsrr_for_matched_pairs`, `R/EDI/R/inference_survival_KK_rank_regr_ivwc_abstract.R::aftsrr_for_reservoir`
- **Sources:** https://raw.githubusercontent.com/stc04003/aftgee/master/R/aftsrr.R https://search.r-project.org/CRAN/refmans/aftgee/html/aftsrr.html https://cran.r-project.org/package=aftgee https://doi.org/10.1093/biomet/asp025 https://www.jstatsoft.org/article/view/v061i11
- **Open copies** (publisher not usable): https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3412573/

### quantile-rank-regression: interior-point-lp, barrodale-roberts-simplex → Preprocessing bootstrap (Chernozhukov, Fernández-Val & Melly 2022, Algorithm 4, extending Portnoy–Koenker 1997). Predict residual signs in each replicate from the full-sample β̂(τ), solve the reduced LP on the M ≈ m·(kn)^{1/2} uncertain observations plus two glob observations, then verify the signs and repair any that are wrong. The paper reports numerically identical estimates to the standard solver.
- **Current:** Each bootstrap or randomization replicate re-solves the full quantile-regression LP from scratch (quantreg rq/rq.fit with br, or fn on the proportion point-fit path).
- **Claimed speedup:** CFM 2022 (ar5iv 1909.05782), bootstrap panel for median regression with k=20 and 50 replications: Stata bsqreg simplex 2.63 s vs preprocessing 0.42 s at n=500 (~6×), 3.14 vs 0.63 s at n=1,000 (~5×), 8.19 vs 1.89 s at n=5,000 (~4×). For a single fit at n=500, k=8, preprocessing gives no gain (fn 0.02 s vs pfn 0.04 s). (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** The benchmark is in Stata with k=20. EDI's designs usually have small p (treatment plus a few covariates), where the reduced problem is not much smaller, so the gain is unproven. When the LP solution is non-unique (ties, discrete y), the reduced problem may pick a different vertex than full br. quantreg exposes it only for its own bootstrap (boot.rq.pwxy) and the pfn/ppro fitters; weighted-bootstrap (BB) replicates would need custom code. With R-level formula overhead dominating at n≈50–200, the rq.fit Adopt matters more.
- **Suggested action:** Do not implement now. Record it in the catalog options tables (NEW-quantile-LP / NEW-quantreg) as considered: exact, cited DOI 10.1007/s00181-020-01898-0, gain unproven at EDI's small p. Re-look only if profiling after the rq.fit Adopt shows replicate QR fits dominate at n ≳ 1,000.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_proportion_quantile_regr.R::compute_estimate_with_bootstrap_weights`, `R/EDI/R/inference_continuous_quantile_regr.R::compute_estimate_with_bootstrap_weights`, `R/EDI/R/inference_proportion_quantile_regr.R::fit_quantile_model`
- **Sources:** https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/1909.05782 https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/quantreg/ChangeLog

### random-number-generation: seed-derivation → Key a small-state or counter-based generator directly by (master_seed, draw index), with the within-draw position as the counter. Options are Philox4x32-10 or Threefry from Random123 (header-only, BSD-3-Clause), or xoshiro/PCG seeded through SplitMix64. Child streams need no R parity, because R never produces them. Index-keyed determinism, and therefore thread-count invariance, is kept. Widening the key to 64 bits or more also removes the 32-bit birthday-collision risk.
- **Current:** Each draw index d in the acceptance-rejection loops (rerandomization_helpers.cpp:230, design_fixed_greedy.cpp, optimal_design_search.cpp, binary_match_search.cpp) builds edi_rng::RRng(seed_for_index(master, d)). That means 675 LCG(69069) steps to fill a 624-word MT state, then a full 624-word twist on the first draw, then about n bounded draws for fisher_yates_shuffle. The child seed is truncated to 32 bits.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified for this workload. Random123 docs (fetched) say 'little or no overhead to creating billions of unique' generators and 'a few cycles per byte'. The quuxplusone benchmark (QuickBench) says mt19937 is about '500 times slower to seed' than xoshiro256ss and about half its output speed. Randompack (arXiv 2605.05099) Table 7 gives Philox-4x64 at 1.07-2.75 ns and xoshiro256++ at 0.73-1.07 ns per 64 bits on non-Windows platforms. My own reading of the code, not a measurement: at n=100, p=5 the setup is about 1,300 word operations, against about 100 draws plus 500 FMAs of real work per candidate. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Changes the realized draws, though not their distribution, for seed-pinned rerandomization, greedy and optimal-design tests, so fixtures must be regenerated (bundle with TODO-149(c)). Philox is slow on one Windows build in Randompack's table (10.46 ns), so measure per platform. Part of the gain is robustness rather than speed: with a 32-bit seed, P(two draw indices share a stream) ≈ 1−exp(−D²/2^33), about 0.69 at D=10^5 draws, which would duplicate candidate allocations.
- **Suggested action:** Micro-benchmark RRng(seed_for_index(...)) construction plus the first draw against the per-candidate work in rerandomization_helpers.cpp at n in {50, 200, 500} and p in {2, 10}. If setup is more than about 30% of a candidate, prototype a Philox-keyed (master, index) child stream behind the seed_for_index call sites, with a key of 64 bits or more. Fix the 32-bit seed truncation in any case. Salmon et al. 2011 is doi 10.1145/2063384.2063405.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/RNG.h::seed_for_index`, `R/EDI/src/RNG.h::RRng`
- **Sources:** https://www.thesalmons.org/john/random123/releases/latest/docs/index.html https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DEShawResearch/random123/main/LICENSE https://quuxplusone.github.io/blog/2021/11/23/xoshiro https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05099 https://arxiv.org/html/2605.05099 https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.11084

### random-number-generation: lemire-bounded-integer → Batched ranged random integer generation (Brackett-Rozinsky & Lemire, Software: Practice and Experience 55(1), doi 10.1002/spe.3369; arXiv 2408.06213). It draws k unbiased bounded integers from one word when the product of the ranges is at most 2^L. With L=32, two draws fit per word whenever the remaining length is at most 2^16, which always holds at EDI's n.
- **Current:** One MT 32-bit word per bounded draw (Lemire multiply-shift with rejection), so fisher_yates_shuffle uses about n words per permutation.
- **Claimed speedup:** The arXiv abstract says it can 'more than double the speed of unbiased random shuffling' for small to moderate arrays. Apache Commons RNG-187 estimates 'up to 2-fold for smaller arrays', and adds that slower generators gain more. Neither benchmarks MT19937 with 32-bit words, so the gain for EDI is not quantified. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Changes the seed→permutation stream, so seed-pinned tests need refreshing. Little gain where shuffles are not the bottleneck. Best bundled with any stream-changing work (TODO-149(c), the seed-derivation finding), not adopted alone.
- **Suggested action:** Add as an option row under catalog P2 and bundle it with the stream-changing RNG work under TODO-149(c). The MIT-licensed batched_random repository is a reference implementation to port and test against.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/RNG.h::bounded_rand`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.06213 https://doi.org/10.1002/spe.3369 https://mail-archive.com/issues@commons.apache.org/msg140899.html https://raw.githubusercontent.com/lemire/batched_random/main/LICENSE

### randomization-null-distribution: monte-carlo-randomization → Anytime-valid sequential Monte Carlo testing by betting (Fischer & Ramdas, JRSS-B 2025). Test martingales bet on whether each redrawn statistic is ≥ or ≤ the observed one. The p-process 1/sup W_t is valid at any stopping time, including a budget cap at r. The binomial and binomial-mixture strategies, and the anytime-valid Besag–Clifford variant, are closed form and O(1) per draw. Draws can be generated in batches from the design, so the kernels stay unchanged.
- **Current:** A standalone randomization p-value always uses a fixed r (default 501), with all r assignments drawn up front as an n×r matrix. Sequential Clopper–Pearson stopping runs only inside the CI search. The planned v1.0.6 TODO-156 (Besag–Clifford) would stop early only for futility.
- **Claimed speedup:** From the paper, not measured in EDI. In the previous run's reading of arXiv 2401.07365v5 §7.4 (Fisher sharp-null example, 53 units, p = 0.026, α = 0.05), the binomial strategy needed a mean of 85 permutations, the binomial mixture 147 and Besag–Clifford (h = 10) 200, against EDI's fixed 501, so roughly 3–6× fewer draws. Gains are smaller at α = 0.01, with some power loss. This run re-checked that the paper is unchanged at v5 and published as JRSS-B qkaf014. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** The reported p-value changes from a Monte Carlo tail proportion to an anytime-valid p-process, which is somewhat conservative. Determinism holds if draw b keeps its per-index seed and batches are processed in order. It needs streaming batch generation from the design instead of one n×r matrix. No reference implementation was found (the arXiv page has no code link), but the formulas are closed form, so there are no license issues. It must be opt-in and validated through the A/B harness.
- **Suggested action:** When v1.0.6 TODO-156 (Besag–Clifford) is designed, include the Fischer–Ramdas binomial-mixture / anytime-valid BC rule as a compared option for the standalone p-value. Add it to catalog P4, and correct P4's claim that Besag–Clifford saves draws when the result is clearly significant. Keep fixed r as the default.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_rand.R::compute_rand_two_sided_pval`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_rand.R::rand_pval_from_tail_counts`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_rand.R::approximate_randomization_distribution_beta_hat_T`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_rand.R::generate_permutations`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.07365 https://doi.org/10.1093/jrsssb/qkaf014 https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12853

### randomization-null-distribution: monte-carlo-randomization → Use the Phipson–Smyth exact Monte Carlo p-value (b+1)/(r+1) per tail, two-sided 2·min(b_ge+1, b_le+1)/(r+1) capped at 1, as SciPy permutation_test and perm do. This is a robustness and validity fix, not a speedup.
- **Current:** p = min(1, max(2/r, 2·min(ge, le)/r)) for two-sided tests and max(1/r, ge/r) for one-sided tests. Here r counts draws from the design, which do not include the observed assignment. Catalog §7 P4 describes this as '(b+1)/(r+1)', but the code does not use that rule (the inventory row says 'Not (b+1)/(r+1)').
- **Claimed speedup:** None (cost unchanged, O(r)). Phipson & Smyth say b/r understates the p-value by about 1/r; at r = 501 that is about 0.002, which makes the test slightly anti-conservative near α. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** This is a robustness and validity finding, not a speed finding. Reported p-values shift up by about 1/r, so seed-pinned tests need refreshed fixtures. The existing floor (1/r or 2/r) partly mitigates the b = 0 case, but not b ≥ 1. Determinism is unaffected.
- **Suggested action:** Fix the catalog P4 description of the current rule. Decide, as a policy question, whether to switch to (b+1)/(r+1), or document why b/r with a floor is retained. Bundle the decision with the v1.0.6 TODO-156 stopping-rule work, since both change the reported p-value.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_rand.R::rand_pval_from_tail_counts`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_rand.R::compute_two_sided_randomization_pval_from_t0s`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_rand.R::compute_rand_two_sided_pval`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/1603.05766 https://doi.org/10.2202/1544-6115.1585 https://docs.scipy.org/doc/scipy/reference/generated/scipy.stats.permutation_test.html

### rerandomization-sampling: rejection-sampling → Early abandoning (re-reported from 2026-11-16). Accumulate the per-component terms of f (d_j^2 or |d_j|) one row of M at a time and reject once the running sum exceeds the cutoff. The terms are nonnegative, so the decision is unchanged except for floating-point rounding exactly at the cutoff.
- **Current:** i.i.d. Fisher-Yates candidates, each scored with the full O(np) d = M(2w-1) and then accepted if f(d) <= cutoff.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified for this problem. arXiv 2102.05221 reports a 'substantial speedup' from early abandoning in threshold search, but for elastic distances. The gain is bounded by the O(np) share of each draw, since Fisher-Yates O(n) remains. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Accept/reject could flip only through rounding at the cutoff. The gain is small when p is small (p <= 5) or p_acc is large. It needs a row-major M.
- **Suggested action:** Add a catalog §P6 row. If it is prototyped, benchmark at n = 100-1000, p = 5-20, p_acc = 1e-2 to 1e-4.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/rerandomization_helpers.cpp::rerandomization_search_cpp`, `R/EDI/R/design_fixed_rerandomization.R::generate_rerandomized_w_batch`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.05221

### rerandomization-sampling: rejection-sampling → MCMC and gradient samplers: PSRSRR (arXiv 2602.07613, pair-switching MH plus an asymptotic uniformity correction) and, new this run, Langevin-Gradient Rerandomization (arXiv 2604.07524, SGLD on a continuous relaxation, explicitly non-uniform on the balanced set).
- **Current:** Exact i.i.d. rejection sampling, uniform on {w : f(w) <= a}.
- **Claimed speedup:** PSRSRR: 10-10,000x, about 66x on STAR with n ~ 974 (arXiv 2602.07613). LGR: 'orders of magnitude' in high d, unquantified in the text (n = 500, p_a = 0.01; Figures 1-2 of arXiv 2604.07524). (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Both change the design law. PSRSRR is only asymptotically uniform. LGR is non-uniform, and its FRT is valid only conditional on the LGR mechanism. The 2025 review (arXiv 2512.05290 §7.4) confirms that PSRR and BRAIN are not uniform either. This is a design-option change, not a speedup.
- **Suggested action:** Keep the default sampler. Add arXiv 2604.07524 (doi 10.48550/arXiv.2604.07524) next to PSRSRR in catalog P6's MCMC row as 'invalid for the uniform design law'. If an alternative design is ever offered, it should be a separate, opt-in design.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/rerandomization_helpers.cpp::rerandomization_search_cpp`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/html/2602.07613v1 https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07524 https://arxiv.org/html/2604.07524v1 https://arxiv.org/html/2512.05290v2

### resampling-refit-loop: barrodale-roberts-simplex → (Re-reported, still stands.) The intercept-only refit is the sample τ-quantile, computable with one std::nth_element (O(m)), with all draws batched in C++ using per-draw-index seeds. For p>0 refits, Portnoy–Koenker preprocessing seeded by the observed fit (Chernozhukov, Fernández-Val & Melly) is exact, but its gains appear only at large n.
- **Current:** r calls to quantreg::rq (BR simplex) through formula + data.frame per sign-flip draw, including the intercept-only case rq(yd ~ 1)
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified for the closed form. It removes the model.frame and simplex overhead; the catalog's own estimate for the formula overhead alone is 3–10×. The preprocessing headline (÷100) is at n=50,000, p=20, not at EDI's sizes. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: unknown; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same estimator only if br's vertex choice on ties and on even m is matched. A bit-identity test against rq(yd~1,tau) is needed.
- **Suggested action:** Fold into the existing Adopt (v1.0.5-TODO-19 / catalog TODO-6b). Special-case p==0 to nth_element after a tie-rule identity test, and otherwise keep rq.fit.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_all_KK_quantile_regr_ivwc_abstract.R::qr_intercept_pairs`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_KK_quantile_regr_ivwc_abstract.R::compute_rand_pval_matched_pairs`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_KK_quantile_regr_ivwc_abstract.R::compute_rand_pval_reservoir`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/1909.05782 https://cran.r-project.org/package=quantreg

### resampling-refit-loop: newton-raphson → (Re-reported, updated.) Collapse each draw to per-distinct-event-time arm counts and run Newton at O(K) per iteration. FastSurvival 2.0.0's coxph_fast now does exact Newton-Raphson with coxph's start, convergence rule and Efron/Breslow ties, so it can serve as a reference implementation.
- **Current:** Per draw: gather, re-sort, then O(n)-per-iteration Newton for the p=1 Cox fit
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified for this kernel. FastSurvival 2.0.0 NEWS makes no speed claim for the Newton version. The gain is O(n) to O(K) per iteration, which is small unless ties are heavy. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Low value, because the function has no in-package caller.
- **Suggested action:** Note this in small_kernel_hoists_batch.md Item C, and act only if compute_coxph_rand_bootstrap_cpp gains a caller. Use FastSurvival (MIT) as a test oracle.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_coxph_regression.cpp::compute_coxph_rand_bootstrap_cpp`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=FastSurvival https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/FastSurvival/news/news.html

### resampling-refit-loop: parallel-refit-loop → (Re-reported, still stands.) Fast robust bootstrap (Salibián-Barrera & Zamar 2002; CRAN FRB): one weighted LS solve per replicate using the full-sample robustness weights, plus a linear correction.
- **Current:** OpenMP over B, each replicate a cold OLS-QR-started IRLS robust fit
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified; roughly K× fewer weighted solves than K-iteration IRLS, in theory. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Estimator change (an approximation to the bootstrap distribution), so it is acceptable only as an opt-in. The estimator-preserving speedup is the warm-start Prototype (TODO-8).
- **Suggested action:** Record FRB in the catalog options table as an estimator-change option. Revisit only if a true MM estimator is adopted; ROBU (arXiv 2606.26611) would then also be relevant for its initialisation.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_robust_regression.cpp::compute_robust_rand_bootstrap_parallel_cpp`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=FRB

### ridit-mann-whitney: delong-structural-components → The Brunner & Konietschke (2025) unbiased rank-based estimator of the Mann-Whitney variance. It is built from the same placements, is valid with ties, is non-negative for n_i >= 2, and is L2-consistent.
- **Current:** The DeLong (1988) structural-components SE, sqrt(var(V10)/n_T + var(V01)/n_C), with sample variances of the placements. It is biased at small n, and it returns 0 when an arm has a single subject.
- **Claimed speedup:** None. It costs the same O(n) after the placements EDI already computes. The gain is accuracy: the authors show that existing estimators, including DeLong's, are biased at small samples (not quantified here). (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** This is about robustness and accuracy, not speed. It changes the SE estimator (the point estimate is unchanged), so p-values and CIs would shift at small n, and the TODO-106 type-I calibration would need re-checking. It needs n_i >= 2 in each arm, the same limit as now. The pooled-reference scaling (n_C/N) would need its own derivation. It is a closed form, so there are no license issues.
- **Suggested action:** Investigate: add the unbiased Brunner-Konietschke variance as an option (or as the small-n default) beside the DeLong SE. Compare the type-I error and CI coverage of both at small n_T/n_C with heavy ties, in the TODO-106 simulation.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_ridit_analysis.cpp::ridit_delong_se_from_scores`, `R/EDI/R/inference_ordinal_ridit.R::ridit_delong_se`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.05038 https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2409.05038 https://ideas.repec.org/a/spr/stpapr/v66y2025i1d10.1007_s00362-024-01635-0.html https://doi.org/10.1007/s00362-024-01635-0

### risk-difference-ci: miettinen-nurminen-score, bisection → Closed-form Farrington–Manning/MN cubic for p̃_C in trigonometric form, plus Brent on z(δ) ∓ z_{1−α/2} for each bound. Both are already implemented in C++ in CRAN lrstat 0.3.4: remlRiskDiff clamps the acos argument, handles the repeated root, and uses the pooled estimate at δ≈0; mnRiskDiffCIcpp calls brent(f, -1, est, 1e-6). cicalc 0.2.2 (R) and ratesci 1.1.1 (R) use the same cubic.
- **Current:** Inner: 60-step bisection on the constrained score for p̃_C, with 1e15 boundary sentinels. Outer: ≤50-step bisection on δ per CI bound with tolerance in p-space. Total ≤2·50·60 score evaluations.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by any source. The catalog's theoretical estimate is ~30–60× fewer flops for the inner solve, and Brent cuts the outer loop from ~50 to roughly 8–15 evaluations. Absolute cost is already microseconds. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same estimator within root tolerance. It is deterministic and has no RNG. The extreme cases (x=0, x=n, both proportions 1, δ→±1) need care with the cubic's root choice; see Anderson arXiv 2205.03460. A grid cross-check against the current bisection is required. Use a tighter Brent tolerance than lrstat's 1e-6 on δ to keep EDI's precision. lrstat is GPL (>= 2), so porting it into GPL-3 EDI is fine with attribution.
- **Suggested action:** When implementing v1.0.5 audit TODO-3 and the brent_ci_inversion.md scope extension to mn_ci_internal, port lrstat's remlRiskDiff (src/miettinen_nurminen.cpp) and its Brent outer root. Test against lrstat::mnRiskDiffCI and remlRiskDiff over an (x_t, n_t, x_c, n_c, δ) grid that includes the boundary cases. Low priority.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/miettinen_nurminen_speedups.cpp::mn_constrained_mle_pc_cpp`, `R/EDI/src/miettinen_nurminen_speedups.cpp::mn_ci_internal`, `R/EDI/src/miettinen_nurminen_speedups.cpp::mn_ci_cpp`, `R/EDI/src/miettinen_nurminen_speedups.cpp::mn_pvalue_cpp`, `R/EDI/src/miettinen_nurminen_speedups.cpp::mn_z_statistic_cpp`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=lrstat https://cran.r-project.org/src/contrib/lrstat_0.3.4.tar.gz https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/cicalc/refman/cicalc.html https://cran.r-project.org/package=ratesci https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.03460 https://doi.org/10.1002/sim.4780091208 https://doi.org/10.1002/sim.4780040211
- **No open copy found** for doi:10.1002/sim.4780040211 (publisher page blocks automated access)
- **No open copy found** for doi:10.1002/sim.4780091208 (publisher page blocks automated access)

### risk-difference-ci: newcombe-hybrid-score → Newcombe (1998b) method 10 with the corrected φ. When AD−BC > 0, the numerator becomes max(AD−BC−N/2, 0). This is ratesci's MOVER-NW ('MOVER_newc') and statsmodels' 'newcombe'.
- **Current:** Paired Newcombe interval with the uncorrected Pearson φ = (n11·n00 − n10·n01)/sqrt(margins), and φ = 0 if any margin is 0 (newcombe_speedups.cpp:128-129).
- **Claimed speedup:** none: this is an estimator/accuracy correction, not a speedup (O(1) either way) (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Robustness/accuracy finding, not speed. It changes the paired CI so that it matches Newcombe's published method and the reference implementations.
- **Suggested action:** Resolve the Investigate verdict: apply the N/2 correction to the positive φ numerator, and add a test against ratesci::moverpairci (Wilson base, Newcombe correction) or statsmodels confint_proportions_paired(method='newcombe').
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/newcombe_speedups.cpp::newcombe_paired_ci_cpp`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=ratesci https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/ratesci/news/news.html https://github.com/statsmodels/statsmodels/blob/main/LICENSE.txt

### robust-m-regression: irls → Finite-termination Newton for the Huber M-estimator (Madsen & Nielsen 1990; Chen & Pınar 1998). It is an active-set Newton on the piecewise-quadratic Huber objective: once the inlier set is correct, one step lands exactly on the minimizer. Each step costs O(np²), the same as IRLS.
- **Current:** IRLS with a Huber or bisquare ψ at a fixed MAD scale: an LDLT solve each iteration, stopping when the relative ‖Δβ‖ < 1e-7, with maxit 50 (the KK classes cap it at 10–25 with acc 1e-3/1e-4).
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified. Chen & Pınar's abstract claims only 'improving the efficiency' and proves finite termination with no assumptions on the problem. A separate source reports Huber IRLS converging in 4–5 iterations, so the iteration saving at EDI's sizes may be small. The main gain is exact convergence under capped maxit. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Applies only to Huber 'M' (convex), not to bisquare. The gain is partly robustness/exactness (no truncated-IRLS error when maxit is capped at 10–25) rather than speed. Adds active-set bookkeeping code. Deterministic.
- **Suggested action:** Revisit catalog O8's 'Newton instead of IRLS' row for the Huber path only. Prototype Madsen–Nielsen/Chen–Pınar Newton and A/B its iteration counts and estimate differences against IRLS at n≈50–1000, p≲10, especially in KK randomization draws where maxit is capped.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_robust_regression.cpp::fast_robust_regression_internal`, `R/EDI/R/inference_continuous_KK_robust_regr_one_lik.R::fit_rlm`, `R/EDI/R/inference_continuous_KK_robust_regr_ivwc.R::fit_rlm_with_treatment`
- **Sources:** https://repository.bilkent.edu.tr/items/01e2b27c-57e2-48a5-ac3f-aa024da2221d/full https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02510408 https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01933216 https://arxiv.org/pdf/2311.13291
- **No open copy found** for doi:10.1007/bf01933216 (publisher version is paywalled)
- **No open copy found** for doi:10.1007/bf02510408 (publisher version is paywalled)

### robust-m-regression: irls → A deterministic S-step start for the true-MM prototype: Peña–Yohai candidates (RobStatTM::lmrobdetMM, pyinit) or DetS-style robust-Mahalanobis subsets (statsmodels RLMDetS/RLMDetSMM), then a bisquare M-step at the S-scale. Both remove the need for an RNG-seed contract.
- **Current:** The method labelled 'MM' is a bisquare M-estimate from an LS start: it has no S-step and breakdown 0. A true MM via FAST-S is planned (catalog §1 TODO-4), but FAST-S is RNG-dependent.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified. The catalog's DetS row estimates ~5–20× current per fit, against ~50–200× for FAST-S. None of the sources fetched gives timings at EDI's sizes. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** This is an estimator change (true MM with high breakdown) relative to the current bisquare-M, and slower than it. Compared with FAST-S, it is deterministic and probably cheaper at small p, but high-breakdown guarantees do not all carry over exactly. statsmodels notes that iterating each start to convergence is still a TODO.
- **Suggested action:** Fill catalog O8's DetS '[cite needed]' with Peña & Yohai 1999 (pyinit/RobStatTM) and statsmodels RLMDetS. In the TODO-4 MM prototype, A/B a deterministic start against FAST-S on time per fit and on breakdown under leverage contamination at n≈50–1000, p≲10.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_robust_regression.cpp::fast_robust_regression_internal`, `R/EDI/R/inference_continuous_robust_regr.R::fit_rlm_model`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=pyinit https://cran.r-project.org/package=RobStatTM https://www.statsmodels.org/dev/_modules/statsmodels/robust/resistant_linear_model.html

### sandwich-variance: sandwich-estimator → The p×p (r×r) formulation, as now shipped in estimatr ≥2.0.0 C++ (lm_robust_helper.cpp). Bell–McCaffrey df: trace(P) and ‖P‖²_F come from a few p×p Gram products of per-cluster p-vectors, P = S − U − Uᵀ + D, without forming the G×G or n×G objects. Meat: clusters with n_g > k are reduced to a k×k eigenproblem via a thin QR of [U_c, X_c], giving A^{-1/2}X = X + Q(B^{-1/2} − I)QᵀX. Equivalently, use dfadjust vignette Lemma 2 (generalized inverse), which handles singular I−H_gg. Same CR2 estimator and the same df.
- **Current:** Per cluster: an n_g×n_g eigen() of I−H_gg for the inverse square root. Satterthwaite df from a dense n×G Umat (each column needs an O(np) X %*% ... product), then crossprod(Umat), O(nG²). R loop over clusters.
- **Claimed speedup:** estimatr 2.0.0 NEWS: about 2× end-to-end on a 1,000-observation, 100-cluster model from the df change alone (a 500×500 cluster matrix replaced by five 3×3 matrices). MacKinnon–Nielsen–Webb (arXiv 2301.04527): the n_g×n_g route is fastest only for cluster size ≤4. Neither number was measured in EDI. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Exact and deterministic, with the same answer within floating-point tolerance. The pseudo-inverse eigenvalue cutoff must match (EDI drops ≤1e-10·max; estimatr drops ≤1e-12) so that fixed-effect/singular clusters agree. Should be A/B tested against clubSandwich CR2 + Satterthwaite. estimatr's loop assumes contiguous clusters, so EDI would need to sort or index by cluster. The gain is per call: small for a single SE, multiplied by B when the SE is studentized in resampling.
- **Suggested action:** Prototype. Port the estimatr r×r Satterthwaite df (MIT, attribute) and the large-cluster k×k reduction into C++ (or vectorised R). Drop Umat. A/B test against the current function and clubSandwich::coef_test(vcov='CR2', test='Satterthwaite'), including a singular I−H_gg case. Priority rises if the CR2 SE is used inside studentized bootstrap / randomization loops.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/helper_robust_sandwich.R::cluster_robust_cr2_variance`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/estimatr/news/news.html https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DeclareDesign/estimatr/master/src/lm_robust_helper.cpp https://cran.r-project.org/package=estimatr https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/dfadjust/vignettes/dfadjust.pdf https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/2301.04527

### sandwich-variance: sandwich-estimator → Monotone pointer over the sorted event times, or survival's single backward pass (coxscore2.c), O(n+K) with identical results.
- **Current:** Binary search per subject for its last event time: O(n log K + (n+K)p + Cp²).
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified. It removes a log K factor from a non-dominant term. (applies at EDI's sizes: no; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Negligible speed gain. Best folded into the I-6 accuracy fix (reverse accumulation of r_exp), which matters more.
- **Suggested action:** Optional: do it together with the I-6 fix in compute_robust_vcov; no standalone change.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_coxph_regression.cpp::compute_robust_vcov`
- **Sources:** https://raw.githubusercontent.com/therneau/survival/master/src/coxscore2.c https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/survival/news.html

### sequential-biased-coin-designs: sequential-replay → A dedicated C++ urn draw kernel with running counts (nT, nC), per-draw seeding like generate_permutations_efron_cpp: O(rn). It never touches private$w, which also removes the side effect.
- **Current:** Urn null draws go through the generic R double loop (r × t calls to assign_wt, each recounting sum(private$w==1) over n entries): O(r n^2) R ops. The loop writes into private$w of the object it is called on and never restores it.
- **Claimed speedup:** O(r n^2) R-level ops to O(r n) compiled; from the complexity analysis in catalog §12 options table (k)/(l), not measured (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** This kernel is already scheduled (v1.0.5 TODO-10, now release_v1_2_0 TODO-22). It is re-reported because of a robustness issue found this run, not as a new speed idea. The generic loop clobbers private$w. The main randomization path is safe because it draws from des_obj$duplicate(), but inference_incidence_cmh.R:196 calls des_obj$draw_ws_according_to_design() on the live design, so on a urn design the observed assignment could be replaced by a null draw. I did not verify this by running EDI. The RNG stream changes but the distribution does not, so a distributional check is needed. As a stopgap, the generic loop could save and restore private$w.
- **Suggested action:** Keep TODO-22 (C++ urn kernel). Separately, check whether inference_incidence_cmh.R:196 and the bootstrap draw sites in inference_all_abstract_rand_bootstrap.R are reached with a live urn design, and either call them on a duplicate or have the generic draw_ws_raw restore private$w.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/design_seq_one_by_one_abstract.R::draw_ws_raw`, `R/EDI/R/design_seq_one_by_one_urn.R::assign_wt`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=randomizeR https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/randomizeR/refman/randomizeR.html https://doi.org/10.18637/jss.v085.i08

### sequential-mc-pvalue-stopping: sequential-monte-carlo-stopping → Anytime-valid sequential Monte Carlo test by betting (Fischer & Ramdas, JRSS-B 2025). Use the binomial strategy with p=1/⌈√(2πe^{1/6})/α⌉, where wealth = (T+1)·p^ℓ(1−p)^{T−ℓ}·C(T,ℓ), or the binomial mixture with c<α, where wealth = (1−Bin(ℓ;T+1,c))/c. Stop to reject when wealth ≥ 1/α and for futility when wealth < α. Keep EDI's batching (validity holds at any stopping time) and fall back to the fixed-r p-value if there is no decision by r. The rule is a deterministic function of the draws, so it keeps thread- and seed-determinism. The paper proves zero resampling risk for limiting p-values below p (binomial) or below c (mixture), which addresses the multiplicity gap the catalog records for the current band.
- **Current:** Per-look 99% Clopper–Pearson band on 2·min(tail counts), checked every max(25,⌈2√r⌉) draws (45 at r=501) after mc_min_draws=max(100, 2·batch). It stops when the band excludes mc_stop_threshold=α and truncates at r. There is no error control across looks. With 0 exceedances the band's upper limit only drops below α=0.05 at n ≥ 210, so rejection can never happen before 225 draws. It runs only inside randomization/BRT CI searches (inference_all_abstract_rand_ci.R:264, inference_all_abstract_rand_bootstrap_ci.R:168), where each draw can be a full refit (GEE etc.).
- **Claimed speedup:** Minimum draws to reject at α=0.05, from the paper's closed-form wealth (my arithmetic, not a benchmark): binomial strategy 44 / 53 / 80 draws with 0 / 1 / 2 losses; mixture with c=0.04 needs 39 / 73 / 105 (the paper itself states 39). The current band needs ≥ 210, i.e. 225 after batching. That is about 3–5x fewer refits for clearly significant δ in the CI bracketing phase. Futility stops can also come before the current 100-draw floor. Near the CI boundary (p≈α) neither rule stops early, so the end-to-end saving per CI search is not quantified. Paper Section 7.4 (Fisher sharp null, α=0.05): binomial mean 85 / median 53 permutations vs 200 for Besag–Clifford (h=10). (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: unknown; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** The fixed-r answer is unchanged, but decisions at early-stopped δ can differ slightly from the current band's, so a CI endpoint may move within MC error. An A/B is needed against fixed r on the existing CI-search tests. Robustness gain: error is bounded across looks, which the current per-look band lacks. The betting rule is asymmetric: it is a one-sided bet on the exceedance rate. It must be applied to the two-sided indicator 1[|T_b| ≥ |T_obs|], or to each tail at α/2, consistently with rand_tail_counts' tie rule. Reference code is MIT, so it is GPL-3 compatible, and the method is a few lines (closed form) with nothing to depend on.
- **Suggested action:** Under performance_profiling_and_upgrades.md TODO-156, add Fischer & Ramdas 2025 (and Gandy–Hahn–Ding 2020 buckets) to the NEW-seq-mc-stopping options table. Correct the table's objection to decision-only rules: the SMC path only decides p(δ) vs α inside CI searches. Prototype the binomial or binomial-mixture stop in sequential_mc_band_excludes_threshold. Measure draws per CI search and endpoint agreement against fixed r=501 on the GEE and other refit-per-draw classes.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_rand.R::compute_two_sided_pval_with_sequential_mc`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_rand.R::compute_two_sided_randomization_pval_band`, `R/EDI/R/inference_ext_sequential_mc_pval.R::sequential_mc_band_excludes_threshold`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_rand_bootstrap.R::compute_two_sided_brt_pval_with_sequential_mc`, `R/EDI/R/inference_all_abstract_rand_bootstrap.R::get_brt_distribution_prefix`, `R/EDI/R/inference_mixin_kk_gee_shared.R::compute_rand_two_sided_pval`
- **Sources:** https://doi.org/10.1093/jrsssb/qkaf014 https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.07365 https://arxiv.org/html/2401.07365v5 https://doi.org/10.1214/26-EJS2514 https://github.com/fischer23/MC-testing-by-betting

### simulation-study-engine: crude-monte-carlo → Boos & Zhang (2000) linear extrapolation in 1/B. Inside each replicate, compute the CI and p-value on several subsamples of the B resample statistics already drawn (e.g. b = 39…B). Then regress the coverage or rejection indicator on 1/b across replicates and report the intercept as the B=∞ coverage or power, with a cluster-robust MCSE. Implemented in simhelpers::extrapolate_coverage / extrapolate_rejection (0.3.0+, exclude_above added in 0.3.2, 2026-11-12).
- **Current:** Each replicate runs bootstrap CIs and p-values at a fixed B_boot (default 201) and randomization p-values at r_rand (default 201). The report gives the raw finite-B coverage, power and size. Their finite-B bias (power loss of order 1/B) is not corrected, so a precise answer needs a large B in every replicate.
- **Claimed speedup:** Boos & Zhang's abstract says linear extrapolation corrects the finite-B bias and so reduces computation time in Monte Carlo power studies. In Pustejovsky's worked example (R=2500), 399 bootstrap draws per replicate plus extrapolation gave coverage 0.946 (MCSE 0.004), against 0.9516 from brute force with B=2399: about 6× fewer resamples. CI computations rose about 8×, so it only pays when draws, which in EDI are refits, cost far more than computing a CI. Not quantified for EDI. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** This changes the reported summary: it targets the B=∞ coverage or power instead of the coverage or power of the B-resample procedure actually run. Per-replicate p-values and CIs are unchanged. It needs the inference classes to expose the per-replicate resample statistics, or subsample CIs/p-values, to the simulation engine, which is an API change. Subsample CIs multiply the CI-computation cost. Linear extrapolation is an approximation; the source example shows a bias of about 0.005 against brute force. It is deterministic if the subsample draws are seeded from the replicate seed. It applies only to cells with bootstrap or randomization inference types, not to asymptotic ones. It is not in the catalog §12 options table (n), which covers Nrep, not B.
- **Suggested action:** Investigate: add an opt-in 'extrapolated' summary for bootstrap and randomization cells. Record the p-values and CIs at nested sub-B values inside each replicate, and validate against a brute-force large-B run on a few cells. Use simhelpers::extrapolate_* (GPL-3) as the reference implementation.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/simulations_framework.R::init_result_key_store_cpp`, `R/EDI/src/result_key_store.cpp::check_in_result_key_store_cpp`
- **Sources:** https://doi.org/10.2307/2669393 https://jepusto.com/posts/Simulating-bootstrap-CIs/ https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/simhelpers/news/news.html https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/simhelpers/refman/simhelpers.html
- **No open copy found** for doi:10.2307/2669393 (publisher page blocks automated access)

### subsampling-m-out-of-n: m-out-of-n-subsampling → Lam's cheap bootstrap: a t-type interval from very few resamples (as few as one), extended to subsampling variants
- **Current:** B=501 refits on subsamples of size b=n^0.7 (or m), then a centered pivot with a basic CI
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified in the abstract. Replacing about 500 refits with a handful would save roughly the ratio B/(few). (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** A different CI construction (estimator change). It relies on asymptotic normality of the estimator, which is exactly what m-out-of-n and subsampling are used to avoid, so validity for non-regular estimators is doubtful. It would be an opt-in 'cheap' CI type only.
- **Suggested action:** Carry forward: record in the catalog as an estimator-change option (an opt-in cheap CI for expensive refits). No change to existing algorithms.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_ext_prw_subsampling.R::approximate_subsampling_distribution_beta_hat_T_impl`, `R/EDI/R/inference_ext_prw_subsampling.R::subsampling_centered_pivot`, `R/EDI/R/inference_ext_m_out_of_n_bootstrap.R::m_out_of_n_bootstrap_centered_pivot`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.00090 https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2202.00090

### survival-frailty-copula-mle: gauss-hermite-quadrature → Cluster-total (sufficient-statistic) collapse. log T = Xβ + u + σε·ε with extreme-value ε is a PH model with frailty exp(-u/σε), so for cluster g at node k the conditional log-lik is const_g + Σ_i δ_i base_wi + D_g δ_k − H_g e^{δ_k}, with D_g=Σδ_i and H_g=Σ_i exp(base_wi), both computed once per evaluation in O(n). Each (g,k) term is then O(1). The gradient needs only per-cluster posterior moments E_g[e^δ], E_g[δ e^δ] and E_g[δ], giving per-observation scalars s_i = (exp(base_wi)·E_g[e^δ] − δ_i)/σε and one Xᵀs GEMV. Total O(np + GK), and the n×K r/w matrices disappear. This is the reduction Charvat (2023) uses to write the cluster integrand for a normal-intercept shared frailty. It is an algebraic identity, so the estimator is unchanged.
- **Current:** Fixed 20-node non-adaptive GH. Per node k it loops over every observation, computing exp(w_ik)=exp(base_i)·exp(δ_k) and storing r_ik and w_ik in n×K matrices. The gradient then updates the p-vector inside the k,g,i loops: O(nK) log-lik plus O(nKp) gradient per evaluation.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by any source. My operation count: node work drops from O(nK) to O(GK), about 2× at EDI's pairs, and the gradient drops from O(nKp) to O(np), about K=20× on that term. This subsumes catalog §5 TODO-4 (O(nK+np)). Theoretical, not measured. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Same estimator, within floating-point rounding (summation order changes), not bitwise. Deterministic. Overflow handling changes: the current per-observation exp fallback becomes a per-cluster log(H_g)+δ_k form, which is actually safer in log space. The catalog notes that an earlier 'math tightening' of this kernel benchmarked slower, so a perf A/B is needed. No new dependency.
- **Suggested action:** Widen catalog §5 TODO-4 to the full cluster-total collapse (O(np+GK)), not just the GEMV hoist. Benchmark it against the current kernel at n=50–500 pairs, p=2–10, and check that log-lik and gradient agree to 1e-10 relative.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_weibull_frailty.cpp::WeibullFrailtyNormalLikelihood`, `R/EDI/R/helper_survival_fits.R::.fit_weibull_frailty_rcpp`, `R/EDI/R/inference_survival_GLMM_weibull_frailty_normal.R::frailty_for_matched_pairs`, `R/EDI/R/inference_survival_GLMM_weibull_frailty_normal.R::shared`
- **Sources:** https://ideas.repec.org/a/taf/amstat/v77y2023i1p41-50.html https://doi.org/10.1080/00031305.2022.2110939
- **No open copy found** for doi:10.1080/00031305.2022.2110939 (publisher page blocks automated access)

### survival-frailty-copula-mle: gauss-hermite-quadrature → Lambert-based adaptive GH (LAGHQ, Charvat, The American Statistician 77(1):41–50, 2023). For a shared frailty model with a normal random intercept, the cluster mode is closed form: b̂_g = D_gτ² − W0(H_gτ² e^{D_gτ²}), using the collapsed statistics above. The curvature is H_g e^{b̂}+1/τ², and the paper gives closed-form gradient and Hessian of the approximate likelihood. This removes both objections the catalog recorded against AGQ for this kernel (no nested optimization, and an exact gradient without implicit differentiation). Fewer nodes (e.g. 5–9) then suffice. Charvat's GPL(>=2) package mexhaz imports lamW and is the likely reference implementation. Boost.Math lambert_w0 (BH, BSL-1.0) supplies W0 in C++.
- **Current:** Fixed 20-node non-adaptive GH. Adaptive GH (TODO-153) was costed in the catalog at only ~1.2–1.6×, because each cluster needs a Newton mode solve on every evaluation and the exact gradient must go through û(θ).
- **Claimed speedup:** The source gives no timings; it claims maximisation is 'convenient and efficient'. Theoretical: one W0 per cluster instead of 3–5 Newton steps, and K≈5–9 instead of 20 nodes, so roughly 2–4× per evaluation on top of the collapse. Not measured. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: no; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Estimator change at quadrature tolerance: AGQ approximates the same marginal-likelihood MLE with a different, generally more accurate rule. Arxiv 2202.07864 shows non-adaptive rules are inconsistent under weak concentration conditions, so accuracy improves. Results will not match the current 20-node GH bitwise, so an A/B against a high-K reference is needed. W0 accuracy near 0 and the branch point is fine here, because the argument is ≥0. Deterministic. Applies only to the Weibull/PH normal frailty, not the generic GLMM engine (other links need a numeric mode solve; see the Stringer route).
- **Suggested action:** Add Charvat 2023 LAGHQ to v1.0.5 TODO-153 (performance_profiling_and_upgrades.md) as the AGQ route for fast_weibull_frailty.cpp, and name the file in its scope. Prototype it after the collapse finding, check mexhaz for the formulas, then A/B against K=50 non-adaptive GH for σu up to e^1.5.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_weibull_frailty.cpp::WeibullFrailtyNormalLikelihood`, `R/EDI/src/fast_weibull_frailty.cpp::gauss_hermite_rule_wf`, `R/EDI/src/fast_weibull_frailty.cpp::fast_weibull_frailty_internal`, `R/EDI/R/helper_survival_fits.R::.fit_weibull_frailty_rcpp`, `R/EDI/R/inference_survival_GLMM_weibull_frailty_normal.R::shared`
- **Sources:** https://ideas.repec.org/a/taf/amstat/v77y2023i1p41-50.html https://doi.org/10.1080/00031305.2022.2110939 https://cran.r-project.org/package=mexhaz https://cran.r-project.org/package=lamW https://cran.r-project.org/package=BH https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.01589 https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.07864
- **No open copy found** for doi:10.1080/00031305.2022.2110939 (publisher page blocks automated access)

### weibull-aft-mle: multistart → Make the first attempt with init omitted (NULL) so survreg uses the distribution's documented init(x, weights) mean/variance start. Before any jitter, add scale-aware deterministic starts: intercept = mean log-time and log-scale from the residual SD (the weibull_smart_cold_start pattern applied to lognormal/loglogistic). Keep the seeded jitter only as a last resort, scaled by SE or |start|. Re-reported from 2026-11-16; the code is unchanged.
- **Current:** Non-Weibull distributions, and the Weibull fallback when the C++ warm start fails, start survreg from init = rep(0, p+1). They then retry up to 50 times with init + rnorm(p+1) jitter (unscaled, seeded).
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified by any source. The expected gain is fewer failed survreg calls per fit: a zero intercept is far from log(time) at typical time scales. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: unknown; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** Converged MLEs should match, but which rare fits fail may change, so an A/B on convergence-failure rate is needed. This is partly a robustness improvement.
- **Suggested action:** Fold into multistart_nonconcave_likelihoods.md as the concrete 'deterministic data-driven starts' prototype: init=NULL first, then a moment start, then scaled jitter. A/B the failure rate and the attempts per fit.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/helper_robust_regression.R::robust_survreg_with_surv_object`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/survival/refman/survival.html

### weibull-aft-mle: newton-raphson → For the unclustered case, call survival::survreg.fit(x = cbind(1, X), y = Surv(...), weights, offset = 0, init = warm, controlvals = survreg.control(), dist = survreg.distributions$weibull, ...) on the prebuilt matrix, falling back to the formula path on error. This is the same Newton–Raphson, likelihood and start, and mirrors the existing coxph.fit interim path. The end state remains the native weighted C++ kernel (bayesian_bootstrap_performance.md item 2).
- **Current:** Each Bayesian-bootstrap draw calls survival::survreg through the formula interface, building a model.frame every draw, with a warm init.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified; no source benchmarks survreg.fit against survreg. The gain is removing the per-draw formula and model-frame overhead, multiplied by B. (applies at EDI's sizes: yes; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** New this run: the survival manual lists survreg.fit under 'Internal survival functions' and warns its arguments 'are not guaranteed to stay the same from release to release'. It needs a tryCatch fallback, a test pinned to the survival version, or a direct move to the native C++ kernel. The robust cluster sandwich still needs the formula path, or a hand-built sandwich.
- **Suggested action:** Add as an interim sub-item beside the coxph.fit interim in bayesian_bootstrap_performance.md item 2, guarded by a fallback. Benchmark per-draw time first; prefer going straight to the native C++ weighted kernel if that is near.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/globals.R::weighted_weibull_bootstrap_surrogate_fit`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/survival/refman/survival.html https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/survival/news.html

### zero-inflated-count-mle: laplace-approximation-tmb → Pass se = !estimate_only, and optionally glmmTMBControl(conv_check='skip', eigval_check=FALSE), on estimate-only replicates. Keep parallel=1 inside an already-parallel replicate loop.
