- **Current:** One glmmTMB::glmmTMB() fit per Bayesian-bootstrap replicate, called with no se argument (default se=TRUE), so sdreport runs even when estimate_only=TRUE.
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified. The glmmTMB manual documents se as 'whether to return standard errors' with no timing. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: medium)
- **Trade-offs:** None for the point estimate. Skipping the checks loses the convergence diagnostics on those replicates.
- **Suggested action:** Re-reported from 2026-11-16, still unfixed at 8b718ae. Add se = !estimate_only at both weighted glmmTMB call sites and time one B=200 run before and after.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_count_zero_augmented_poisson_abstract.R::fit_zero_augmented_model`, `R/EDI/R/inference_count_zero_augmented_poisson_abstract.R::compute_estimate_with_bootstrap_weights`, `R/EDI/R/inference_count_hurdle.R::compute_estimate_with_bootstrap_weights`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/package=glmmTMB https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/glmmTMB/refman/glmmTMB.html

### zero-inflated-count-mle: laplace-approximation-tmb → Weighted direct C++ fits (catalog §10 TODO-3). fastglm 0.1.4 already ships weighted C++ fitters: fastglm_hurdle (logistic plus truncated Poisson/NB, IRLS with Brent θ) and fastglm_zi (SQUAREM-EM, IRLS M-steps). Use them as a reference and test oracle for adding weights to EDI's own fast_hurdle_negbin / fast_zinb / fast_zero_augmented_poisson kernels, or as an interim back-end.
- **Current:** glmmTMB (TMB tape build plus nlminb) per weighted replicate, about 10–100 ms build each, for fixed-effects-only ZIP/ZINB/hurdle models.
- **Claimed speedup:** From the fastglm CRAN benchmarks vignette (package author's machine), at p=2: ZIP n=1000 median 2.47 ms, ZINB n=1e4 78.6 ms, hurdle-NB n=1e4 32.1 ms. There is no direct comparison with glmmTMB. In the R Journal glmmTMB paper (NB GLMM), mgcv took 0.03× and gamlss 0.24× of glmmTMB's time. The speedup over glmmTMB at EDI sizes is therefore not quantified. (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** The weighted MLE is the same estimator. fastglm_zi is EM-based and only matches pscl to about 1e-5, so an EM back-end stopped at a tolerance may differ slightly from glmmTMB. Determinism has to be checked. Adds a dependency (GPL≥2, compatible) unless used only as a test oracle; porting weights into EDI's kernels avoids that.
- **Suggested action:** In catalog §10 TODO-3, record fastglm_hurdle/fastglm_zi (weights=) as the reference for weighted kernels. Prototype weights in EDI's C++ kernels and benchmark against glmmTMB per replicate at n=50–2000.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/R/inference_count_zero_augmented_poisson_abstract.R::fit_zero_augmented_model`, `R/EDI/R/inference_count_zero_augmented_poisson_abstract.R::compute_estimate_with_bootstrap_weights`, `R/EDI/R/inference_count_hurdle.R::compute_estimate_with_bootstrap_weights`
- **Sources:** https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/fastglm/vignettes/benchmarks-fastglm.html https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/fastglm/refman/fastglm.html https://cran.r-project.org/package=fastglm https://journal.r-project.org/articles/RJ-2017-066/ https://doi.org/10.32614/RJ-2017-066

### zero-inflated-count-mle: truncated-series-summation → A scalar tail-probability series with an adaptive cutoff and a rank-1 update per observation, or a trigamma-free evaluation of the trigamma expectations (Yu, Dousti Mousavi & Yang).
- **Current:** Brute-force per-observation series over y=1..max(10000, μ+12sd), building a functor and a p×p Hessian for each y (dead code).
- **Claimed speedup:** Not quantified. The arXiv abstract says 'highly efficient and much more accurate than previous methods'. Analytically, each term drops from O(p²) plus allocations to O(1). (applies at EDI's sizes: unknown; keeps the estimator: yes; confidence: low)
- **Trade-offs:** Only matters if the expected information is ever wired in. Otherwise delete the dead code.
- **Suggested action:** Delete the dead expected_hessian, or rewrite it with a tail-series or trigamma-free method if it is ever needed.
- **Affects:** `R/EDI/src/fast_hurdle_negbin.cpp::expected_hessian`
- **Sources:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.04798 https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2306.04798

## 6. Taxonomy terms proposed by agents
- adjacent-cat-cr-logit-mle: problem `adjacent-cat-cr-logit-mle` synonyms: "Poisson trick / Poisson log-linear equivalence for adjacent-categories logit" (brglm2::bracl and Kosmidis 2023 fit AC logit as a Poisson log-linear model on grouped counts; this led to the compression finding)
- adjacent-cat-cr-logit-mle: problem `adjacent-cat-cr-logit-mle` search_terms: "grouped data / covariate-pattern frequency weights ordinal logit" (The sufficient-statistic compression of treatment-only designs is found under 'grouped data', not under optimizer names)
- adjacent-cat-cr-logit-mle: problem `adjacent-cat-cr-logit-mle` software: "brglm2::bracl" (Rival AC-logit implementation (GPL-3) with bias reduction)
- adjacent-cat-cr-logit-mle: problem `adjacent-cat-cr-logit-mle` software: "VGAM acat/cratio/sratio" (Reference AC/CR implementations (Fisher scoring))
- adjacent-cat-cr-logit-mle: problem `adjacent-cat-cr-logit-mle` software: "ordinalNet" (Fits acat/cratio/sratio families; penalized coordinate descent reference)
- adjacent-cat-cr-logit-mle: method `newton-raphson` software: "rms::lrm.fit / rms::orm.fit" (NR-with-step-halving ordinal fitter with a published optimizer benchmark (Harrell))
- atkinson-optimal-biased-coin: method `atkinson-da-biased-coin` software: "covadap" (CRAN ClinicalTrials task view lists it as a covariate-adaptive randomization implementation)
- atkinson-optimal-biased-coin: method `atkinson-da-biased-coin` synonyms: "DoptBCD" (carat's function name for the design; it surfaces implementations in searches)
- atkinson-optimal-biased-coin: method `atkinson-da-biased-coin` narrower: "block inverse / Schur complement with w-independent C_t" (the catalog's adopted speedup for null draws; useful search term)
- atkinson-optimal-biased-coin: method `atkinson-da-biased-coin` narrower: "rank-Greville recursive least squares (rank-deficient)" (could replace per-t column selection plus refactorization in the precompute)
- atkinson-optimal-biased-coin: problem `atkinson-optimal-biased-coin` search_terms: "sequential D-optimal design Sherman-Morrison greedy" (the broader literature's term for the same per-arrival kernel)
- balanced-allocation-optimization: problem `balanced-allocation-optimization` broader: "k-cluster / densest k-subgraph (cardinality-constrained BQP)" (The exact-solver literature for cardinality-constrained binary quadratics is published under these names)
- balanced-allocation-optimization: method `simulated-annealing` software: "tabuSearch (CRAN), NMOF threshold accepting" (The R metaheuristics for binary strings listed in the Optimization task view)
- balanced-allocation-optimization: method `branch-and-bound-milp` narrower: "FLSSS fixed-size subset sum" (The p = 1 reduction is a fixed-size subset-sum search)
- balanced-allocation-optimization: problem `balanced-allocation-optimization` search_terms: "Gram-Schmidt walk design" (The main randomized vector-balancing alternative; a design change to rule out)
- bayesian-bootstrap: method `bayesian-bootstrap` synonyms: "fractional random weight bootstrap" (Name used by Xu et al. 2020 and the fwb package for the same Dirichlet-weight bootstrap)
- bayesian-bootstrap: method `bayesian-bootstrap` synonyms: "perturbation bootstrap" (Synonym used across the random-weight bootstrap literature)
- bayesian-bootstrap: method `bayesian-bootstrap` software: "fwb" (Main active R implementation (1.0.0, Oct 2026), with seed-only deterministic weights and fast vcovFWB)
- bayesian-bootstrap: method `bayesian-bootstrap` narrower: "generative bootstrap sampler / amortized weighted M-estimation" (The current line of refit-avoiding approximations (GMS/GBS, Ribbon) to watch)
- beta-regression-mle: method `likelihood-factorization` software: "gamlss.dist BEINF" (Its ZOIB parameterization (log p0/p2, log p1/p2) is the same multinomial logit as EDI's C++, so it is a reference for the BB/PB parameterization mismatch)
- beta-regression-mle: problem `beta-regression-mle` software: "BetaRegression.jl" (A pure Fisher-scoring beta-regression implementation (MIT) to compare against L-BFGS)
- beta-regression-mle: problem `beta-regression-mle` search_terms: "ordered beta regression" (The main rival model for [0,1] responses (glmmTMB ordbeta, ordbetareg))
- beta-regression-mle: method `lbfgs` narrower: "Fisher-scoring polish after quasi-Newton" (The betareg recipe; this is the actual alternative to EDI's L-BFGS)
- binary-glm-mle: method `lbfgs` software: "RcppNumerical fastLR" (The main R L-BFGS logistic-regression rival; it underlies fastLogisticRegressionWrap)
- binary-glm-mle: problem `binary-glm-mle` software: "logistf" (Reference Firth logistic implementation, for the uncalled penalized evaluator)
- binary-glm-mle: problem `binary-glm-mle` software: "brglm2" (Adjusted-score/Firth IWLS for binomial GLMs, the same authors as detectseparation)
- binary-glm-mle: method `irls` narrower: "adjusted score IWLS (bias-reduced IWLS)" (How rival Firth fitters solve the penalized problem (arXiv 2307.07342))
- binary-glm-mle: problem `binary-glm-mle` software: "maxLik" (fracreg fits the fractional QMLE through maxLik)
- blocking-partition: method `branch-and-bound-milp` narrower: "subset (bitmask) dynamic programming with lowest-element anchoring" (An exact solver-free alternative for n ≤ ~22 that removes label symmetry; this is where the strongest finding came from)
- blocking-partition: method `branch-and-bound-milp` narrower: "minimum-weight perfect non-bipartite matching (block size 2)" (Exact polynomial special case when n = 2B)
- blocking-partition: method `branch-and-bound-milp` software: "designmatch" (A CRAN blocking/matching package that now defaults to the HiGHS solver)
- blocking-partition: method `branch-and-bound-milp` software: "nbpMatching" (Exact solver for the pair-block special case, already an EDI import)
- blocking-partition: problem `blocking-partition` search_terms: "approximation algorithm for blocking (Karmakar)" (The 2022 JRSSB paper establishes NP-hardness for blocks > 2 and gives a bottleneck-objective approximation)
- blocking-partition: method `balanced-clustering` narrower: "balanced k-means optimal transport (BalLOT)" (Newer balanced k-means heuristic (arXiv 2512.05926))
- blocking-partition: method `balanced-clustering` software: "fastcluster" (Drop-in replacement for the Ward holdout's hclust)
- bootstrap-confidence-interval: method `double-bootstrap` narrower: "sequential iterated bootstrap (SPRT inner stopping, Lee-Young 1996)" (Lowers the inner-level cost of the iterated bootstrap)
- bootstrap-confidence-interval: method `double-bootstrap` narrower: "asymptotic iterated bootstrap (analytic coverage correction, Lee-Young 1995)" (Replaces the second level with analytic calculations)
- bootstrap-confidence-interval: method `double-bootstrap` narrower: "second-level interpolation (Hall-Lee-Young 2000)" (Allows smaller inner B at the same simulation error)
- bootstrap-confidence-interval: method `double-bootstrap` narrower: "reuse of inner distributions across calibration grid (common random numbers)" (The basis of the finding)
- bootstrap-confidence-interval: problem `bootstrap-confidence-interval` broader: "cheap bootstrap" (Low-B alternative, recurring in problem-level searches (an estimator change))
- bootstrap-index-generation: method `iid-index-resampling` narrower: "batched ranged random integer generation" (Brackett-Rozinsky & Lemire 2025 (SPE) is the only newer bounded-draw technique)
- bootstrap-index-generation: method `iid-index-resampling` software: "Randompack" (2026 MIT cross-platform reproducible RNG library with an R interface and bounded-integer benchmarks; a determinism reference)
- bootstrap-index-generation: method `stratified-resampling` narrower: "bitset rejection sampling without replacement" (dqrng's rate-dependent method; the main no-stream-change option for sample_group_units)
- bootstrap-index-generation: method `cluster-block-resampling` software: "boottest" (Reference fast wild cluster bootstrap (Stata/Julia) cited for cluster-bootstrap speed claims)
- bootstrap-index-generation: method `stratified-resampling` software: "resample (samp.bootstrap)" (CRAN index-matrix generator, a BSD reference)
- bootstrap-randomization-test: method `brt-monte-carlo` narrower: "sequential Monte Carlo testing by betting" (Fischer & Ramdas 2025, an anytime-valid early stop that also stops for rejection)

- bootstrap-randomization-test: method `kaplan-meier-product-limit` software: "FastSurvival" (C++ fast KM/log-rank/RMST on CRAN, 2.0.0)
- bootstrap-randomization-test: method `brt-monte-carlo` narrower: "anytime-valid Monte Carlo p-value" (Stoepker & Castro 2409.18908 and Fischer & Ramdas use this term)
- clmm-ordinal-mixed-fit: problem `clmm-ordinal-mixed-fit` software: "rms::orm (cluster random intercept, AGQ)" (rms 8.2-0 (2026-10-22) added random-intercept ordinal models with AGQ and Louis-identity information; it is a new rival implementation)
- clmm-ordinal-mixed-fit: problem `clmm-ordinal-mixed-fit` software: "mixor" (Fortran MIXOR wrapper (AGQ + Fisher scoring) for ordinal mixed models, listed in the MixedModels task view)

- clmm-ordinal-mixed-fit: method `gauss-hermite-quadrature` narrower: "cluster-weighted vs observation-weighted quadrature likelihood (case weights)" (clmm applies case weights inside the integrand; Bayesian bootstrap of clusters needs weights on each cluster's log-integral)
- clmm-ordinal-mixed-fit: method `gauss-hermite-quadrature` narrower: "clmm nAGQ negative (non-adaptive GHQ)" (clmm's switch to standard GHQ, which matches EDI's native rule)
- complete-randomization-draws: method `fisher-yates-shuffle` narrower: "batched ranged random integers" (Brackett-Rozinsky & Lemire's term; the main constant-factor shuffle speedup)

- grouping-and-level-coding: problem `grouping-and-level-coding` search_terms: "hash-based vs sort-based group-by aggregate" (Phrase used by the main empirical study (arXiv 2411.13245))

- interval-censored-npmle: problem `interval-censored-npmle` search_terms: "left-truncated interval-censored NPMLE algorithm" (Recent algorithm work (Waller et al.) is framed this way)

- jackknife-replicates: method `jackknife` synonyms: "CV3 / CRV3 jackknife cluster-robust variance" (Econometrics name for the delete-one-cluster jackknife variance; the fast exact formula is published under it.)
- jackknife-replicates: method `jackknife` narrower: "V-fold jackknife" (2026 name for the grouped (delete-a-group) jackknife (arXiv 2607.22493); fills the catalog's [cite needed].)

- random-number-generation: problem `random-number-generation` search_terms: "ziggurat normal generator" (The main rival to AS241 inversion for N(0,1), and the subject of a JSS comment)
- randomization-null-distribution: method `monte-carlo-randomization` narrower: "anytime-valid Monte Carlo p-value (testing by betting)" (The main rival stopping rule (Fischer & Ramdas) is published under this name)
- randomization-null-distribution: method `monte-carlo-randomization` narrower: "Phipson-Smyth (b+1)/(r+1) correction" (The p-value formula convention matters for validity; EDI uses b/r)
- randomization-null-distribution: problem `randomization-null-distribution` search_terms: "cheap permutation testing" (A recent line of work (arXiv 2502.07672) on reducing permutation cost)
- randomization-null-distribution: method `monte-carlo-randomization` software: "hamstest" (New 2026 Python package for small permutation p-values, with C++ adapters)
- randomization-null-distribution: method `monte-carlo-randomization` software: "statmod::permp" (Reference implementation of exact Monte Carlo permutation p-values)
- rerandomization-sampling: method `rejection-sampling` narrower: "mirror allocations (complement w -> 1-w for symmetric criteria)" (The review 2512.05290 §7.4 cites it as halving draws; it needs a recorded rejection reason (not i.i.d.; T(1-w) = -T(w)))
- rerandomization-sampling: method `rejection-sampling` narrower: "Langevin-gradient rerandomization (LGR)" (New Apr 2026 sampler, arXiv 2604.07524)
- rerandomization-sampling: problem `rerandomization-sampling` software: "cvcrand" (R Journal package doing best-fraction constrained randomization)
- rerandomization-sampling: method `selection-algorithm` software: "miniselect" (BSL-1.0 C++ Floyd-Rivest/selection library)
- resampling-refit-loop: problem `resampling-refit-loop` search_terms: "k-step bootstrap" (The Davidson–MacKinnon / Andrews name for the one-step bootstrap family; it surfaces the main literature.)
- resampling-refit-loop: method `bayesian-bootstrap` synonyms: "fractional random weight bootstrap" (fwb's name for the same Dirichlet-weight refit bootstrap)
- resampling-refit-loop: method `bayesian-bootstrap` software: "fwb" (CRAN package that refits models with warm starts, release 2026-10-14)
- resampling-refit-loop: method `newton-raphson` software: "FastSurvival::coxph_fast" (Exact Newton Cox reference since 2.0.0 (2026-11-16))
- resampling-refit-loop: method `parallel-refit-loop` software: "FRB" (Fast robust bootstrap package, a recurring alternative)
- resampling-refit-loop: method `barrodale-roberts-simplex` narrower: "Portnoy-Koenker preprocessing for bootstrap" (Exact speedup for QR bootstrap refits (Chernozhukov et al.))
- ridit-mann-whitney: method `delong-structural-components` narrower: "unbiased Mann-Whitney variance estimator (Brunner-Konietschke / Bamber)" (A tie-valid, unbiased alternative to the DeLong placement variance at the same cost; found under 'Brunner-Munzel variance' and not under DeLong terms.)
- ridit-mann-whitney: method `delong-structural-components` synonyms: "Brunner-Munzel variance" (The same placement-based variance of the relative effect appears in the Brunner-Munzel literature.)
- ridit-mann-whitney: problem `ridit-mann-whitney` search_terms: "relative effect / stochastic superiority" (The Brunner/Konietschke (rankFD) and Brunner-Munzel literature names the estimand this way.)
- ridit-mann-whitney: problem `ridit-mann-whitney` software: "rankFD" (CRAN package by the authors of the unbiased variance estimator; a reference implementation of relative-effect inference.)
- ridit-mann-whitney: method `ridit-scoring` software: "ridittools" (Rival ridit SE implementation (archived on CRAN 2025-01-26).)
- risk-difference-ci: method `miettinen-nurminen-score` software: "lrstat::mnRiskDiffCI / remlRiskDiff" (C++ reference with closed-form cubic + Brent outer root, GPL>=2)
- risk-difference-ci: method `miettinen-nurminen-score` software: "cicalc::ci_prop_diff_mn" (R implementation with trig cubic; Apache-2.0)
- risk-difference-ci: method `miettinen-nurminen-score` synonyms: "restricted maximum likelihood (REML) estimates with specified risk difference" (lrstat's naming for the constrained MLE)
- risk-difference-ci: method `bisection` narrower: "ITP (interpolate-truncate-project) method" (bracketing method with bisection worst case and superlinear speed)
- risk-difference-ci: method `newcombe-hybrid-score` synonyms: "MOVER-NW" (ratesci name for paired Newcombe with corrected phi)
- risk-difference-ci: problem `risk-difference-ci` search_terms: "Elliptically Optimal confidence interval" (2026 closed-form competitor interval (estimator change))
- robust-m-regression: method `irls` narrower: "finite-termination Newton for Huber (Madsen–Nielsen)" (Exact-convergence alternative to IRLS for Huber M-estimation)
- robust-m-regression: method `irls` software: "statsmodels RLMDetS / RLMDetSMM" (BSD-licensed deterministic S/MM reference implementation)
- robust-m-regression: problem `robust-m-regression` software: "pyinit" (Deterministic Peña–Yohai S-estimator start (GPL))
- robust-m-regression: problem `robust-m-regression` software: "robustreg" (Rcpp IRLS Huber/bisquare M-regression: a rival implementation)
- sandwich-variance: method `sandwich-estimator` narrower: "Satterthwaite df via r×r Gram products (no J×J matrix)" (estimatr 2.0.0 optimisation directly applicable to cluster_robust_cr2_variance)
- sandwich-variance: method `sandwich-estimator` software: "dfadjust" (MIT package with the p×p Lemma-2 CR2 route and BM df)
- sandwich-variance: method `sandwich-estimator` software: "coxdev" (GPL≥3 header-only C++ Cox score/deviance, relevant to Lin–Wei score residuals)
- sandwich-variance: method `sandwich-estimator` narrower: "CV3 / cluster jackknife (vcovJK)" (a frequently suggested alternative; an estimator change, worth tagging)
- sequential-biased-coin-designs: method `efron-biased-coin` narrower: "Hollander-Peña recursion (exact randomization distribution)" (Exact alternative to Monte Carlo null draws for linear rank statistics under BCD; relevant to exact-enumeration TODO-6)
- sequential-biased-coin-designs: method `urn-design` narrower: "Friedman urn (non-exchangeable)" (Explains why no Pólya/beta-binomial shortcut exists for Wei's UD)
- sequential-biased-coin-designs: problem `sequential-biased-coin-designs` software: "RARfreq" (CRAN rival for DBCD/SEU urn sequence generation)
- sequential-biased-coin-designs: problem `sequential-biased-coin-designs` search_terms: "biased-coin minimization power evaluation" (Surfaced 2026 SIGA paper (arXiv 2608.05527) on avoiding nested rerandomization)
- sequential-mc-pvalue-stopping: method `sequential-monte-carlo-stopping` synonyms: "Monte Carlo testing by betting" (Title term of Fischer & Ramdas JRSS-B 2025, the strongest candidate)
- sequential-mc-pvalue-stopping: method `sequential-monte-carlo-stopping` narrower: "binomial mixture betting strategy" (The concrete stopping rule with closed-form wealth)
- sequential-mc-pvalue-stopping: method `sequential-monte-carlo-stopping` narrower: "p-value buckets" (Gandy-Hahn-Ding method in simctest::mctest)
- sequential-mc-pvalue-stopping: method `sequential-monte-carlo-stopping` software: "MChtest" (CRAN truncated-SPRT / Besag-Clifford implementation (Fay))
- sequential-mc-pvalue-stopping: method `sequential-monte-carlo-stopping` software: "rfvimptest" (CRAN sequential permutation testing (SPRT/SAPT))
- sequential-mc-pvalue-stopping: problem `sequential-mc-pvalue-stopping` search_terms: "risk of overestimated significance" (Stoepker & Castro's criterion for sequential MC p-values)
- sequential-mc-pvalue-stopping: method `sequential-monte-carlo-stopping` software: "MC-testing-by-betting" (MIT reference R code for Fischer & Ramdas)
- simulation-study-engine: method `crude-monte-carlo` narrower: "Boos-Zhang extrapolation of bootstrap power/coverage in 1/B" (Cuts per-replicate resample counts in simulation studies; it is the basis of this run's finding)
- simulation-study-engine: method `crude-monte-carlo` narrower: "sequential stopping rules for Monte Carlo" (Name used by the Wu & Kawai 2025/2026 review for precision-targeted Nrep)
- simulation-study-engine: problem `simulation-study-engine` software: "simhelpers" (GPL-3 rival that implements MCSE and Boos-Zhang extrapolation)
- simulation-study-engine: problem `simulation-study-engine` software: "SimDesign" (Main R simulation-study framework (GPL>=2))
- simulation-study-engine: problem `simulation-study-engine` software: "rsimsum" (MCSE summaries for simulation studies)
- simulation-study-engine: problem `simulation-study-engine` software: "SimEngine" (R Journal 2025 simulation framework with per-replicate seeding)
- simulation-study-engine: problem `simulation-study-engine` search_terms: "number of replications Monte Carlo study" (Phrase the replication-count literature uses (Brooks et al., Mundfrom et al.))
- stacked-design-construction: method `ordinal-binary-expansion` synonyms: "sequential logit / stopping ratio model" (Used by Stata seqlogit/crreg, VGAM sratio and ordinalNet for the same CR family.)
- stacked-design-construction: method `ordinal-binary-expansion` narrower: "stage-case format (person-stage data)" (The name Bauldry et al. use for the CR expansion EDI performs.)
- stacked-design-construction: method `ordinal-binary-expansion` software: "GLMcat" (JSS 2025 Rcpp/Eigen package for cumulative, adjacent and sequential models under one Fisher-scoring framework.)
- stacked-design-construction: method `ordinal-binary-expansion` software: "brms cratio / Stata crreg" (Direct-ML CR fits without data reshaping, the TODO-3 direction.)
- stacked-design-construction: method `ordinal-binary-expansion` narrower: "amalgamated conditional logistic regression" (Mukherjee et al. stacked binary-collapsing conditional likelihood with sandwich SE, the analog of EDI's adjacent-category clogit stacking.)

- stereotype-logit-mle: problem `stereotype-logit-mle` software: "gnm (Mult term, eliminate)" (Fits the stereotype model as a Poisson GNM; documents its random-start behaviour)
- stereotype-logit-mle: method `multistart` narrower: "METOD early-terminated multistart" (Most recent multistart early-termination method found)
- stereotype-logit-mle: problem `stereotype-logit-mle` search_terms: "ordered stereotype model (OSM)" (Name used in the clustord and Fernandez/Spiess literature)

- stratified-rd-variance: method `robins-breslow-greenland-variance` narrower: "Greenland-Robins (GR) / modified GR (mGR) variance" (names used by Qiu et al. 2408.12541 and RobinCar)

- stratified-rd-variance: problem `stratified-rd-variance` search_terms: "sparse-stratum asymptotics risk difference" (key framing in the recent MH-RD variance literature)

- subsampling-m-out-of-n: method `m-out-of-n-subsampling` software: "dqrng" (fast without-replacement sampling reference)

<details><summary>Methods reviewed with no acceptable faster option (190)</summary>

- adjacent-cat-cr-logit-mle: `log-sum-exp`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial, O(K) per call. The max-shifted form is the accurate standard (Blanchard, Higham & Higham, IMA JNA). Nothing faster at equal accuracy.
- atkinson-optimal-biased-coin: `atkinson-da-biased-coin`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Rechecked the previous result (2026-11-16, run 43903509109). There are no code commits since then, and the code still rebuilds X'X and runs FullPivLU per (b,t) (generate_permutations.cpp:80-119, atkinson_assign.cpp:24-63). The fastest acceptable method for the hot path, the null draws (draw_ws_raw / generate_permutations_atkinson_internal), is still the block-inverse / Schur-complement rewrite. The catalog already lists it as Adopt (P6, v1.0.5 TODO-5/TODO-3): precompute C_t^-1 once per t, then O(p^2) per (b,t). It is not implemented yet. The new searches found nothing faster than that. They covered the problem itself, synonyms (Atkinson biased coin, DoptBCD), the broader family (sequential D-optimal design, covariate-adaptive randomization, RLS), the narrower variants (Sherman-Morrison, Cholesky up/downdate via Eigen LLT::rankUpdate, rank-revealing QR updating for the per-t column selection, rank-Greville RLS for rank-deficient designs) and the software (carat 2.3.0, covadap, randomizeR, CRAN ClinicalTrials task view). Sequential D-optimal work uses the same Sherman-Morrison/greedy kernel, which the catalog has already considered and rejected for drift and column-set changes. Rank-Greville (doi 10.1016/j.amc.2021.125996) could in principle avoid explicit column selection, but it would only speed up the once-per-t precompute, which is not the hot path after the Schur rewrite, and no licensed code is published. carat's DoptBCD uses the same closed-form probability and gives no performance claims. The 2026 arXiv covariate-adaptive papers (2611.08378, 2608.13442) are about different designs or theory and say nothing on computation. The per-arrival assign_wt is O(tp^2+p^3) once per arrival and not hot; the LDLT micro is already catalog P5/TODO 6.
- balanced-allocation-optimization: `dinkelbach`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). No 2025–26 binary fractional-programming result was found. The Charnes-Cooper versus Dinkelbach literature favours Dinkelbach when few outer iterations are needed (the case here), and no benchmark for the binary case exists. Gains still come from cheaper subproblems: enumeration (v1.0.5-TODO-1), seeding (v1.0.5-TODO-13) and the backend findings.
- balanced-allocation-optimization: `cholesky-ldlt`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Researched in dense-linear-algebra. Here it is a one-off O(np²) whitening per setup, and the reuse of the rank-p factor is already tracked (v1.0.5-TODO-1/-2).
- balanced-allocation-optimization: `qr-decomposition`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Researched in dense-linear-algebra. It runs once per setup; the thin-Q̂ storage of P is already recorded in the inventory.
- balanced-allocation-optimization: `size-based-dispatch`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial orchestration. The cutoff should depend on the solver and on p (route p = 1 to BLDM+CKK if that finding is adopted); there is no algorithmic alternative.
- bayesian-bootstrap: `bayesian-bootstrap`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Re-checked what is new since 2026-11-16 (no code commits since). Weight generation is still one vectorized rgamma(B*n_units,1) call, O(Bn), with the context built once per call. It is negligible next to the B weighted refits. The rexp/uniform-spacings routes give the same distribution but change the realized draws, and the saving does not matter. Every recent proposal that avoids the B refits is an approximation of the refit distribution, so each is an estimator change: amortized generators (GMS/GBS, Shin-Wang-Liu JCGS 2024; Sohn-Rockova arXiv 2608.02480; Lopes-Polson-Sokolov 2025/26), influence-function linearization (Ribbon, arXiv 2606.27269, Jun 2026) and one-step Newton replicates (Davidson-MacKinnon; Moulton-Zeger 1991). Owen's 2025 Beta(1/2,3/2) / power weights (fwb 0.6.0) and Lam's cheap bootstrap (fwb 1.0.0) change the weight law or the interval, not the cost per replicate; they are accuracy and interval-method options, not speedups. fwb 1.0.0 adopts seed-only weights independent of worker count, which matches EDI's determinism model and needs no change. Per-replicate weighted refits (g-comp weighted logistic) are already warm-started; a fully iterated weighted MLE is needed to keep the estimand.
- beta-regression-mle: `analytic-derivatives`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Exact O(np) NLL+gradient and O(np²) observed/expected information (Ferrari–Cribari-Neto closed forms) are at the cost lower bound for an exact likelihood. Checked since 2026-11-16: betareg 3.2-6 (2026-08-26) only adds analytic score/hessian to the BetaR distribution class; TMB/glmmTMB use AD, which is no faster than hand-coded analytic derivatives for a fixed-effects model (TMB paper: similar performance without random effects). The dead all-n mu pass in ZeroOneInflatedBeta::operator() is an already-recorded incidental (catalog §3 TODO 2a).
- beta-regression-mle: `closed-form`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial: Smithson–Verkuilen squeeze and the lgamma/lbeta loglik/deviance/AIC sums are single O(n) passes; nothing faster. Alternatives for 0/1 responses (XBX extended-support betareg, ordered beta, scale-location-truncated beta arXiv 2509.13167) are estimator changes, not speedups.
- beta-regression-mle: `lbfgs`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Rivals: betareg (optim BFGS + Fisher-scoring polish, φ start from Ferrari–Cribari-Neto moment formula; vignette example 51 BFGS + 3 FS iterations) and BetaRegression.jl (pure Fisher scoring, max 100 iterations). Both are already covered by catalog §1 v1.0.5 TODO-7 (moment φ₀ + Fisher-scoring polish, Prototype) and options-table row O12. The ZOIB joint L-BFGS split is already Adopt/Prototype (catalog §3 TODO 2a/2b, §11 TODO-1). EDI's C++ L-BFGS is already 28x faster than betareg.fit at N=1000 per its own benchmark. No new LBFGSpp release or line-search evidence was found after 2026-11-16. Warm starts for bootstrap refits are only weakly supported (a single IceCube analysis reports a 3.6x median reduction in evaluations when the inverse Hessian is also carried over), and EDI's reusable worker already warm-starts.
- binary-glm-mle: `analytic-derivatives`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Closed-form score Xᵀ(y−μ) and Hessian −XᵀWX (and the probit generalized-residual forms) cost O(np²), which is the price of reading X, so nothing can beat them asymptotically. AD rivals were re-searched (TMB arXiv 1509.00660; the phylogenetics AD study, arXiv 2211.02168). No source shows AD beating a fused hand-written GLM gradient. No code commits since the 2026-11-16 result.
- binary-glm-mle: `lu-decomposition`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Researched in dense-linear-algebra. Here it is a single p×p LU log-det in the uncalled Firth evaluator, so not a hot path. Rival Firth fitters (fastglm firth, brglm2, logistf; Zietkiewicz–Kosmidis bounded-memory IWLS arXiv 2307.07342) use modified-score IWLS rather than an LU objective. That matters only if the evaluator ever gets a caller.
- blocking-partition: `greedy-blocking`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). blockTools is still 0.6.6 (2025-01-22) and quickblock still 0.2.2 (threshold blocking with a variable block count, so not a drop-in). scclust 0.2.5 has a minimum size only and does not restrict the number of clusters. Karmakar's 2022 approximation algorithm optimises a bottleneck objective, which is a different estimator, and has no R implementation. The quantile-binning strata builder runs once per design; rsample/stratamatch use the same quantile approach. At about 27–33 ms per draw for greedy, there is nothing to replace.
- blocking-partition: `linear-search`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). largest_divisor_at_most scans down from floor(sqrt(n)), O(√n) modulo operations run once per design construction. Trial division is the usual baseline, and factoring would not pay off at any realistic n.
- bootstrap-confidence-interval: `bca-interval`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Re-checked the arXiv BCa listing through 2026-10: no cheaper way to compute the acceleration has appeared. bcaboot 0.2-3 (CRAN, 2021) is unchanged, and SciPy 1.18 BCa still uses a jackknife-type acceleration with batching. The earlier conclusion stands. z0, a and the quantiles are O(B). The jackknife refits are the cost; the influence-function acceleration is already v1.0.5-TODO-7, and grouped jackknife / ABC were already rejected because they change â or need no fewer evaluations.
- bootstrap-confidence-interval: `percentile-bootstrap-interval`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Percentile, basic, studentized and symmetric intervals, tail-count p-values, the Wald fallback and the smoothed percentile interval all run on a precomputed distribution in O(B) to O(B log B), which costs microseconds next to the B refits. New searches found no 2025–26 primary work that changes this. The cheap bootstrap and BLB change the estimator. Besag–Clifford / anytime-valid sequential p-values (arXiv 2409.18908, 2404.15586) are already tracked as v1.0.5 catalog TODO-8 / TODO-156. Smoothed-bootstrap bandwidth papers (Polansky–Schucany 1997, Ho–Lee 2005) concern accuracy, not speed.
- bootstrap-index-generation: `cluster-block-resampling`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Unchanged since 2026-11-16 and there are no code commits since. resample_group_rows_internal does one counting pass plus O(G) Lemire draws and an O(output) expansion, which is optimal. The pair-preserving draw is O(n_R + m) per replicate. The faster cluster methods found this run do not apply: MacKinnon's precomputed per-cluster sums, aggregated-matrix resampling (arXiv 2311.15829) and the wild cluster bootstrap (boottest, MacKinnon-Nielsen-Webb 2023) are all OLS-specific statistic shortcuts or estimator changes, not faster index generation. fwildclusterboot was archived from CRAN on 2024-05-29. Balanced group-level resampling is still tracked under audit J.
- bootstrap-index-generation: `largest-remainder-allocation`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial: a closed-form apportionment, O(G log G) over a handful of strata and run once per call. Any other rounding rule changes the allocation, which is an estimator change.
- bootstrap-index-generation: `sort-and-sweep`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). build_draw's stable_sort runs only when preserve_order is set, on the PRW subsampling path, next to a full refit. A stable counting sort keyed on row index (keys at most n) would be O(N+n) and give the identical order. Sources found this run (Apache Arrow ARROW-7526, the Boost list) confirm counting sort wins on small key ranges, but the inventory already records this as immaterial. No new evidence.
- bootstrap-randomization-test: `kaplan-meier-product-limit`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Checked FastSurvival 2.0.0 on CRAN (MIT, 2026-11-16), the scikit-survival 0.26–0.28 notes and the 2024–26 arXiv RMST resampling papers (2408.15607, 2501.05836). Nothing beats the already-Adopt presort-once plus O(n) counting pass per arm (catalog §5, TODO-18). Multinomial-weight and Poisson-bootstrap formulations (arXiv 1412.1735) are the same idea as that planned presort.
- bootstrap-randomization-test: `logrank-sweep`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Already Adopt (presort + merge, TODO-18). FastSurvival survdiff_fast (two-pointer merge, FH weights), nph 2.1, coin 1.4-6 (2026-09-30) and the cheap quadratic-form permutation log-rank (arXiv 1807.05504) bring nothing faster for the BRT draw loop. 1807.05504 changes the statistic.
- bootstrap-randomization-test: `ridit-scoring`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). The O(n+L) level-slot rebuild (ridit_kernel_level_slots.md TODO-3) is still the only faster option. Ridit (CRAN) is archived and pridit adds nothing algorithmic.
- bootstrap-randomization-test: `sort-and-sweep`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). The O(n+K) counting variant for the JT kernel is already tracked as TODO 9. Sources put radix sort behind std::sort at n ≤ 1000 for general keys, and base R's radix itself falls back to insertion sort below n=200, so only counting by level helps at EDI's sizes. That is already tracked.
- bootstrap-randomization-test: `streaming-moments`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial: an O(n) single pass, bounded by reading the input. The shifted one-pass accuracy fix is already Adopt (TODO-4).
- bootstrap-randomization-test: `value-space-bisection-selection`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). No 2025–26 work on HL or X+Y selection found. Monahan (DescTools), Antille's O(n log n) linearization and Johnson–Mizoguchi are all already weighed in catalog §6 (tie-safe early stop, wilcox_hl_kernel_hoisting.md TODO-3/4).
- clmm-ordinal-mixed-fit: `adaptive-quadrature`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). QUADPACK oracle in ordinal_clmm_cloglog_link_probe runs once per session per ordinal version (2 clmm fits plus 80 integrate() calls). It must stay independent of GH, and its cost is negligible. Checked Boost.Math tanh-sinh / GSL QUADPACK material: tanh-sinh only wins on endpoint-singular integrands, and this integrand is smooth. Re-checked ordinal NEWS: no clmm link-swap fix through 2026.7-26, so the probe is still needed.
- clmm-ordinal-mixed-fit: `analytic-derivatives`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). OrdinalLikelihood interval probability is closed form, O(K) per observation per node. The remaining speedup, an analytic Louis-identity observed information replacing the FD Hessian, is already catalog §4 TODO-2 (Adopt). New supporting evidence: rms 8.2-0 (2026-10-22) uses a Louis-identity sparse information for its random-intercept orm. TMB/CppAD AD is not faster than hand-coded gradients (AD review arXiv 1811.05031).
- clmm-ordinal-mixed-fit: `model-simplification-surrogate`: unclear (unchanged). The inventory row is stale. At 8b718ae1, compute_weighted_clmm_estimate (inference_ordinal_KK_clmm_abstract.R:256) and the KKGLMM weighted path (inference_ordinal_KK_combined.R:485) both call weighted_ordinal_random_intercept_fit (ordinal::clmm with a random intercept), not a fixed-effects surrogate. weighted_ordinal_bootstrap_surrogate_fit is still used only by non-mixed ordinal classes. The speed and estimand question has moved to the GH weighted-path finding. Recommend retiring or rewriting this row.
- clmm-ordinal-mixed-fit: `multistart`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). The 5-start log σ grid plus L-BFGS plus FD-Hessian Newton polish is cheap at d = K+p. The only real speedup, an analytic Hessian in the polish, is TODO-2. nloptr MLSL (L-BFGS local, low-discrepancy starts) is meant for unknown multimodality and costs more than a 1-D σ grid. No 2025–2026 evidence against deterministic σ-start grids was found.
- complete-randomization-draws: `bernoulli-coin-flips`: no_faster_acceptable_found (updated). Trivial (one draw per subject or pair, using the R-parity MT stream). Checked what is new since 2026-11-16: the bit-efficient Bernoulli literature (binary-expansion and geometric methods, ~2 bits/draw) and randomness recycling (Draper & Saad, SODA 2026) save random bits, not wall time, with MT words this cheap. Bit-packing and non-MT generators were already rejected in catalog P1 because they break the stream. Change from the previous result: the Koning & Hemerik subgroup sign-flip test (Biometrika 2024, doi 10.1093/biomet/asad050) is dropped as a finding here. It changes the reference set and the p-value (an estimator change), and the current arXiv v3 abstract claims more power at the same number of transformations, not fewer draws, so it belongs to the randomization-test problem, not to the draw kernel. Per-column MT seeding cost stays under v1.0.6 performance_profiling_and_upgrades.md TODO-149(c) (counter-based child streams). The 2026 Philox-vs-MT preprint could not be fetched (403).
- conditional-logit-matched-fit: `newton-raphson`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). fast_cpoisson_combined_internal is a C++ Newton at small p on a concave canonical-link likelihood, so convergence is quadratic. Trust-region / truncated Newton (Lin-Weng-Keerthi) targets large sparse p. glm2-style step-halving (Marschner RJ 2011) and the fastglm step-halving IRLS are robustness changes, matching the existing halving/LDLT-check Adopt item (catalog §1, v1.0.5 TODO-2), not speedups. No commits since the last research.
- conditional-logit-matched-fit: `analytic-derivatives`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Closed-form score/info at O((n_d+n_R)p²), which is at the input-reading lower bound. AD (TMB/CppAD) adds tape overhead at small p. Caching lgamma(y+1), which is constant in the parameters, and a SYRK-style fused sqrt(W)X crossprod (scikit-learn sandwich_dot discussion) are constant-factor micro-optimizations already noted. Nothing new.
- conditional-logit-matched-fit: `closed-form`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial: for m=2 strata the conditional likelihood is expit((x1-x2)'b), O(n_d p) per evaluation. GLR recursion (survival::clogit exact) collapses to the same 2-term sum.
- constrained-binomial-mle: `active-set-projection`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Checked: projected-Newton and gradient-projection literature (Bertsekas; Forsgren–Murray; Davis SSC 2008), small dense QP solvers (qpOASES/qpmad/Eiquadprog per arXiv 2510.21773), and aRD/BSW (SQP via osqp/quadprog). The projection reuses the iteration's LDLT at O(|A|p²+|A|³), negligible next to O(np²). A QP or conic solver would replace the whole scheme rather than speed up this step (see the irls finding).
- constrained-binomial-mle: `cholesky-ldlt`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Researched in dense-linear-algebra. Here it is one LDLT plus one solve per variance call, O(p³) against an O(np²) build of X'WX; nothing problem-specific to reuse.
- constrained-binomial-mle: `kkt-boundary-analysis`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Checked: chi-bar-squared and constrained score-test literature (arXiv 2206.15178, 2601.03909), and the log-binomial MLE-finiteness LP (Schwendinger 2021, implemented in detectseparation). The multiplier solve reuses the LDLT and is tiny. An interior-point conic solver returns the duals directly but is not cheaper. Robustness side note, not speed: EDI could add the LP finiteness check (detectseparation::detect_infinite_estimates) before fitting, to tell infinite-MLE data apart from slow convergence. The earlier note on dropping all negative multipliers at once instead of following Lawson–Hanson NNLS still applies.
- count-glm-mle: `irls`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). fast_poisson_internal is canonical-link Newton (= IRLS/Fisher scoring) with an Eigen LDLT solve and step-halving (≤10 halvings, cached Δη, so each probe is O(n)). Checked what is new since 2026-11-16. fastglm 0.1.4 (2026-11-11) NEWS lists no Poisson algorithm change. Its vignette's fastest Poisson path (LLT Cholesky, ~0.6 ms at n=1000, p=30, per its own benchmark) is the same normal-equation Cholesky IRLS that EDI runs in C++. glm2 and speedglm are unchanged, and statsmodels and scikit-learn newton-cholesky are the same algorithm. The shrinkage-IRLS preprint (Asimit et al. 2025) changes the estimator. One-step or warm-start bootstrap shortcuts change the estimator, or are already present (EDI accepts warm_start_beta and a warm Fisher information). The modified-Poisson and cluster-sandwich work since then (rqlm 4.5-1, Noma 2026) is about finite-sample inference, not fitting speed. Nothing faster at EDI sizes.
- count-glm-mle: `lbfgs`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). L-BFGS is an option only; the default is IRLS (fast_poisson_regression.cpp:96/406). Sources (BranchGLM docs, scikit-learn solver guidance) place quasi-Newton ahead of Fisher scoring only at large p (≳50). EDI's p is small and the likelihood is strictly concave, so exact-Hessian Newton converges quadratically. LBFGSpp (MIT) is still the right L-BFGS backend if one is needed. Nothing new since 2026-11-16.
- count-glm-mle: `analytic-derivatives`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). The closed-form Poisson score X'(y−μ) and information X'WX cost O(np) and O(np²), the floor for reading X. AD (TMB/CppAD, Stan math) at best matches hand-coded derivatives and adds overhead. Fusing the likelihood, gradient and Hessian into one pass, or SIMD exp, gives only a constant-factor micro-optimization. Nothing new since 2026-11-16.
- count-glm-mle: `closed-form`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial: Pearson χ²/(n−p) dispersion times the inverse diagonal is O(n + p³), the standard quasi-Poisson estimator; nothing faster.
- covariance-from-information: `analytic-derivatives`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). SE = sqrt([I^-1]_jj) from an information matrix that is already computed in closed form and cached through shared(). Checked AD alternatives (TMB JSS paper, nabla 0.7.2 released 2026-10-28, CppAD speed suite) for anything new since 2026-11-16. AD only matches hand-coded derivatives at best on small simple models, and the sources found compare AD with ADMB, not with analytic derivatives. Nothing faster that keeps the same estimator. The count-family LR-CI bisection 'Investigate' item is a CI-inversion question, not an information-inversion one.
- covariance-from-information: `cholesky-ldlt`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Researched in dense-linear-algebra. How this problem uses it: one LDLT plus a single-RHS solve for one diagonal entry, at p up to about 20. Selected-inversion work (arXiv 2025-2026: Serinv, sTiles, incomplete-LDL^T) targets large sparse matrices and saves nothing on small dense ones. The R wrapper compute_variance_from_information_matrix does an R qr() rank check before the Eigen LDLT, which factors twice. That constant factor is already tracked in v1.1.0-TODO-155. The ordinal Hessian-built-twice redundancy is recorded as incidental.
- covariance-from-information: `eigen-pseudo-inverse`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Researched in dense-linear-algebra. This is a post-fit fallback, not a hot path. An eigendecomposition Moore-Penrose inverse is also what SAS CALIS uses for singular information below 60 parameters, which confirms it as the standard choice. Nothing new.
- covariance-from-information: `qr-decomposition`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Researched in dense-linear-algebra. chol2inv on an R factor that already exists is a closed form. The QR rank check before LDLT in the R wrapper is the constant-factor double factorization tracked in v1.1.0-TODO-155. No code changes since the last run.
- cox-ph-fit: `finite-differences`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Checked the complex-step and FD Hessian literature again: complex-step second derivatives are worse than FD, and Richardson extrapolation costs more evaluations. The FD helpers (.cox_score_breslow_fd_r, .cox_information_breslow_fd_r) appear to be dead code and have analytic replacements. Nothing new.
- cox-ph-fit: `lbfgs`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). No new benchmark of L-BFGS against Newton on the Cox objective at small p. FastSurvival notes that quasi-Newton is cheaper per iteration but makes less progress. At p ≤ ~10, the analytic-Hessian Newton is the faster choice, and L-BFGS remains an option path.
- cox-ph-fit: `linear-scan`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial: a single O(n) pass, and the O(n·S) which() incidental is already recorded. Nothing faster.
- cox-ph-fit: `sandwich-estimator`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). SAS PHREG documents the Lin-Wei sandwich via DFBETA, and the coxdev manual gives O(n)-per-column score residuals. Both match EDI's prefix-sum form, which is already optimal.
- cox-ph-fit: `sort-and-sweep`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). The radix-vs-comparison crossover is still in the hundreds to thousands of elements (djbsort speed notes 2026, ska_sort). The sort runs once per dataset and is cached via XPtr, so it is not a hot path. Keep.
- cumulative-link-ordinal-mle: `newton-raphson`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Newton with step-halving is still the reference and the fastest in Harrell's benchmarks: NR and Levenberg-Marquardt were best, nlminb close behind. ordinal 2026.7-26 (CRAN) still uses modified Newton. rms 8.0-0 and 8.2-0 add step-halving refinements: a -2LL tolerance of eps/10 and halving when the linear predictor changes by a large amount. Its Levenberg-Marquardt option is claimed to converge better with large offsets, a robustness alternative rather than a speedup at EDI's d of about 3-15. Nothing faster.
- cumulative-link-ordinal-mle: `reparameterization`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Centering and scaling is one O(np) pass. The log-increment threshold reparameterization (statsmodels OrderedModel, polr) was already rejected in catalog §2 because it destroys concavity. No new evidence.
- cumulative-link-ordinal-mle: `solver-fallback-ladder`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial orchestration. The rungs run only on failure. Checked VGAM 1.1-14 NEWS, ordinal 2026.7-26 and fastglm 0.1.4: nothing changes the ladder logic.
- dense-linear-algebra: `qr-decomposition`: no_faster_acceptable_found (new). Column-pivoted Householder QR is still the standard rank revealer at these sizes. The newer rank revealers do not fit EDI. Randomized CPQR/HQRRP (Duersch & Gu; RandLAPACK, arXiv 2507.00976) target large matrices and are RNG-dependent. Local-max-volume pivoting (arXiv 2405.04330) costs up to 2x CPQR. Pivoted Cholesky (dpstrf) is cheaper but squares the conditioning. QR updating (Bernardi, Busatto & Cattelan arXiv 2412.05905, v3 2026; CRAN fastQR 1.1.5, 2026-11-10, GPL>=2) is the reference implementation for options already rejected or tracked: catalog O11 (incremental column-append QR for qr_reduce_preserve_cols_cpp) and kk14_incremental_covariance.md (per-arrival rebuild). It is new evidence, but it does not overturn the keep-set flip risk the catalog recorded. The per-draw R loop in compute_rand_bootstrap_ci_affine_coefs and the double factorization in drop_linearly_dependent_cols are already tracked as implementation-level work.
- dense-linear-algebra: `lu-decomposition`: no_faster_acceptable_found (new). In invert_free_information, FullPivLU followed by information.inverse() (a second, partial-pivot LU) is already tracked in v1.1.0 TODO-155 and kept for bit-compatibility. On SPD information, LLT/LDLT would be ~1.5-2x faster at 8x8 (FullPivLU x1.9 vs LLT in the Eigen benchmark), but only at the O(p^3) level, which the O(np^2) Hessian build dominates. The LU log-determinant in evaluate_penalized_neg_loglik_at could be 2*sum(log diag(L)) from LLT, but the function has no caller and its sign issue (Investigate) matters more.
- dense-linear-algebra: `gauss-jordan-elimination`: no_faster_acceptable_found (new). The faster option (LLT whitening once, then Euclidean distances: O(np^2+n^2p) instead of the O(n^2p^2) per-pair S^-1 matvec in distance_matrix_mahal_cpp) is already Adopt in catalog P1 / TODO 1, so it is not re-reported. Searches found nothing beyond that: Gauss-Jordan and Cholesky inversion are both O(p^3), and Cholesky has the better constant for SPD.
- dense-linear-algebra: `eigen-pseudo-inverse`: no_faster_acceptable_found (new). It runs only as a fallback (covariance_from_information after LDLT fails) or where the null-space eigenvectors are needed (fast_zinb identified_pseudo_inverse_free_information), so it needs a spectral decomposition. CompleteOrthogonalDecomposition is ~4x faster than SVD at 8x8 (Eigen benchmark), but it does not give the eigenvector loadings and would change the truncation semantics, which is an estimator change on rank-deficient information. At p<30, SelfAdjointEigenSolver costs microseconds.
- dense-linear-algebra: `schur-complement`: no_faster_acceptable_found (new). score_test_from_score_information forms the full covariance_from_information(I_nn) and then a matvec. One LDLT of I_nn solved against I_n,psi (or a block LDLT/Cholesky of the whole I, where the trailing block is the Schur complement, per Higham 2023) avoids the explicit inverse. This is already noted in the inventory verdict and in v1.1.0 TODO-155, and it is a constant factor at p<30. Nothing new found.
- design-matrix-preprocessing: `linear-scan`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial. which_cols_vary_cpp compares each column against row 0 and exits early, O(np) in the worst case, which is the cost of reading the input. It still has no R caller outside RcppExports. No commits since the 2026-11-16 result, and the problem-level searches found nothing new.
- design-matrix-preprocessing: `streaming-moments`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial. scale_columns_cpp computes the mean and SD with two passes, O(np) and memory-bound. arXiv 2410.21922 (seen again this run) confirms that two-pass is more accurate than Welford at large offsets, and a one-pass sum-of-squares formula cancels catastrophically. The function has no R caller. Nothing new since 2026-11-16.
- exact-conditional-tests: `clopper-pearson-interval`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Closed form: two qbeta calls, O(1); nothing can be faster. This run checked binom 1.1-2 (2026-07-07), PropCIs, BlakerCI and exactci 1.4-5, plus a Monte Carlo band search. None offers a faster CP computation; BlakerCI and exactci change the interval (a different estimator), not its speed. The minlike-p vs central-CI mismatch (Fay 2010, R Journal) is a coherence issue that is already recorded (catalog §6 incidental 14), not a speed issue.
- exact-rank-null-distribution: `closed-form`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial. build_log_factorials and build_log_choose_table each make a single O(n) pass of cumulative log sums, built once per test, so nothing faster exists. No code commits since 2026-11-16.
- gamma-family-functions: `asymptotic-series-recurrence`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Rechecked Boost digamma (fetched): it uses a rational approximation on [1,2] and shifts down from [2,10] to reach it, so it does at least as many recurrence steps as EDI's upward shift to x>=8. It is more accurate (<1 eps) but has no timing data. The arXiv listing for 2021-2026 has no fast double-precision digamma/trigamma method; 2111.07686 and 2109.08392 target arbitrary precision. In the NB fits, digamma and trigamma run only once per distinct y per iteration (m_digamma_yptheta tables in fast_negbin_regression.cpp), so they are off the O(n) hot path. A cumulative recurrence over consecutive integer y (ψ(y+1+θ)=ψ(y+θ)+1/(y+θ)) would save little and adds rounding drift, so it is not reported. No faster acceptable option.
- gamma-family-functions: `closed-form`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial (O(1) closed form). lgamma(y+1) is already cached per distinct y (m_lgamma_y1), which is the log-factorial lookup idea. The NB fit hot loop calls std::lgamma rather than fast_lgamma for lgamma(y+θ) and lgamma(θ), but only once per distinct y, so the cost is negligible. R 4.5.1 NEWS fixes dnbinom/bd0 at extreme arguments. That is accuracy, matching the existing v1.0.5 TODO-8 near-Poisson item, not speed.
- gamma-family-functions: `lanczos-stirling`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). No new double-precision lgamma method since the last run. CORE-MATH/glibc and LLVM libc work in 2024–2026 is correctly rounded binary32/half precision; binary64 is a hard-to-round-cases research note. Lanczos g=7 with 9 terms plus a 7-term Stirling series for x>=8 is already minimal O(1). TFP's x>=8 Stirling threshold matches EDI's. The SLEEF and VDT pages found give no lgamma timing. An accuracy aside, as last time: fast_lbeta cancels at large a,b, and TFP log_gamma_difference (Stirling-correction cancellation, Apache-2.0) is a reference for an accurate version. Nothing faster.
- gaussian-lmm-fit: `generalized-least-squares`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). A single GLS solve at fixed VC (the estimate-only replicate path) is O(n·p²+p³). Forming XᵀV⁻¹X is unavoidable once per new X/weights, so nothing is cheaper for one solve. mmrm (Apache-2.0, TMB) does the same whitening-plus-Cholesky GLS per θ. The Wansbeek–Kapteyn closed-form V⁻¹ for incomplete panels is the same quasi-demeaning EDI already uses. Re-checked lme4 2.1-0 (2026-10-23) and glmmTMB 1.1.15: no new LMM algorithm.
- gaussian-lmm-fit: `laplace-approximation-tmb`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Fallback only. Laplace and INLA's inner step are exact for a Gaussian LMM, so this method changes no estimate. The glmmTMB 1.1.14/1.1.15 NEWS lists no optimizer speedup (the RTMB back-end is described as speed parity). The FBMS paper (arXiv 2509.00753) reports RTMB slower than lmer in its workflow. The C++ closed-form path remains the fast path.
- gaussian-lmm-fit: `analytic-derivatives`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). The analytic observed information is computed once per fit at O(n·p²), the same cost as forming XᵀWX, so it is optimal. If the Gram precompute is adopted, the Hessian can be read off the same blocks at O(p²), but the total cost is unchanged. TMB/AD (mmrm, glmmTMB) would only add overhead.
- gaussian-lmm-fit: `closed-form`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial: an O(1) stack-allocated 2×2 inverse. Nothing is faster.
- gcomp-marginal-effects: `delta-method`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial: closed-form gradient times covariance, O(p²) after an O(np) gradient pass. The logistic and fractional-logit paths already use the analytic gradient (gcomp_speedups.cpp compute_gcomp_logistic_post_fit, solved against the LDLT). The FD users (marginal_estimand_delta_se/numerical_gradient_central; ordinal_gcomp_post_fit_cpp) already have the analytic gradient recorded as Adopt (v1.0.5-TODO-8, v1.0.5-TODO-5). Checked for anything new since 2026-11-16: the AD option in Margins.jl/marginaleffects (JAX) gives exact Jacobians, but at EDI's p in the tens the analytic gradient is already exact and cheaper. Nothing new.
- gcomp-marginal-effects: `finite-differences`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). inference_ordinal_gcomp.R compute_md_gradient makes 2(p+K−1) R→C++ calls, each O(n(p+K)). An analytic gradient would be a single O(nKp) pass, already recorded (catalog §2 v1.0.5-TODO-5, low priority), so nothing new. The fractional-logit FD fires only as a fallback. AD (Margins.jl claims 3-5x over FD; marginaleffects JAX autodiff, experimental) and complex-step/Richardson (numDeriv, pracma) are not cheaper than the already-planned analytic gradient, and the catalog already rejected complex-step. No new 2026 source changes this.
- gcomp-marginal-effects: `g-computation`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). The plug-in standardization is O(np), or O(n(p+K)) for PO, bounded below by reading X. The cost is dominated by the outcome-model fit. Rivals checked this run (marginaleffects 1.0.0 of 2026-09-03, stdReg2 1.0.7, risks 0.4.3, RISCA 1.0.7, drord) all use the same fit + average + delta/sandwich or bootstrap. Margins.jl (arXiv 2601.07065, Jan 2026) claims a 622x speedup over marginaleffects, but that comes from formula compilation and allocation avoidance in a general-purpose tool. EDI's C++ kernels already do a direct O(np) pass. Per-replicate refits: the linear/quadratic one-step bootstrap (Claeskens, Aerts & Molenberghs 2003) avoids iterative refits, but it approximates the replicate estimator, so it is an estimator change, and it belongs to the resampling problem (J), not here. The 2026 small-sample papers (arXiv 2605.19772, 2509.07369) concern variance accuracy and estimand, not speed (cf. Incidental 16).
- gcomp-marginal-effects: `sandwich-estimator`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). HC0: LDLT of XᵀWX + O(np²) meat + O(p³) inverse, the standard minimum. sandwich 3.1-2/3.1-3 NEWS (2026) has no algorithmic speedups. clubSandwich 0.7.0 CR2 and the 2025 'more efficient CR2' blog concern CR2 small-sample corrections, which EDI does not use (it uses a CR0-type cluster meat via unordered_map, O(np²)). Nothing faster at EDI sizes.
- gee-fit: `cholesky-ldlt`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Researched in dense-linear-algebra. Here it is a single p x p LDLT solve per scoring iteration (QR fallback). That is O(p^3) against O(n p^2) for forming the information matrix, so the solve is never the bottleneck at EDI's sizes. The previous conclusion stands: LLT is only marginally faster and gives up robustness.
- gee-fit: `method-of-moments`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Closed-form O(n) pass over pair Pearson products. Nothing can be faster. The alternatives found (quasi-least squares, residual pseudo-likelihood for the ICC, Chaganty & Joe 2004) are estimator changes aimed at bias or efficiency, not speed. The phi-scaling fix (alpha = num/((N_pairs-p)*phi)) is still present in fast_gee.cpp:125-130.
- generic-smooth-mle-optimization: `lbfgs`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). No code commits since the previous result (code_commits_since empty). optimize_likelihood_lbfgs still uses LBFGSpp with backtracking strong-Wolfe, max_linesearch 100, past=1. Checked for anything new since 2026-11-16. LBFGSpp's GitHub page lists no new releases and no performance claims. No 2025–26 paper compares the line-search choice by evaluation count. arXiv 2405.10897 reports the same number of evaluations as L-BFGS-B's Moré–Thuente search. At EDI's small p (≈2–50), full BFGS or ucminf (BFGS with trust-region-monitored line search) would not reduce evaluations in any benchmarked way. The real alternative for concave fits is Newton, already catalogued (catalog §1 TODO-7 and §2 TODO-1/2). The marqLevAlg R Journal comparison (17–18 parameters) shows L-BFGS-B taking 244–823 iterations against 6–24 for Marquardt–Levenberg on a hard joint model, with similar wall-clock. That supports the existing Newton-default items rather than a new L-BFGS change.
- glmm-marginal-likelihood: `bfgs`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). The R-level optim(BFGS) in fit_combined_hurdle already has a Prototype (damped Newton/trust-region with the analytic Hessian). Today's searches found only general evidence that L-BFGS/BFGS followed by a Newton polish is the GLMM pattern. ucminf 1.2.3 (2026-04-02) is a BFGS with a trust-region-monitored line search: no new algorithm.
- glmm-marginal-likelihood: `laplace-approximation-tmb`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). glmmTMB 1.1.15.2 (2026-09-29, AGPL-3), TMB 1.9.25 (2026-08-22) and RTMB 2.0 (2026-09-17) bring no new algorithm. TMB's JSS speed claims (1.5-100x) are relative to ADMB, and RJ-2017-066's are relative to lme4/glmmADMB. Li & Signorelli 2606.15933 and the 2016 small-cluster probit review again report Laplace's lower accuracy at cluster size 2. EDI's own C++ GH kernels remain the faster route (existing Prototype/Investigate items).
- glmm-marginal-likelihood: `log-sum-exp`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial: O(K) per group, max-shift fused with the weights. Nothing faster.
- glmm-marginal-likelihood: `closed-form`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial: O(1) link cdf/pdf policies. Aside: for a probit random intercept with m_g<=2, the pair likelihood is a bivariate normal CDF with rho=sigma^2/(1+sigma^2), which is quadrature-free. This is my derivation and unsourced. It would only matter for the engine's ProbitLink users (ordinal CLMM, another problem), not the logistic/Poisson kernels here.
- glmm-marginal-likelihood: `soft-barrier-penalty`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial, O(1). Chung et al. 2013 / blme penalties change the estimator, not the speed. No new evidence.
- greedy-swap-allocation-search: `best-improvement-swap`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). I checked for anything new since the 2026-11-16 seed. No code commits touched these files. The best-improvement rule defines the design's allocation distribution: first-improvement, Kernighan-Lin, tabu and simulated annealing reach different local optima, so they are design changes (the Aloise et al. 2025 EJOR study, DOI 10.1016/j.ejor.2025.04.019, finds no general winner even on speed). The newer allocation literature (pair-switching rerandomization arXiv 2103.13051; Metropolis-Hastings PSRSRR arXiv 2602.07613; Langevin-gradient rerandomization doi 10.1007/s11222-026-11029-x; discrepancy designs arXiv 2608.18057) proposes different designs, not a faster exact best-swap search. GreedyExperimentalDesign 1.6.1 (CRAN, 2026-04-30) adds a GPU full-greedy loop (wgpu, apparently 4-byte float argmin) with an O(p) incremental update. EDI already has that incremental update, and a GPU float path would break EDI's accuracy, determinism and dependency constraints. The exact speedups that keep the same optimum (Gram G = M'M for O(1) per pair, routing Mahalanobis through the pruned D engine with P = 4G) are already tracked (catalog §8 P2, small_kernel_hoists_batch.md Item A). The pruned scan's per-pair delta A_i + B_j − 2P_ij is a biased bichromatic MIPS / closest-pair problem; no exact subquadratic method pays off at n in the hundreds to low thousands. From reading the code, not the literature, and still open: in DOptimalPruneObjective the prune offset is 2·max_P (global). A per-treated-row max_j P_ij would be a tighter bound that is still valid.
- grouping-and-level-coding: `hash-map-grouping`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial (expected O(n), one pass). Checked against the 2026 sources: the hash-vs-sort study (arXiv 2411.13245) finds hashing is the better choice for small inputs and few groups, which is EDI's setting. absl/Swiss-table flat maps beat std::unordered_map by a constant factor, but sample_mode, find_discordant_pairs and compute_survival_strata_ids run once per call, outside any replicate loop, so the gain is immaterial. collapse::fmode uses the same hash-plus-first-tie algorithm. No code commits since the last research.
- grouping-and-level-coding: `sort-and-sweep`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). These steps are one-time O(n log n) layout and level coding (GLMM data constructors, init_levels, get_levels/map_y_to_1K), dominated by the fits that follow. libc++ added an LSD-radix path to std::stable_sort in 2025, but it applies only to the default comparator on arithmetic types, not to EDI's index sort with a custom comparator, and R builds usually use libstdc++. A counting sort on dense group ids (O(n+G)) remains immaterial, as the catalog already records. collapse qF/radixorder use the same sort-or-hash choice. Nothing new since 2026-11-16.
- hodges-lehmann-estimation: `selection-algorithm`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Unchanged from the 2026-11-16 result (no code commits since). For n_T·n_C ≤ 4096, hl_from_groups/hl_signed_rank/median_in_place materialize the differences and run std::nth_element (introselect, O(N)); above that they hand off to sort plus value-space bisection. This search found no new head-to-head benchmark at small N. Floyd–Rivest (miniselect, BSL-1.0) still has only anecdotal or asymptotic advantages, and one analysis says it reduces to the same algorithm at small N. Any gain is a constant factor on a step dominated by materializing the differences. Rfast and matrixStats offer nothing faster for one vector.
- hodges-lehmann-estimation: `value-space-bisection-selection`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Unchanged. Checked for anything new since 2026-11-16: no 2025–2026 arXiv paper on selection in X+Y or sorted matrices. Searches return only Kaplan et al. 2018 (soft heaps), de Berg–Thite 2008 and Serang 2021 (top-k, not the median). DescTools NEWS shows only a C++ Monahan port (0.99.52), a two-sample version (0.99.53) and long-long indexing (0.99.54), with no benchmark against bisection. robslopes 1.1.4 (R Journal RJ-2023-012) is randomized (Matoušek), and its 10^3× speedups are measured against brute-force O(n²) code, not against an O(n·L) bisection. Monahan and Johnson–Mizoguchi selection were already rejected in the catalog (§6 options table), and no new evidence overturns that. The levers remain wilcox_hl_kernel_hoisting.md TODO-3 (tie-safe early stop) and TODO-4 (warm bracket).
- hodges-lehmann-estimation: `walsh-average-enumeration`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Unchanged. These are the stats::wilcox.test(conf.int) paths: exact sorted Walsh averages for m<50, otherwise a normal approximation with uniroot. Both are cheap at EDI sizes, and the point estimate already uses the C++ kernel. NAG g07eaf/g07ebf and DescTools use Monahan 1984, which the catalog already covers. npsm::hodges_lehmann.ci just wraps wilcox.test. coin 1.4-6 (2026-09-30) offers no HL estimator. The weighted HL branch was flagged as dead code last run; no change since. The open item is the SE scale (F7/I2), a correctness bug, not a speed one.
- kaplan-meier-rmst: `mover-interval`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Closed form from two intervals, O(1); nothing faster. Tang 2021 (MOVER under stratified sampling, doi 10.1002/sim.9230) and McGrath et al. arXiv 2503.03065 v2 (2026) concern interval quality and SE estimation, not speed. FastSurvival's medsurv_fast uses a different estimator (kernel-smoothed-hazard delta-method SE), so it would be an estimator change, not a speedup.
- kk-sequential-matching: `brute-force-distance`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). The weighted Euclidean scan over the reservoir is O(|R|d), one query per arrival, and the weights change with each KK21 refit, so a tree built once cannot be reused. The scikit-learn guide (re-fetched) says brute force wins when there are very few queries or N is below about 30, and its 'auto' rule picks brute force for D>15. Searching tree versus SIMD brute-force benchmarks and FNN/RANN (both L2/kd/cover tree, and they need a fixed metric) found nothing new since 2026-11-16. The scan is already the minimum exact cost.
- kk-sequential-matching: `mahalanobis-quadratic-forms`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). A GEMM rowsum((D·S⁻¹)∘D) costs O(|R|p²), the same order as Cholesky whitening. The covariance changes every arrival, so cached or incremental reservoir distances are invalidated each time. Rank-one Cholesky updates (O(p²) per arrival instead of O(p³)) only touch the S⁻¹/solve part, which kk14_incremental_covariance.md TODO-3 already tracks. The searches found no exact dynamic-metric nearest-neighbour structure newer than the approximate one already rejected (Qin et al. 2023). mvnfast's maha and Rfast::mahala use the same brute-force algorithm and give only constant-factor gains over stats::mahalanobis, and EDI already does this in C++. Keep holds.
- kk-sequential-matching: `monte-carlo-pair-sampling`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). O(Bd) with B=500 per arrival, followed by stats::quantile on B values. Exact enumeration when t(t−1)/2 ≤ B is already a tracked Prototype (catalog §7, v1.0.5 TODO-35). It changes the design draws for a given seed, so it is a design-rule change, not a speedup. Streaming quantile sketches (KLL, t-digest) are approximate and pointless at B=500, since sorting is exact and cheap. The incomplete-U-statistic literature turned up no faster exact estimator. The rejection step that keeps pairs distinct costs O(1) expected per draw.
- kk21-covariate-weights: `closed-form`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial: closed-form simple-regression t, one O(np) pass, so nothing can be faster. The accuracy note from the last run (centered two-pass sums) still applies. No code commits since.
- kk21-covariate-weights: `irls`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Rechecked fastglm 0.1.4 (2026-11-11). It runs the same IRLS (Cholesky) with step-halving, so it is no faster per iteration. Rfast::logistic_only fits many univariate logistic regressions by C++ Newton, the same per-covariate structure as EDI. The beta φ grid → Brent/Newton option is already catalog P4 / v1.0.5 TODO 4, so it is not re-reported.
- kk21-covariate-weights: `newton-raphson`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Weibull AFT univariate and reduced fits use damped Newton on the analytic Hessian, the fastest-converging choice at p ≤ ~10. The 2026 search found only tutorials: survreg converges in 3-5 Newton iterations, and lgspline uses Brent for scale plus Newton for β, which is not faster. Nothing new.
- likelihood-ci-inversion: `safeguarded-newton-root`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). I checked for new endpoint methods. arXiv 2404.02774 (profile likelihood via constrained optimisation / ODEs, 2024) makes no speed claim and is aimed at continuous families of limits. The RVM paper (Fischer & Lewis 2021, doi 10.1007/s11222-021-10012-y) is unchanged. logistf still uses Venzon–Moolgavkar. I found no 2025–2026 safeguarded-Newton benchmark. The cataloged signed-root Newton (v1.0.5 audit TODO 4) remains the improvement to make.
- likelihood-ci-inversion: `warm-start-continuation`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). New sources only confirm that warm starts help. The IceCube thesis (arXiv 2609.12941) reports a 3.6× median cut in likelihood evaluations from neighbour warm starts in profile fits. aquakin uses a continuation sweep with a cold anchor. Both are path-dependent chaining, the option row 5 rejects. The deterministic tangent predictor (v1.0.5 TODO-5, catalog NEW-ci-refit-warm-start) is still the path-independent choice, and nothing newer turned up. The code still disables warm starts for *_ci keys (std_mod_cache.R:122).
- likelihood-tests-null-refits: `closed-form`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial: LR = 2(nll0-nll1) and gradient statistic are O(1) closed forms plus one chi-square tail; nothing faster. The only p^3 step is the Schur complement in the score test (constant factor, v1.1.0-TODO-155). No commits since the previous result.
- likelihood-tests-null-refits: `irls`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Checked fastglm 0.1.4 (CRAN, 2026-11-11, new since last result): it is C++ IRLS with a Cholesky solve and step-halving, the same algorithm class as EDI's C++ Newton/IRLS. Its benchmark vignette's ~3-8x gain over glm.fit is a gain of C++ over R, which EDI already has. Refits are already warm-started. No newer arXiv IRLS method keeps the estimator and wins at EDI's sizes.
- likelihood-tests-null-refits: `newton-raphson`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). The constrained C++ Newton refits with fixed_idx and warm starts are the standard optimal approach for concave GLM likelihoods. Trust-region profile-interval algorithms (Fischer & Lewis 2021, Venzon-Moolgavkar) target the CI endpoint search (row M, brent_ci_inversion.md), not the per-delta refit. fastglm_ordinal (2026) also uses observed-information Newton with an expected-information certificate, the same as EDI. Nothing new since the last result.
- likelihood-tests-null-refits: `parametric-bootstrap`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Monte Carlo Bartlett factor, recomputed per delta. New items checked: Hanada & Sugimoto 2025/2026 (Biometrics, arXiv 2508.08758) give an analytic 'simplified Bartlett correction', but it is specific to random-effects meta-analysis, so for EDI it would be an estimator change. pbkrtest 0.5.5 PBmodcomp computes the same mean-of-LR Bartlett factor (nsim = 1000 default); its seqPBmodcomp early stop applies to tail p-values, not means. I found no variance-reduction or cheaper estimator of E[LR|H0]. The catalog's own option (one factor per CI, v1.0.5 TODO-3) still covers the space.
- likelihood-tests-null-refits: `ridge-regularization`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Escalating ridge on the (p-1)x(p-1) nuisance block runs once per score test, at most 8 small solves. Searches on Tikhonov / diagonal loading for score tests and on nearPD / modified Cholesky turned up nothing faster with the same answer. Its calibration remains a robustness question, not a speed one.
- likelihood-tests-null-refits: `schur-complement`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Researched in dense-linear-algebra. Here it runs once per test, not per replicate. The only gain is one LDLT solve in place of the full inverse (constant factor, v1.1.0-TODO-155).
- link-function-evaluation: `closed-form`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). Trivial (closed form, O(1) per element). logit/inv_logit/sample_mode and the ordinal link cdf/pdf/pdf' are one pass of arithmetic, so nothing is faster. Checked since 2026-11-16: no commits touched these files. Eigen gained vectorized double erf/erfc (commit 5133c836, Nov 2024; generic_fast_erfc packet path), which could in principle SIMD-batch the probit erfc. But EDI's ordinal helpers evaluate per observation inside a link switch, RcppEigen still bundles Eigen 3.4.0, and the packet traits are gated on EIGEN_FAST_MATH on SSE/AVX512/NEON, so there is no drop-in win. Robustness note, not speed: the cloglog clamp z<-37 -> F=0 floors F where F≈e^z≈1e-16, so log F is lost for extreme negative z.
- link-function-evaluation: `rational-approximation`: no_faster_acceptable_found (unchanged). fast_atan is Cephes range reduction plus a 5/5 rational at about 1 ulp. VDT's fast_atan (LGPL-3, Cephes-derived) reports 11 ns scalar and 8.3 ns AVX versus 19.7 ns libm on an i7-3930K. That is the same construction as EDI's, so there is no algorithmic gain, and the AVX gain needs batched calls, which the per-observation cauchit loop does not do. Cephes erfc for probit is likewise standard. Arm optimized-routines erfc (table plus series, about 1.2-1.7 ULP) is a SIMD design, not faster per scalar call at full precision.
